## Migration Plan
No schema or proto-breaking change; all proto changes are additive. Data-affecting items:
- **No automatic history deletion.** The restart-time one-shot prune and its marker key are removed. Existing hidden-session rows are removed only by the operator-triggered `PruneHiddenSessionNotifications` RPC (Story 2.7, PR 2b), which defaults to dry run.
- **Prune safety rules** (hard requirements, tested): (1) `apply=false` unless explicitly set; a dry run returns and logs the ids, grouped by reason; (2) rows whose session resolves `Unresolved` or `NotASession` are always kept, which includes rows of sessions deleted before this feature shipped (84 of 319 rows in the research sample, `research/pitfalls.md`), because hidden-ness is undeterminable; they are reported as `undeterminable: N`; (3) an unread row with `is_pending_decision` (e.g. an unread WARNING, `IsActionableType` at `server/notifications/store.go:55`) is kept unless the request sets `include_unread_actionable=true` (operator decision O4, DECIDED: default keeps them); (4) the RPC returns `FailedPrecondition` unless the `VisibilityIndex` reports `seeded=true`, so an un-seeded index cannot cause mass keep-or-delete misclassification; (5) before an apply it copies the store file to a **timestamped** backup `notifications.json.pre-prune-<UTC yyyymmddThhmmssZ>.bak` (a second apply never overwrites the first backup) and logs removed count and ids at INFO; (6) authorization: auth on `:8444`; on `:8543` there is no auth (`SetupAuth`, `server/server.go:1321`, has no non-test caller, so the local chain at `:1700-1708` runs only `ProbeGuard`), so the RPC is in the `LocalWriteGuard` procedure set (whole procedure, rebinding gate: Host in the configured allowlist, Origin loopback or allowed, no `LoopbackBound` condition, so a LAN-hostname browser on the supported bind keeps working; Story 1.8) and **an apply appends a `kind=prune` audit line (matched count, `include_unread_actionable`, dry-run flag, listener, peer address, Host, Origin; no row content) to the audit sink before it deletes** (the sink core exists from PR 2a-2, Task 2.8h; a failed append means no delete); no extra role (the product has a single operator), not exposed over MCP, and every apply logs the caller's remote address and listener. `ClearNotificationHistory` (same exposure today) is deliberately not widened here and is listed as a follow-up. Re-runnable: it is keyed on current state, not a one-shot marker.
- **Reversibility**: apply is irreversible by design; the newest timestamped `.bak` (and any earlier ones) is the rollback. Everything else is flag-controlled or a clean `git revert` (no schema).
- **Proto changes** (additive only; they land in **three contract PRs** so that no frozen type depends on an unrun spike or an un-reviewed design). **Contract PR 1 (Story 1.4, startable now)**: `session.proto`: `NotificationHistoryRecord.is_pending_decision = 14` (last field is 13, `last_occurred_at`), `ClearNotificationHistoryRequest.notification_ids = 2`, `ListSessionsRequest.hidden_only = 9` (highest is 8), and the `PruneHiddenSessionNotifications` message pair and RPC (stub returns `CodeUnimplemented` until Story 2.7); `events.proto`: `NotificationEvent.is_pending_decision = 10` (last field is 9); metadata constant `events.MetadataKeyAutoRemediating`. **Contract PR 2 (Story 1.6, after the focused re-review PASS on items 3 and 4)**: `UpdateFeatureFlagRequest.scope = 3` and `FlagMutation mutation = 4` (an enum spelled with the buf `ENUM_VALUE_PREFIX`: `FLAG_MUTATION_UNSPECIFIED = 0`, `FLAG_MUTATION_SET_ENABLED`, `FLAG_MUTATION_SET_DISABLED`, `FLAG_MUTATION_CLEAR_SCOPE`, `FLAG_MUTATION_RESET_GLOBAL`; it replaces the earlier `bool clear_override`, whose meaning flipped on whether `scope` was empty, and the set values carry the enabled state so a `SET` cannot write `false` because the plain `bool enabled` was omitted; today `name = 1`, `enabled = 2`), `FeatureFlag.scopes = 5` (repeated `FeatureFlagScopeOverride`; today fields 1-4), and the `GetDeliveryGateStats` message pair and RPC with the **gate-on-time shape** (per-bucket `gate_on_seconds` global and per kind, `uptime_seconds`, `routine_events_while_on`, a `kind` label, `process_start`, a flag-change history and a server-computed `soak_streak_hours`; stub `CodeUnimplemented` until Story 2.8). **Contract PR 2 does not merge until both shapes are settled in Story 1.6, because it freezes them** (review-repair iteration 1, ADV-B3 and ARCH-C8; reshaped again in iteration 2, ADV-N3/N5, ARCH-C22/C25/C26); "Reset to default" is `mutation = RESET_GLOBAL`. **Contract PR 3 (Story 1.7, after Spike 1.3g and the re-review PASS on item 1)**: the `ReplyToPendingQuestion` message pair and RPC (stub until Story 5.6), so its request/response shape (option buttons versus free text, answer keystrokes) is not frozen before the spike decides it. **The `ReplyOutcome` enum (values spelled `REPLY_OUTCOME_<NAME>` for buf lint: `UNSPECIFIED = 0`, `SENT`, `SEND_INDETERMINATE`, `NOT_SENT`, `NO_PENDING`, `SUPERSEDED`, `STALE_PROMPT`, `NOT_HIDDEN`, `NOT_WAITING`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `DISABLED`; `IN_PROGRESS` was removed in review-repair iteration 2 because nothing could emit it) must be complete before this PR merges** (review-repair iteration 1, ARCH-B1/ADV-B1/ADV-B2). The keystroke mode of the reply (`ReplyKeystrokes`) is a server-side choice recorded by Spike 1.3g and is not a field of this message. `hidden_session_readonly_guards` and `hidden_session_reply` need no proto change (`knownFeatureFlags` entries). `make proto-gen`; generated output stays gitignored; run `make registry-generate`.
- **New local files**: `<config dir>/delivery-gate-stats.json` (Task 2.8f: atomic write, schema version, checksum, corruption recovery, written by the `StatsStore` file adapter in `server/services`, not by the pure `deliverygate` package) and `<config dir>/audit/hidden-session-replies.jsonl` (sink core and `flag_change` lines from PR 2a-2, Task 2.8h; extended by the O7 steer in Task 5.2e, by Reply in Story 5.6 and by the prune apply in Task 2.7c; directory 0700). Both files are local, mode 0600, and are not part of notification history.

## Observability Plan
- **Logs** (slog via `log`): every suppression logs `delivery_suppressed` with `channel`, `session_id`, `notification_type`, `reason`; every allowed hidden delivery logs `hidden_delivery_allowed` with `channel`, `class`. Shadow mode logs `delivery_would_suppress`. `Unresolved` fail-open logs WARN `delivery_unresolved_fail_open`. Per `(session_id, type, reason)` the line is rate-limited to once per 60s with `suppressed_since_last`; the limiter map is bounded (LRU, 4096 keys) so it cannot grow without limit (production data has 166 events from one session).
- **Metrics, in-process first** (Phase 4, pre-mortem #2): `telemetry.GetMeter()` returns `otel.Meter(ServiceName)`, a no-op meter, whenever `globalProvider` is nil (`telemetry/telemetry.go:274-279`, VERIFIED), and OTel export is opt-in (`docs/how-to/enable-opentelemetry.md`); whether the live instance exports metrics is UNVERIFIED. So every counter below is **mirrored in-process** in `server/deliverygate/metrics.go` (a package-level atomic per counter and label set; one helper increments the OTel instrument and the mirror so they cannot drift), 72 hourly buckets are persisted (through a `StatsStore` adapter outside the pure `deliverygate` package) to `<config dir>/delivery-gate-stats.json` so a restart does not erase the soak, and the operator reads them through the `GetDeliveryGateStats` RPC (reachable on `:8543` without auth and on `:8444` behind auth; aggregate counters only) and a Settings status line (Story 2.8). The buckets also record when the gate was on, so the soak is derivable rather than remembered (ADV-B3). OTel instruments (pattern of `server/services/watch_stream_metrics.go`) remain for operators who do export metrics. Counters: `notification_delivery_suppressed_total{channel,type,reason,kind}`, `notification_delivery_would_suppress_total{channel,type,reason,kind}`, `notification_hidden_delivered_total{channel,class,kind}` (`kind` is the closed set `review`, `triage`, `diagnose`, `other`, `unresolved`; a review-only Stage 2 flip is therefore not mixed with other kinds' shadow counts), `notification_delivery_unresolved_total{class}` (class = the policy class the event would have had if resolved: routine leaks are visible on their own), `notification_gate_index_miss_total`, `notification_gate_index_refresh_total{result}` (`started`, `skipped_min_interval`, `timeout`, `ok`), `notification_unresolved_resolved_later_total` (an ID that missed the index but exists in storage after the refresh: an index bug), `notification_legacy_hidden_suppressed_total{site,type,class}` (one increment at each of the **seven** legacy checks, added in 2a; the closed `site` set is `send_notification_low` (`notification_service.go:142`), `session_events_cold_restore` (`session_service_events.go:76`), `session_events_rate_limit_detected` (`:193`), `session_events_recovery_succeeded` (`:234`), `session_events_recovery_failed` (`:252`), `autonomous_generic` (`autonomous_orchestration_service.go:619`), `rqm_suppress_for_hidden` (`review_queue_manager.go:442`); the L413 raw `inst.Hidden` read feeds the last one and is deleted with it; `class` is computed with `ShouldDeliver` at the legacy site so the soak can see which swallowed events the policy would deliver), `notification_rpc_unversioned_total` (SendNotification requests lacking `ssq_notify_schema`, Story 2.5), `hidden_session_reply_total{outcome}` (Story 5.6; the closed set is the `ReplyOutcome` names `sent`, `send_indeterminate`, `not_sent`, `no_pending`, `superseded`, `stale_prompt`, `not_hidden`, `not_waiting`, `rate_limited`, `disabled` plus `invalid`, `audit_failed`, `refused`; the earlier `send_failed`, `send_failed_claim_released` and `in_progress` are gone), `hidden_session_backlog_steer_total{outcome}` (`sent`, `no_link`, `rejected`, `audit_failed`, `guard_bypass`, `refused`, `busy`; Task 5.2e), `hidden_session_audit_degraded_total{mode}` (`flag_change_queued`, `flag_change_fallback_log`, `steer_fallback_log`; Tasks 2.8h and 5.2e), `hidden_session_reply_registered_unreplyable_total{cause}` and `hidden_session_reply_duplicate_proofless_total` (Task 5.6n), `hidden_session_write_lease_wedge_notified_total` (Task 5.0f), `hidden_session_write_lease_busy_total{writer}` (`reply`, `driver`, `autonomous`, `nudge`, `steer`, `mcp`, `ratelimit`, `other`; Story 5.0), `notification_gate_filter_panic_total` (Task 2.3e), `notification_crash_coalesced_total` (Task 2.10d). No `session_id` label. `notification_gate_filter_duration_seconds` histogram, plus a **publish-filter latency budget that is measured, not guessed** (Task 2.3f): the filter runs on the caller goroutine of ~108 `Publish` sites, some under locks, so the budget is relative to the existing `Publish` cost. `BenchmarkPublishWithFilter` (index of 0 and of 1,000 sessions; hit, miss, hidden-routine and non-notification events) must stay within 5% of `BenchmarkPublishNoFilter` at p99; the benchmarks run in the background per `docs/reference/benchmarks.md`; the measured absolute numbers replace any INFERRED figure in `docs/reference/notification-delivery-gate.md`, and the histogram's p99 on the manual instance during Stage 1 is compared with the benchmark. The 5% ratio is the initial threshold (INFERRED) and is recalibrated once, from the first benchmark run.
- **Write lease** (Story 5.0): counter `hidden_session_write_lease_busy_total{writer}`, gauge `hidden_session_write_lease_held_seconds{writer}` (longest current hold) and WARN `terminal_write_lease_wedged` after `LeaseWedgeWarnAfter` (INFERRED 30s); mirrored in-process like every other counter. Once per wedge episode a tray-level WARNING (never pushed) is raised for the session (Task 5.0f); the flag `terminal_write_lease` (default on) is the live escape hatch.
- **Client**: no new telemetry beyond existing `useAuditLog` hooks.
- **Soak verification (Stage 2)**: (a) `would_suppress` by type shows only routine types (type detail comes from the since-process-start section; the persisted buckets are reduced to `(counter, kind, class)`, so the type review needs a process lifetime that covers it, or the INFO log lines); (b) `hidden_delivered{class}` shows only `failure` and `needs_human`, and `hidden_delivered{class=failure}` by title is reviewed for hook noise (per-tool errors must be absent after Task 2.5e); (c) **legacy comparison, rewritten**: the legacy checks swallow events before they reach the bus, so `would_suppress` cannot be a superset of `legacy_hidden_suppressed` (different populations). Instead: for `legacy_hidden_suppressed{class=routine}` nothing is expected downstream (after 2b those events publish and the gate suppresses them; Story 2.9 proves it with a test per site), and for `legacy_hidden_suppressed{class=failure|needs_human}` the per-day volume by `type` and `site` is listed and reviewed before 2b: that is exactly the new-delivery volume the 2b commit will unlock, and it goes into the 2b PR description; (d) **unresolved, with a stated bound** (see "Accepted residual leak" below): `unresolved{class=routine}` per day is listed and every entry classified; any `unresolved_resolved_later` increment fails the soak (it means the index missed a session that exists); (e) `rpc_unversioned_total` is zero (installed `ssq-notify` matches the repo); (f) **the evidence is the `GetDeliveryGateStats` output, not an OTel dashboard**: the Stage 3 / 2b PR description pastes the RPC output whose server-computed `soak_streak_hours` is at least 24 and whose `stats_file_status.writable` is true. `soak_streak_hours` is the sum of `gate_on_seconds` (capped by `uptime_seconds`) over the buckets **since the last off flip** (the whole 72-bucket window when there is none), counting **only buckets whose `routine_events_while_on` is non-zero** (a bucket with only failure-class traffic exercises no suppression), so a restart, a mid-soak rollback or a zero-traffic or failure-only window cannot be mistaken for a pass, and an off flip restarts the streak instead of locking the figure out for 72 hours (review-repair iteration 2, ADV-N3, ARCH-C25); a figure from an unwritable stats file is lost on restart and does not satisfy the stage; (g) no explicit `false` at the global value or at any kind override (the flag's `status_detail` is empty, Task 2.9d); (h) **outcome, not only acceptance**: the before/after table of hidden-session pushes, toasts and history rows (Story 1.5 baseline versus the post-soak re-measurement, Task 2.9e) is in the 2b PR description and recorded in `requirements.md` Success Metrics.

## Accepted residual leak (stated bound; requirements.md metric aligned)
The success metric "zero routine hidden-session deliveries" is a **steady-state** target with enumerated, individually countable exceptions, because fail-open on `Unresolved` is deliberate (ADR-002). The bound:
- **Sessions created or restored by this process**: zero leaks. The index is seeded synchronously from the loaded instances in `BuildRuntimeDeps` before any instance is wired or started (Task 2.3c-b), and every session created by this process is upserted in the create path before its tmux pane exists (Task 2.2c). Proven by the startup test and the created-before-hook test.
- **Start-up window**: between process start and `storage.LoadInstances()` (`server/dependencies.go:652`) there are no session-scoped producers, so the `Seeded()==false` window carries no hidden-session events by construction (verified order: load at `:652`, wiring loop `:876`, start loop `:930`). Measured by `notification_gate_index_miss_total` during start-up (expected 0).
- **Known stragglers**: (a) events arriving after a restart for a hidden session that was deleted before the restart (tombstones are in-memory only, 24h TTL, LRU 10,000), (b) the first event of a session created by a path Spike 1.3e did not find (the async refresh primes the index for later events), (c) a renamed session until the rename feed point upserts the new title (Task 2.2c handles rename). Each increments `notification_delivery_unresolved_total{class}` and logs WARN `delivery_unresolved_fail_open`.
- **Acceptance**: `unresolved{class=routine}` for a session that exists in storage is a defect (`notification_unresolved_resolved_later_total` must stay 0). For deleted-before-restart stragglers the operator accepts a small non-zero count, reviewed in the soak, with no numeric budget invented here (UNKNOWN until the soak shows the real rate). The version-skew case (old `ssq-notify`) is a separate, intentional fail-open (Story 2.5).

## Risk Control
- **Feature flags**: `hidden_session_gate` (server), `notification_tray_v2` (web) and `hidden_session_readonly_guards` (server, default on, global only; Story 5.2, O7 repair: the non-deploy escape hatch for the unary read-only guards), `hidden_session_reply` (server, default on, global only; Task 5.6j: the Reply kill switch; Reply rollback never goes through the guards flag) and `terminal_write_lease` (server, default on, global only; Task 5.0f: with it off the write lease is non-exclusive, today's behavior, and Reply returns `NOT_SENT`). All live-settable via `UpdateFeatureFlag`, registered in `knownFeatureFlags` (`server/services/feature_flag_service.go:169`). `notification_tray_v2` is global. `hidden_session_gate` has a global value plus **per-hidden-session-kind overrides** (`review`, `triage`, `diagnose`, `other`; Story 2.11, ADR-004, operator decision O1). Precedence, most specific first: kind override, then explicit global, then the registry default. The gate reads a `FlagCache` (never `LoadConfig` on the publish path). Only flags marked scopable in `knownFeatureFlags` accept a scope.
- **Gate flag semantics (ADR-004, revised)**: flag **off = today's behavior for hidden-session suppression** (all seven legacy checks still in place, new push-status and auto-approved paths ungated) plus shadow counters; the flag-independent changes are listed under Stage 0. Flag **on** = the policy applies on top of the legacy checks in PR 2a. The legacy checks are deleted only in PR 2b, in the same commit that flips the default to on. After that commit, flag off means "deliver everything for hidden sessions" (the documented escape hatch; failure direction is over-delivery, never a lost notification). **Explicit `false`**: a `hidden_session_gate: false` persisted in `config.json` survives the default flip (`config/config.go:1793`, `GetFeatureFlagWithDefault` honors an explicit false), so after 2b it would silently re-open the flood. The plan does not override an explicit operator choice; instead 2b (a) logs WARN `hidden_session_gate_explicit_false` at startup, (b) sets the flag's `status_detail` to "Gate is OFF: hidden sessions deliver everything" so the Settings panel shows it, through a **composite provider** (ordered contributions joined with "; ") owned by Task 2.8g, because `SetStatusDetailProvider` keeps one provider per flag name (`server/services/feature_flag_service.go:326-333`, `statusDetailProviders[name] = fn`) and Tasks 2.8g (writer not running), 2.9d (explicit global false) and 2.11g (per-kind false) all write this flag's detail, so the last registration would otherwise hide an explicit false (review-repair iteration 2, ADV-N4), (c) lists the check in the Stage 3 prerequisites, and (d) names it in the 2b PR description and `docs/reference/notification-delivery-gate.md`. **Per-kind overrides are covered the same way** (Phase 4, pre-mortem #3): a persisted `false` override for any `HiddenKind` (`feature_flag_scopes`) also logs the WARN (one line per kind) and puts "Gate is OFF for kind review" (or the kinds) into `status_detail`, and the Stage 3 prerequisite is mechanical: `GetDeliveryGateStats` shows an empty `soak.explicit_off_scopes`, i.e. no explicit false at the global value and none at any kind (read separately from the composite `status_detail`, which also carries benign text such as "stats writer not running", ARCH-C33c). "Reset to default" (`mutation = RESET_GLOBAL`) deletes the explicit global key; rolling the flag off during the soak is exactly how an explicit `false` gets written, so the Stage 2 checklist ends with "flip back on or Reset to default, never leave an explicit off".
- **Staged rollout**:
  - Stage 0: merge PR 2a with the flag default off. Hidden-session suppression is unchanged because every legacy check is intact. **Flag-independent behavior changes at merge, all called out in the PR description**: (1) the `ssq-notify` enum fix (ADR-003), (2) `delivery_class` / `ssq_notify_schema` metadata stamps from the hook handler (inert unless the gate is on), (3) **a session entering Crashed now publishes a FAILURE notification** (toast, push, history) for visible AND hidden sessions (Story 2.10, own commit, revertible alone): a crash is silent on every channel today, `PermanentlyFailed` already notifies the same way, and the failure direction is over-delivery (recorded plan assumption: the operator decided hidden crashes (O3); notifying visible crashes applies the same standard; restricting it to hidden sessions is a one-line condition if vetoed; the storm risk for visible sessions is mitigated by minimal coalescing built into the same commit, Task 2.10d: at most 3 individual crash notifications per 60s, the rest summarized in one), (4) the in-process counter mirrors, the hourly `delivery-gate-stats.json` file and the `GetDeliveryGateStats` RPC (read-only observability, no delivery effect), and the audit sink core with its `flag_change` lines (Task 2.8h: creates `<config dir>/audit/` mode 0700 and the 0600 file the first time a gate flag is flipped; no delivery effect); (5) **at contract PR 1, before 2a**: `IsPendingDecision` replaces `IsActionableType` as the one predicate behind `is_pending_decision`, `Clear` and `ClearByIDs`, so an unread WARNING stamped `auto_remediating=true` is no longer pinned and no longer protected from "Clear history" (Story 3.2, ADR-008); listed in the contract PR 1 description. In the PR text, "PR 2a" means the pair PR 2a-1 and PR 2a-2; the gate flag may be turned on only after 2a-2 (the stats RPC) has merged, **and that is now enforced, not prose**: `hidden_session_gate` is registered in `knownFeatureFlags` only in PR 2a-2 (until then `UpdateFeatureFlag` rejects the name as unknown, `feature_flag_service.go:380-390`), and once registered, enabling it returns `FailedPrecondition` while the stats writer is not running (Task 2.8g).
  - Stage 1: verify on the manual instance (CLAUDE.md recipe, ports 62871/62872, never `make install-service`).
  - Stage 1b (forcing function, Phase 4, pre-mortem #5): a gate that ships dark is not shipped. **Owner: the operator.** When **both PR 2a-2 and PR 2e have merged** (PR 2e is a prerequisite: the review-only flip needs the per-kind override, and the reminder never degrades to a global flip; ADV C15), the merging agent (or the operator, in the same sitting) creates **three** dated backlog items with `create_backlog_item`: "Flip `hidden_session_gate` on for kind `review`" due 7 days after that merge, "Flip `hidden_session_gate` globally (Stage 3 prerequisite (2) needs the global figure, not only the `review` one) once the `review` phase shows nothing unexpected in `would_suppress`/`hidden_delivered`" due 10 days after it, and "Open PR 2b (default-on + legacy deletion) once `soak_streak_hours >= 24` with `stats_file_status.writable`" due 14 days after it (review-repair iteration 2, minor m3); the dates and ids are recorded in the PR 2a-2 and PR 2e descriptions and in the checklist at the top of `docs/reference/notification-delivery-gate.md` (Task 2.8e). The Settings status line ("Shadow: N hidden events would have been suppressed in the last 24h", Task 2.8d) is the standing visual reminder: `would_suppress` growing while `suppressed` stays 0 means the project is not done.
  - Stage 2: operator flips the gate on live (first with the `review` kind override alone if wanted, then global), 24-48h soak against the Observability criteria. The evidence is the `GetDeliveryGateStats` output (Observability criterion (f)). The checklist includes the **synthetic probe procedure**, ends each probe with "the probe row appeared in the tray within seconds" (a silent drop is noticed, not read as a stalled counter), (review-repair iteration 4, ADV-N25; **default applied, the operator may override**: a live probe is allowed, visibly labelled, tray-only, and counted because it traverses the real gate) and ends with "leave no explicit off". **Procedure**: unless an organic hidden failure **and** an organic hidden needs-human event were already delivered while the gate was on, the operator picks a **live hidden session** from Background activity (no hidden test session is created: VERIFIED `Hidden: true` is set only by `SpawnReviewSession`, `SpawnDiagnosticSession` and triage, `server/services/session_service_diagnose.go:60,106`, `backlog_service_triage.go:3266`, and `CreateSessionRequest` has no hidden field; the Story 2.6c e2e helper runs against the isolated test-mode server on a random port, `tests/e2e/global-setup.ts`, so it cannot target the live instance) and runs against the live port `ssq-notify -s '<that session title>' --type error -p medium -t '[synthetic-probe] gate failure-path check' -m 'ignore this notification'`, and the same with `--type question` for needs-human (mapped to `INPUT_REQUIRED` by ADR-003's fix, Task 2.5; confirmed in Spike 1.3a). If no hidden session is live the probe waits for one, or an organic event is accepted. **`-p medium`, not `-p low`** (review-repair iteration 6, ADV-N30): VERIFIED at `origin/main` `a91e7e46a`, `SendNotification` returns "Notification suppressed for hidden session" for `hidden && Priority == LOW` **before any event is published** (`server/services/notification_service.go:139-144`), and the plan keeps every legacy check until PR 2b, so a `-p low` probe would never reach the gate and would move no counter. `-p medium` keeps it off push (`shouldNotify` pushes only an urgent notification, or an approval-type one, `server/push/subscriber.go:128-136`; for `--type question`, that it is not approval-type is INFERRED and confirmed in Spike 1.3a), and Slack is invoked from the review-queue and callback paths, not from `SendNotification` (`research/features.md` item 12; INFERRED, not re-run), so the probe lands in toast, tray and history only, carries the visible `[synthetic-probe]` label and is dismissed by the operator. **No metadata hint is honored for it** (a `probe=true` hint that skipped push or Slack would let any session label its own failures to hide them). It counts toward `failure_delivered_while_on` and `needs_human_delivered_while_on` because it traversed the real `ssq-notify -> SendNotification -> resolver -> gate` path, and it is also counted in `probe_delivered_while_on`, so the Stage 3 PR shows how many counted deliveries were synthetic; a probe row in the `hidden_delivered{class=failure}` title review (criterion (b)) is expected and labelled. **What the probe exercises and what it does not: it proves the `ssq-notify` bus path only.** Not exercised, and checked another way: the crash builder (`sessionExitedPublisher`, Story 2.10: T-CR-01 and T-CR-02 in CI, no live check), the status-change push path (Story 2.4, `AllowStatusChange`: its CI tests and the soak's `suppressed{channel=push_status}` counter), the approval and `INPUT_REQUIRED` path from `approval_handler.go` (an organic hidden approval or question in the soak, or its CI tests) and `markSessionPermanentlyFailed` (`session_driver.go:1004`: T-CR-05 in CI). A producer counts as live-verified only when an organic event for it was seen, and the Stage 3 PR template says so in one sentence. Prerequisite checklist: installed `~/.local/bin/ssq-notify` matches the repo (verify the file hash against `scripts/ssq-notify`) and `rpc_unversioned_total` is zero (ADR-003); the server fails open for a hidden session's unversioned request in the meantime (Story 2.5, `HintUntrustedType`).
  - Stage 3: PR 2b, one commit that flips the default on and deletes the legacy checks. Prerequisites (all mechanical, each pasted into the PR description): (1) `GetDeliveryGateStats` returns an empty `soak.explicit_off_scopes`, i.e. no explicit `false` at the global value in `config.json` and none at any per-kind override (`feature_flag_scopes`) (this field is read instead of the composite `status_detail`, which can be non-empty for a benign reason such as a stopped stats writer), or the operator explicitly accepts the WARN + status line; (2) the `GetDeliveryGateStats` output with `soak_streak_hours >= 24`, **`stats_file_status.writable == true`, `failure_delivered_while_on >= 1` and `needs_human_delivered_while_on >= 1`** (an empty, zero-traffic or failure-only window, an unwritable stats file or an off flip inside the last 24 hours does not satisfy criterion (c); an earlier off flip only restarts the streak; the bucket that contains the off flip is excluded, and a window in which the failure path or the needs-human path was never exercised does not pass, review-repair iterations 3 and 4, ADV-N19, ADV-N25; the PR description also pastes `probe_delivered_while_on` and says that the probe proves the `ssq-notify` path only; **the probe may satisfy `failure_delivered_while_on >= 1` by itself** (default applied, review-repair iteration 6b, ADV-m22; the operator may override to organic-only), and the PR description states whether the criterion was met by the probe alone, i.e. whether `failure_delivered_while_on == probe_delivered_while_on`); (3) the reviewed new-delivery volume from soak criterion (c) is in the PR description; (4) the before/after table (Story 1.5 baseline versus the post-soak re-measurement, Task 2.9e) is in the PR description. (5) if the binary was ever rolled back to an older build, the per-kind overrides are re-verified (an older binary's `SaveConfig` drops `feature_flag_scopes`). A flag-off rollback after this commit re-opens the hidden-session flood; that is accepted and documented, not hidden.
  - Stage 4: operator runs the prune RPC as a dry run, reviews, then applies.
  - Tray flag follows Epics 3/4 the same way (default off; off = legacy list and modal panel). **PR 3 merges behind the default-off flag; the operator records the ADR-009 device checks DV-1..DV-9 (Task 3.7d) on his own Android and iOS devices, which agents cannot run, in the PR 3 thread BEFORE turning `notification_tray_v2` on**; DV-1 failing switches to the documented bottom-dock fallback (CSS/variant change) before the flag is enabled.
- **Rollback**: Stage 0-2: flip the flag off (= today's behavior). After Stage 3: flip off (deliver-everything) or `git revert` PR 2b (restores the legacy checks; no schema). `.bak` restores pruned history.
- **Reply residual risk (operator decision to keep Reply, review-repair iteration 3)**: Reply is the only feature in this plan that types into a live agent on the operator's say-so from a phone, and its worst case is plain: **a typed answer lands in the wrong dialog, and if that dialog is a permission dialog the answer can approve a tool run in a hidden agent** (option 1 is "Yes"). The `hidden_session_reply` kill switch stops further replies but **cannot undo a keystroke that was already sent**. The design lowers the chance but does not remove it: every dialog-producing hook supersedes pending questions, a live pane fingerprint (with the question text in the region) is compared before the first byte and again before the Enter, the keystroke plan is a function of the question shape (a digit never gets a trailing Enter), the hook that registers a question must carry a sender proof, and every send is audited before it happens. Not closed: (a) the gap between the last live-fingerprint compare and the keystroke write, which cannot be closed from userland (microseconds to milliseconds, plus one write already in the syscall; `DigitOnly` shrinks the worst case to a digit typed into a dialog that drew in that window), (b) operator-typed partial input that sits outside the fingerprint region (INFERRED: the input line is not in the question-and-options region), and (c) a hostile local process of the same user (a change during the settle wait is no longer a residual: the pre-Enter compare catches it; review-repair iteration 4, ADV-m11). Mitigation by sequencing, not by cutting: Reply is **last in Epic 5**, nothing else depends on it, it starts only after Spike 1.3g has recorded its results **and a re-review of the Reply design passes**, and it can be switched off in one flag flip (ADR-010 "Residual risk"). The reduced alternative the review offered ("View output" only, no typing) is what ships until then. **Accepted limit (default applied in review-repair iteration 4; the operator may override): Reply works only for sessions started after hook proof ships**; a session alive at upgrade shows "Reply unavailable for this session (started before reply support was enabled)" until it is restarted (Story 5.6, ADR-010 decision 2d4).

## Operator decisions (O1-O8 all DECIDED)
Decided earlier (do not reopen): hidden sessions notify only for failures and needs-human; routine completions never notify on any channel. O1-O6 were decided by the operator before plan iteration 3. **O7 arose in Phase 4 (pre-mortem #1) as a planner default and was confirmed by the operator on 2026-10-09; O8 (appetite and scope) was decided by the operator on 2026-10-09** (triad iteration 1). `requirements.md` carries a one-line pointer to this table.

| ID | Decision | Choice (DECIDED) | What it changed |
|----|----------|------------------|-----------------|
| O1 | Per-scope feature-flag override (requirements.md "Risk Control") | **Build it.** Scope = hidden-session kind (`review`, `triage`, `diagnose`, `other`), `hidden_session_gate` only; precedence kind override > explicit global > default (Story 2.11, ADR-004) | New Story 2.11; ADR-004 rewritten; requirements.md Risk Control no longer deviates; `UpdateFeatureFlagRequest` gains `scope` and the `FlagMutation` `mutation` enum |
| O2 | Hidden-session `INPUT_REQUIRED` ("Claude has a question") under read-only | **Add an audited Reply control now.** One typed RPC, `ReplyToPendingQuestion`, answers only an outstanding question; audited, rate-limited, rejected when nothing is pending, a pinned row in the Story 5.1d caller table (ADR-010, ADR-005 exception) | New Story 5.6; Story 5.1d/5.3/5.4 and `design/ux.md` Surface 12 updated; requirements.md scope item 2 records the single write exception |
| O3 | Hidden crash / permanent failure is a failure-class notification | **Yes.** `PermanentlyFailed` already publishes an ERROR via `markSessionPermanentlyFailed` (`session/session_driver.go:1004`); a **Crashed** transition has no producer today, so Story 2.10 adds a FAILURE notification from `sessionExitedPublisher` (`server/services/session_service_events.go:109`). Visible crashed sessions also gain it (plan assumption, see Stage 0) | Story 2.10 rewritten; `StatusKind` and the push-path crash builder removed from the design |
| O4 | Prune of unread actionable hidden rows | Default never deletes them; `include_unread_actionable=true` is an explicit per-run opt-in | Unchanged (Story 2.7) |
| O5 | Which Stretch items to cut | **SUPERSEDED by O8 (2026-10-09).** The former Stretch items (Quiet mode, tray hotkey, `top-sheet` and `landscape-panel` variants, push service-worker handoff) are in scope, sequenced last within their epic, each keeping its dependencies and gating spikes (for example the hotkey chord spike) | Stretch labels and the cut list are removed; Stories 4.5 and 5.5 and Tasks 4.2e and 4.2f are ordinary scope |
| O6 | Desktop tray handle overlaying terminal columns | **Fixed desktop handle**; keep the Task 4.2h visual check; escalate only if columns are lost | Task 4.2h unchanged; no draggable-handle work |
| O7 | Backlog "Steer" composer on hidden review sessions vs the Story 5.2 steer guard (pre-mortem #1) | **DECIDED: confirmed by the operator on 2026-10-09 (option (a)): preserve the shipped behavior through a narrow, typed, tested path.** Evidence (VERIFIED at `013856269`): `BacklogItemDetail.tsx:628` calls `updateSession(s.sessionId, { steerMessage })`; `isSteerable` returns true for `review` role sessions that have not ended (`web-app/src/lib/backlog/sessionKind.ts:52-55`); `SpawnReviewSession` creates them `Hidden: true` (`server/services/session_service_diagnose.go:54-60`); the steer branch is `session_service_update.go:~313-322`. Without the exemption Story 5.2 would turn every backlog steer of a live review session into `FailedPrecondition`. **Options considered**: (a) **chosen**: the `UpdateSession` steer branch allows a hidden target only through `access.BacklogSteerWriter(link)` where `link` is a `BacklogReviewLink` (hidden kind `review`, not ended, backlog-linked); audited in the Story 5.6 sink; a **second** UI exception whose entry point takes the `BacklogReviewLink` parameter (a pinned `acquirer` row, Story 5.1d); a characterization test is written and green on `main` BEFORE the guard lands (Task 5.2d); the unary guards sit behind the live-settable `hidden_session_readonly_guards` flag. (b) block steer for every hidden session and remove steer from the backlog UI for review sessions (pre-mortem claims this breaks backlog-operator-feedback-loop Epic 2.2; UNVERIFIED by this patch). (c) allow steer for all hidden kinds (rejected: re-opens the write ADR-005 exists to block) | Story 5.2 (Tasks 5.2d-f), Story 5.1d pinned-caller table (two UI exceptions, one token-gated steer writer and the `acquirer` rows), ADR-005 decisions 3, 5 and 8, ADR-010 boundary sentence. **Option (b) was rejected**; for the record it would have meant: drop Tasks 5.2d (keep the characterization test as the removal test) and 5.2e, shrink the allowlist back to one entry, and add a Backlog UI task to hide the composer for review sessions |

Also confirm when reviewing ADR-005: `RestartSession` / `RestartShell` reject hidden targets when called from the UI RPC handlers (decided in ADR-005; internal callers unaffected).

## Defaults applied (operator may override)
Planner defaults applied in review-repair iterations 4 and 5. None is an operator decision of the O1-O8 kind, none cuts scope (O8), and each is reversible by the operator; they are listed so a reader can overrule one without reading the repair log.
1. **Merge-order split (ADV-N26, ADV-N27)**: PR 1f becomes PR 1f-a (the raw request-record stamp, which PR 2a-2 needs) and PR 1f-b (the verdict, the guard set and the verified-hostname set, which PR 2c and PR 5u need), and PR 5 becomes PR 5 (Stories 5.1, 5.1d, 5.2, 5.3, the read-only guarantee; split again in iteration 6 into PR 5 streams and PR 5u unary), PR 5b (5.4), PR 5c (5.5) and PR 5r (5.6, Reply). Sequencing only; the scope is unchanged. *Override*: merge in the earlier bundled order.
2. **Live Stage 2 probe policy (ADV-N25)**: a live probe is allowed against an existing hidden session, carries the visible `[synthetic-probe]` label, uses `-p medium` (a `-p low` probe is dropped by the legacy hidden-session check at `notification_service.go:139-144` before the gate) so it is not pushed, honors no metadata hint, is counted toward the delivered counters because it traverses the real gate, and is shown separately as `probe_delivered_while_on`; failure and needs-human are separate counters and Stage 3 requires both; the probe proves the `ssq-notify` path only. *Override*: organic events only (Stage 3 then waits for them).
3. **Wedged-lease policy (ADV-N21)**: one tray-level WARNING (never pushed) per wedge episode after `LeaseWedgeWarnAfter`; the remedy is the existing Pause or Delete; no forced release and no "unstick" control. *Override*: a WARN log only (the driver's prompt and answer key then stop silently) or a pushed notification.
4. **Reply works only for sessions started after hook proof ships (ADV-N23)**: a session alive at upgrade shows "Reply unavailable for this session (started before reply support was enabled)" until restarted; the cause is counted. *Override*: restart the existing sessions once at rollout.
5. **Hook proof shape (ARCH-C37)**: keyed to the session UUID, no nonce, delivered through a per-session 0600 proof file outside the worktree, read only when present (fail-open), instead of argv, only the two curl builders proofed (the other four senders are not replyable), a proofless duplicate deduped by dialog digest while a different proofless dialog still supersedes.
6. **`terminal_write_lease` flag default on (ARCH-C36c)**: off = a non-exclusive lease (today's behavior) and Reply returns `NOT_SENT`.
7. **Verified-hostname set (m12)**: recomputed from `validateFn` every detector cycle (default 5 minutes), never from `d.networks`; static from boot when the detector is disabled.
8. **`UpdateSession` `program` and `auto_approve` refused for a hidden target (ADV-N28)**, with the `hidden_session_readonly_guards` flag as the escape hatch like the other unary guards.
9. **The operator's backlog WIP cap of 2 is not lifted** for this project; calendar time follows from it (see "Wall-clock blockers"). *Override*: lift it.
10. **Simplified guard set (review-repair iteration 5)**: the write-surface guard machinery is typed capabilities plus four cheap checks, and the whole-tree five-class scan (rules L1-L3, P1-P3, S1-S3) is a deferred upgrade that Task 5.1d-0 may add, never a default; no behavioral protection is removed. *Override*: build the heavier scan now (Story 5.1d returns to about 575k raw, 479k CU).

Git hygiene: stage specific files only; never `git add -A`; open PRs ready for review (this repo overrides the draft default); never force push.

**Order of merges** (state of the PR chain, not a time line): `1 -> 1c`; `1d` after the re-review PASS and the stats and `FlagMutation` shapes are settled; `1f-a` (the request-record stamp; independent; no PASS needed; **before `2a-2` and `1f-b`**); `1g` (Task 2.6d alone; startable now; **before `5`**); `1f-b` (the verdict, the guard set and the verified-hostname set, after the re-review PASS on the verdict and after `1f-a`; **before `2c` and `5u`**, not before `2a-2`); `2a-1`; `2a-2` (needs `1d` and `1f-a`; carries the audit sink core, Task 2.8h); `2e` (needs `2a-1`, `2a-2`, `1d`; before the Stage 1b reminders are created); `[soak]`; `2b`; `2c` (after `1f-b` and `2a-2`); `3` any time after `1c`; `4` after `3`; `5a` (the write lease; **after `2a-2`**, which carries the audit sink core its flag flips are recorded by; before `5u`); `5` (streams: Stories 5.1, 5.3 and the stream half of 5.1d; 5.3 first or together with 5.1; needs only `1g`; no Reply in it); `5u` (Story 5.2 and the unary half of 5.1d: Tasks 5.2b and 5.2e are one PR and **need `5`, `5a`, `1f-b` and `2a-2`**); `5b` (Story 5.4, after `4` and `5`); `5c` (Story 5.5, after `5`); `1e` after Spike 1.3g and after the `ReplyOutcome` enum is complete; **`5r` (Story 5.6, Reply) last, after `1e`, `5`, `5u`, `5a`, `1f-b`, `2a-2`, Spike 1.3g and the re-review of the Reply design**. The graph is acyclic (checked in review-repair iterations 4 and 5; iteration 6 added `5a -> 2a-2`, split `5` into `5` and `5u`, and re-checked by script, below): `1f-a`, `1c`, `1d` and `3` depend on no later item; `1f-b -> 1f-a`; `2a-2 -> {1d, 1f-a}`; `2c -> {1f-b, 2a-2, 2b}`; `5a -> 2a-2`; `5 -> 1g`; `5u -> {5, 5a, 1f-b, 2a-2}`; `5b -> {4, 5}`; `5c -> 5`; `5r -> {1e, 5, 5u, 5a, 1f-b, 2a-2, spike}`; `1g` depends on nothing (iteration 6b re-walk: still acyclic, no back edge).

**Shared-file merge order** (Epics 3, 4 and 5 all edit `NotificationContext.tsx`, `NotificationPanel.tsx`, `NotificationToast.tsx` and `NotificationItem.tsx`; the contract PRs remove only the proto collision): (1) Story 3.1 (extract `ToastStack`, split the context) lands before any other web story touches those files; (2) Story 3.6 (the `Announcer`) lands before any story adds a live role or a status line (Stories 4.4, 5.3, 5.6 call `announce()`); (3) Epic 3 merges before Epic 4, and Epic 4 before the Story 5.4/5.6 client parts that edit `NotificationItem.tsx`/`NotificationToast.tsx`/`BackgroundActivity.tsx` (Task 5.3f's testid change may land with PR 3 if the hidden variant ships earlier); (4) each later PR rebases on main before it opens and re-runs `pnpm run lint:duplicates`. The backlog WIP cap of 2 concurrent sessions means the web files have one writer at a time.

## Effort (price-weighted cost, not human time)
Method: `~/.claude/skills/sdd/skills/ESTIMATION.md`. There are **no human-time figures** in this plan: the earlier day, week, per-task-minute and tolerance-band figures were removed (triad iteration 1). A **task** is one worker-agent run in one focused context (at most 3-5 files). A **story** is its tasks plus the verification multiplier. Bands and weights are **INFERRED**; recalibrate after the first implementer run.

- **Weights** (fresh input 1, cache write 1.25, cache read 0.1, output 5) and an **assumed mix** of 12% output with the remaining 88% split 8:10:70 across fresh input, cache write and cache read give about **0.9 CU per raw token** for implementation stories (0.12x5 + 0.08x1 + 0.10x1.25 + 0.70x0.1 = 0.875) and about 0.69 for the read-heavy spike and measurement stories at 8% output (INFERRED; the harness reports one undifferentiated token total, so conversion is by this assumed mix).
- **Class midpoints** used for the table: XS 20k, S 55k, M 130k, L 240k raw tokens per task. No L task is planned (Tasks that looked L were split in triad iteration 1: 2.3c, 3.8b, 4.3b, 5.1c, 5.6c, 2.8c).
- Wall-clock blockers are listed separately below; they are not in the token estimate.

**Sum and verification multiplier.** 224 tasks sum to about **15.1M raw tokens** and about **13.1M CU** before verification (the story-row sums were re-added by script after this patch's edit, and equal the subtotals: raw 1820 + 3690 + 720 + 2455 + 2205 + 4250 = 15,140k, CU 1463 + 3226 + 634 + 2150 + 1940 + 3712 = 13,125k; the same script first reproduced the previous revision's 15,290k raw and 13,242k CU, and 224 tasks, from the unedited table). The per-story verification multiplier of x1.5-2 (spec-compliance and code-quality review per story, `6-verify` layers, CI reruns) takes that to about **19.7-26.3M CU** (midpoint 23.0M). Size band, informational only (not a scope gate, operator decision O8): **XL** (over 15M CU; Large is 3-15M); the plan proceeds, with no question and no cut. **Change from the previous revision (15.3M raw, 13.2M CU, 19.9-26.5M after verification), review-repair iteration 5 (guard-machinery simplification): -150k raw and -117k CU, about -1.0% raw and -0.9% CU. Story 5.1d goes from M (4xM + 1xS) 575k raw, 479k CU to M (2xM + 3xS) 425k raw, 362k CU: the spike (Task 5.1d-0) drops from M to S because it derives one short table instead of running five rule families over the tree, Task 5.1d-c drops from M to S because its fixtures shrink from about fifteen controls to about eight, and Task 5.1d-d drops from M to S; Tasks 5.1d-a and 5.1d-b stay M. The task count stays 224 and the story count 42 (no task was added or removed: the spike and the tasks were re-pointed and re-classed). The cost is a ceiling the spike can raise: if Task 5.1d-0 finds a real gap and adds the heavier scan or one of its rules, Story 5.1d returns toward its previous 575k raw (the removed machinery was priced as the old spike M, Task 5.1d-a M and Task 5.1d-c M). All INFERRED from file count and shape, not measured.** Planning overhead already spent (research, nine review and repair rounds, two triad rounds) is not in this total. The class counts per story come from the task list in this file (recounted by script); the per-task class assignment is INFERRED from each task's files and shape, not measured.

**Critical path in agent waves** (INFERRED; a wave is a set of runs that can proceed concurrently): (a) **server chain** `2.1 -> 2.2 -> 2.3 -> {2.4, 2.5, 2.6} -> PR 2a-1 -> {2.8, 2.10} -> 2.8h -> 2.8g -> PR 2a-2 -> PR 2e -> [soak] -> 2.9 -> 2.7`: about 10 waves plus the soak (2.8h then 2.8g are serial tail commits of 2a-2, and 2e sits before the Stage 1b reminders; PR 1f-a, a small record stamp, runs beside Epic 1 and is the only part of Story 1.8 that 2.8h waits on; PR 1f-b is a prerequisite of 2.7 and PR 5u, not a link of this chain); (b) **web chain** `3.1 -> {3.2, 3.5, 3.6} -> {3.3, 3.4, 3.7, 3.8, 3.9} -> 3.10 -> 4.1 -> 4.2 -> {4.3, 4.4} -> 4.5`: about 12-14 waves and the longest chain, because Epics 3 and 4 serialize on the shared files above (one writer at a time under the WIP cap); (c) **Epic 5 chain** `5.0 -> {5.1 -> 5.1d-0 -> 5.1d -> 5.2 (5.2d, then 5.2b+5.2e as one PR, then 5.2f), 5.3} -> 5.4 -> 5.5 -> 5.6 (5.6k, 5.6l and 5.6n beside 5.6b)`: about 10-11 waves (Story 5.0 is a leading link, the 5.1d-0 spike is one more, which review-repair iteration 5 made shorter but not removed so the wave count is unchanged, and Reply is the last link and now its own PR 5r, so the read-only guarantee in PR 5 no longer waits on it), with 5.2 additionally behind PR 1f-b and PR 2a-2 and 5.6 additionally behind Spike 1.3g, Contract PR 3, the re-review of the Reply design, PR 5, PR 5u, PR 5a, PR 1f-b and PR 2a-2. With two concurrent streams the web chain sets the agent-wave critical path; the calendar, however, is set by the wall-clock blockers below, not by the waves. **Calendar caveat (review-repair iteration 4)**: the wave counts assume unconstrained concurrency; the operator's backlog WIP cap of 2 concurrent sessions, not the waves, sets the calendar for 224 tasks ("Wall-clock blockers"), so the waves are a lower bound on elapsed time and not an estimate of it; no human-time figure is given.

**Triad repair 1 and this table (2026-10-09).** No task was added: the phone approval card (Task 3.8d), the stable `TrayEntryChip` (Task 4.2i), the width checks (Task 4.2a), the Pin tray mode (Task 4.2e), the settings and "What changed" card (Task 4.4h), the XA-16 and XA-17 checks (Task 4.5d), the IME guard (Task 5.6f), the release note (Task 5.6e), the outcome-metric work (Tasks 1.5a, 1.5b, 2.9e, 3.10b) and the flaky-test requirements (Tasks 2.1d, 2.6a, 2.8f) are folded into existing tasks of the same cost class, so the per-story rows, the subtotals and the sums above are unchanged. The Pin tray mode and the approval card are the two additions most likely to exceed their task's class (Task 4.2e and Task 3.8d); if the implementer finds that, the overrun checkpoint below applies and the task is split then. The table is not re-priced (INFERRED).

**Overrun checkpoint**: if spend on completed stories exceeds the table value by more than **25%**, pause and report the delta in CU with its cause (a wrong estimate or a real surprise). Informational, not an invitation to cut (operator decision O8).

## Sequencing and PR order (decision and rationale)
- **Why the gate first (R1).** It removes the daily flood on every channel (web push, toast, history, Slack, auto-approved) with no UI change, has the smallest blast radius (flag default off, shadow counters first, legacy checks kept until default-on) and is the only part that needs no mobile device. Its cost is the largest single epic, so the plan keeps its first slice small: PR 2a-1 is the gate with a **global flag only**; the stats RPC and the crash notification follow in 2a-2 (the soak needs them); the prune RPC (2c) is **not** in R1 because it does not affect whether the flood stops; the per-kind override (2e) **is** in R1 since review-iteration 1 because the Stage 1b review-only flip depends on it.
- **R2 versus R3 order (decided).** Toast stack (R2) before hidden-session reachability (R3). Reasons: (1) at comparable cost class, the toast stack is startable now (Contract PR 1 only) while Epic 5 waits on Spike 1.1, Spike 1.3g, Contract PR 3 and the focused re-review, so ordering it first removes a blocked dependency from the web stream; (2) once R1 ships, hidden-session events are only failures and needs-human, so the dead-end link is hit only on those (rare) events, whereas the unmanageable toast burst is hit on every multi-notification burst of visible sessions too (INFERRED; the Story 1.5 baseline counts both so the order is checked against data before R2 starts); (3) PR 5 cannot ship its server guards without the client view anyway. This reverses the earlier R2/R3 order.
- **Release boundaries (not cut lines; everything ships, O8).** A merge or release can stop at any of these and still be coherent: **R1** = Epic 1 + Contract PRs 1 and 2 + PR 2a-1 + PR 2a-2 + PR 2e + the soak + PR 2b (fixes the routine hidden-session flood on every channel; the contract stubs returning `CodeUnimplemented` are inert). **R2** = Epic 3 toast stack behind `notification_tray_v2` (merging it changes nothing until the device checks are recorded). **R3** = Story 5.0 (the lease, own PR 5a) first, then PR 5 (streams: Story 5.3 together with or before 5.1 and the stream half of 5.1d: client read-only view, deep link, "View output"; server stream guards; the unary guards, O7 path and guard flag are PR 5u; **neither waits on Reply**), **where 5.2 needs PR 1f-b and PR 2a-2 as well**, so the read-only guarantee merges without Reply (**until PR 5u merges, "read-only" in R3 means the attach streams only**: `WriteToSession` and the other unary writes are not yet guarded and the banner copy says "Terminal input is disabled in this view", ADV-N36); Story 5.6 (Reply, PR 5r) is the last item, only after Spike 1.3g, a passing re-review of the Reply design, Contract PR 3, PR 5, PR 5u, PR 5a, PR 1f-b and PR 2a-2. **R4** = Epic 4 tray and the late-sequenced items, Story 5.4 Background activity, and Story 5.5. Never merge: Stories 5.1/5.2 without 5.3; Epic 4 without Epic 3 (it is stacked); Story 5.4 without Epic 4; the Reply (5.6) without Spike 1.3g, 5.3, Story 5.0 and Contract PR 3; Story 5.2 without Story 5.0, PR 1f-b and PR 2a-2.

## Late-sequenced items (formerly Stretch; IN SCOPE, operator decision O8)
These were beyond `requirements.md` in the earlier plan and are now ordinary scope, **sequenced last within their epic** and keeping their dependencies and gating spikes:
- Story 4.5 Quiet mode: after Story 4.4 (it only touches the context and the tray header).
- Task 4.2f tray hotkey and shortcut-dialog entry: after Task 4.2g; **the chord spike comes first** (`Alt+N` is an unverified candidate: macOS dead key, terminal `ESC n`); if the spike finds no safe chord the hotkey is not shipped and TH-3/TY-4's hotkey half record "no safe chord" (that is a finding, not a cut).
- Task 4.2e `top-sheet` and `landscape-panel` variants: after Task 4.2g and Spike 1.2 (they extend `trayVariant.ts`); until it lands the capped bottom sheet serves the keyboard-open and landscape cases.
- Story 5.5 push service-worker handoff: after Story 5.3.
- The mobile top dock, the 1-line top chip with the keyboard open and the landscape cap in Story 3.7 are first-wave (ADR-009): `requirements.md` requires toasts to clear the soft keyboard and the input line.

Story criteria quote only a subset by id; the rest are owned **by reference**: the ux.md criterion is the acceptance criterion of the owning story below, and `implementation/validation.md` has one test row per id. TE-1..TE-7 are no longer "by reference": Tasks 3.3e and 4.3e-4.3g own them. Closed during plan repair (VERIFIED by opening the files; cite in Spike 1.3 results):
- **Requirements scope item 5 / Open Question 2 ("Claude Notification ... via tmux")**: RESOLVED, not a hidden-session leak and not an ungated channel. The subtitle is `source_app=tmux`, set by `scripts/ssq-hook-handler:231-235` (`[[ -n ${TMUX:-} ]]` for any stapler-squad-managed tmux session) and rendered as `via ${sourceApp}` by `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationToast.tsx:158` and `NotificationItem.tsx:83`; the title is the hook default "Claude Notification". The named session `staplersquad_stapler-squad-background-llm-model-pinning-r2` has `hidden=0` in `sessions.db` (the "background" is the backlog item slug), so it is a visible Backlog work session, and its type-1 (APPROVAL_NEEDED) priority-2 record is covered by the type-keyed policy, not by hidden gating. Evidence: `research/features.md` section 2 (lines 22-26) and `research/pitfalls.md` section 0 (lines 6-14). Recorded for readers in Task 4.5c; guarded by validation T-SN-06, T-SN-08, T-E2-30; `requirements.md` Open Question 2 is marked resolved. The session-record claim (`hidden=0`) comes from the research agent's read-only query and was not re-run in this patch.
- **MCP vs `WriteToSession`**: no `server/mcp` code calls `TerminalService.WriteToSession`; MCP write tools call `session.SendKeysWithTimeout` (`server/mcp/tools_terminal.go:325`) and `inst.SendKeys` (`:402`) directly, so a unary guard in `terminal_service.go` does not affect dispatchers.
- **Steer guard placement** (`steerInstance` is the pre-split name: after review-repair iterations 2 and 3 its two callers reach `steerUnderLease` plus `steerAuthorized` and `steerInternal`): `steerInstance` (`server/services/session_service_update.go:371`) has two callers: the `UpdateSession` steer branch (`:320`) and `SteerActiveSession` (`session_service_liveness.go:101`, used by autonomous/backlog steering). The access **decision** goes at the top of the `UpdateSession` handler (before any mutation), never inside the writer, which only requires an unexported `steerAuthorization` token. **Review-repair iteration 2 (ARCH-NB1)**: `UpdateSession` is itself a generated handler method and calls the writer at `session_service_update.go:320`, so a scan rule "functions on the internal list are not callable from handlers" would fail on the plan's own design; `steerInstance` is therefore split into the token-gated `steerAuthorized(ctx, auth, inst, msg)` (the only steer writer a handler may call) and the thin internal `steerInternal(ctx, inst, msg)` (used by `SteerActiveSession`, `session_service_liveness.go:95-101`, no handler caller), and token construction is confined to one defining file by the capability-literal lint (Story 5.1d check (a); review-repair iteration 5 replaced the scan rules S1-S3 with that lint and with pinned callers). (review-repair iteration 1; the earlier text put the guard in the branch at `:315-320`, after title, tag and note mutations had already been applied). The branch is also the shipped backlog "Steer" composer's path to hidden `backlog:review` sessions (`BacklogItemDetail.tsx:628`, `sessionKind.ts:52-55`, `session_service_diagnose.go:54-60`), which is why the guard carries the O7 exemption (Story 5.2).
- **RQM `suppressForHidden` redundancy**: redundant for routine reasons (poller `shouldSkipSession` and `Determine()` Hidden gate); see Tech Debt Disposition. Slack wiring is unreachable for routine reasons, so no Gate rewiring.
- **Autonomous L619**: review-role runs return at ~L549 before the generic notifier, so there is no duplicate and the documented "intentionally none" holds.
- **Bus wiring point (revised in iteration 3)**: the bus is built in `server/services/session_service.go:1008,1022` (`events.NewEventBus(100)`), `deps.EventBus = svc.EventBus` at `server/dependencies.go:648`, producers are wired at `dependencies.go:856,865,878,1144` and `session_service.go:970`, all before `server/server.go:300`, which sits inside the `else` of `if configErr` (notification store block, `server/server.go:285-335`). So the filter is installed at bus construction and the `Gate` owns its index from construction. `BuildRuntimeDeps` calls `storage.LoadInstances()` at `dependencies.go:652`, then wires each instance (`:876`) and starts them in a background goroutine (`:930-933`); seeding the index synchronously right after `:654` therefore precedes every restore-time producer (`onColdRestoreLostHistory`, rate-limit callbacks). `server.go` only wires the consumers (push, approval) and the ticker (Task 2.3c-c).
- **Crash and PermanentlyFailed producers (VERIFIED)**: `markSessionPermanentlyFailed` calls `GetNotifier().Notify(inst.UUID, ..., 7 /*ERROR*/, urgent, important)` (`session/session_driver.go:1004`), so PermanentlyFailed already has a bus producer. `MarkCrashed` (`session/instance_crash.go:40-52`) fires `EventExited`, whose only server listener, `sessionExitedPublisher` (`server/services/session_service_events.go:109-117`), publishes a `session.updated` with `status`; `buildStatusChangeNotification` returns false unless `Status == Stopped` (`server/push/subscriber.go:173`). A crash is therefore silent on every channel today, for visible and hidden sessions.
- **Guidance mechanisms (VERIFIED)**: `GuidanceRequestService` (`proto/session/v1/guidance_request.proto`) is a durable agent-initiated question store: `AnswerGuidanceRequest` persists the answer and notifies (`server/services/guidance_request_service.go:317-350`); it never types into a terminal, and `Answer` has no ownership check (single-operator posture). `AskUserQuestion` is a different flow: the hook is deferred to the native terminal dialog (`server/services/approval_handler.go:420-428`) and the notification carries `nil` metadata (`:759-773`). So Reply does not use the guidance store, and (re-review ARCH-B1) it does **not** reuse the steer send primitive either (`session.SubmitContentWithEnter`, `session/pane_submit.go:141`, blindly retries Enter at `:126`): it has its own `SubmitReplyOnce` (Task 5.6k); session-scoped guidance requests on a hidden session stay answerable through the existing RPC, which is not a terminal write.

## Pending focused re-review (NOT resolved; a PASS is required before the dependent stories start)

**Stop rule for the focused re-review (triad repair 1; engineering).** The re-review ends at the first run that reports **0 blockers: 0 blockers = PASS**, whatever the number of concerns. Concerns and minors of a passing run are recorded in `implementation/architecture-review.md` and `implementation/adversarial-review.md` and in the repair log as accepted or follow-up items; they are **not** looped through another review round. A run that reports one or more blockers restarts only the repair-then-re-run cycle for those blockers. A PASS covers the text as reviewed; a later edit that changes a reviewed design (a new capability, a changed state machine, a changed claim lifecycle) is re-reviewed for that edit only, not from the start. Why a stop rule: the seven passes so far each found fresh defects in whatever machinery was newest (NB1, NB3, NB6, NB7, ADV-N22, ARCH-C35; see the paragraphs below), so "no findings at all" would never arrive; a blocker (a design that can type twice, bind to the wrong dialog or approve a permission dialog) is the stop condition and a concern is not.
`implementation/architecture-review.md` and `implementation/adversarial-review.md` (both now overwritten with the focused re-review's own text) were first written before four late additions. **This plan does not claim these items are reviewed or resolved.**

**Re-review iteration 1 (2026-10-09) found 7 blockers: 4 architecture (ARCH-B1 release-on-failed-write can type the reply twice, ARCH-B2 "authenticated like every other UI write" is false on `:8543`, ARCH-B3 the scan allowlist cannot be "exactly two", ARCH-B4 cwd-prefix attribution can register a question under the wrong session) and 3 adversarial (ADV-B1 the same double-typing root as ARCH-B1, ADV-B2 the claim binds to "a question was asked" rather than the dialog on screen, ADV-B3 the stats RPC and file cannot evidence "gate on for 24h with non-zero hidden traffic"); ARCH-B1 and ADV-B1 share a root, so they are 6 distinct defects.** The architecture file lists concerns C1-C14; the adversarial file lists C1-C17 in its body (its header says 14) plus 6 minors. **The review-repair iteration 1 patch (2026-10-09) edited plan.md, ADR-004, ADR-005, ADR-010, validation.md, design/ux.md and requirements.md for all seven blockers and every concern group (see "Repair log (Review-repair iteration 1)"). That patch has had no independent review: the coordinator re-runs the focused architecture + adversarial re-review next, and every blocker below stays unverified-resolved until it passes.** 

**Re-review iteration 2 (2026-10-09) found 2 blockers on the architecture side and 0 on the adversarial side**, and the **review-repair iteration 2 patch (2026-10-09) edited them; the re-review is to be re-run and nothing below is marked verified-resolved.** Architecture: **NB1** (the scan forbade `UpdateSession` from calling `steerInstance`, which the design requires at `session_service_update.go:320`; patched by splitting the writer into the token-gated `steerAuthorized` and the internal `steerInternal` and making token construction a scanned property) and **NB2** (the claim was bound to `StatusNeedsApproval`, the permission-dialog state, and to a fingerprint taken at promotion, so a permission dialog appearing after an answered question could be promoted and later approved by a Reply; patched by superseding on every dialog-producing hook, requiring a question token in the fingerprint region, bounding promotion to seconds and replacing the hard-coded status with the status set Spike 1.3g records). The architecture file lists concerns C15-C26 and the still-PARTIAL C1, C12 and C14; the adversarial file lists N1-N12 and minors m1-m7 (0 blockers); the patch edited plan.md, ADR-004, ADR-005, ADR-010, validation.md, design/ux.md and requirements.md for every one of them (see "Repair log (Review-repair iteration 2)"). Status of every row: **patched in review-repair iteration 2; focused re-review pending (not re-run)**.

**Re-review iteration 3 (2026-10-09) found 2 blockers on the architecture side (NB3, NB4) and 0 on the adversarial side.** NB1 and NB2 are resolved in design per that review (residual concerns C31 and C32 below). **NB3**: scan rule S4 required every listed writer to reference the non-reentrant `TerminalWriteLease`, but the listed writers call each other, so the O7 steer and every internal steer would have seen "busy"; **NB4**: the rebinding gate's Host allowlist included unverified, network-controlled reverse-DNS names. Concerns C27-C33 (architecture) and N13-N20 and minors m8-m10 (adversarial) came with them, plus the recommendation to defer Reply. **The review-repair iteration 3 patch (2026-10-09) edited all of them** (the lease is a passed `*HeldLease` with one acquirer per chain and its own Story 5.0 and PR 5a; S4 is replaced by L1-L3; the Host allowlist is the verified set with the `:8444` and IP-literal rules decided; see "Repair log (Review-repair iteration 3)"). **The operator chose to keep Reply in scope**; it is sequenced last in Epic 5, hard-gated on Spike 1.3g and on a passing re-review of the Reply design, and ships behind `hidden_session_reply`. **The re-review is to be re-run on this patch and nothing here is marked verified-resolved.**
**Re-review iteration 4 (2026-10-09) found 2 blockers on the architecture side (NB5, NB6) and 0 on the adversarial side.** NB3 (scan rule S4 against the non-reentrant lease) is **resolved in design** per that review (the passed `*HeldLease`). NB4 was reopened as **NB5** (the verified-hostname set was fed from `d.networks`, which holds unverified boot-time names and IP literals, so the hostile-PTR hole stayed open and T-RP-65 would have passed anyway) and **NB6** (rule L1 applied to every classified write, including the table's own "no lease" rows and the inner session layers, so the scan would fail on the real tree). Concerns C34-C41 (and C28, partial) and N21-N28 with minors m11-m13 came with them. **The review-repair iteration 4 patch (2026-10-09) edited all of them** (the verified set is recomputed from `validateFn` each cycle and never from `d.networks`; the write surface is partitioned into five classes with an explicit primitive-layer boundary, a scan-feasibility spike and a positive control; a busy lease no longer burns a dialog-latch attempt; the nesting backstop is stated honestly; PR 1f and PR 5 are split; the hook proof is redesigned; `auth_mode` comes from a real validator; `SwitchWorkspace` is guarded before any mutation; lease ownership is one rule; a wedged lease raises a tray warning and has a tested remedy; the audit lines are ordered by a persisted `seq`; the Stage 2 probe is labelled and split; `UpdateSession`'s restart route is guarded; see "Repair log (Review-repair iteration 4)"). **The re-review is to be re-run on this patch and nothing here is marked verified-resolved.**

**Review-repair iteration 5 (2026-10-09, the last repair round) was a simplification, not a finding-driven round.** The previous repair agent concluded that the write-surface guard machinery was partly over-engineered (the type-level `*HeldLease` and the writer capabilities already make "forgot the lease or the guard" a compile error for every primitive that takes one, which makes a whole-tree L1-L3 scan largely redundant for that class; the five-class partition, the P1-P3 boundary rules and the long pinned lists cost maintenance to guard unreviewed future code; each review pass found fresh defects in exactly that machinery: NB1, NB3 (S4), NB6 (L1), ADV-N22, ARCH-C35). The patch replaced it with four checks and moved the heavier scan to a deferred upgrade owned by Task 5.1d-0 (see "Repair log (Review-repair iteration 5 - simplification)"). **The simplified design has had no independent review.** Item 1 and item 2 of the table below now also cover the four-check guard set and its stated blind spots (ADR-005 decision 3); nothing here is marked verified-resolved, and the re-review must be re-run on the patched text.


**Re-review iteration 5 ("pass 5", 2026-10-09) found 2 architecture blockers (NB7: the capture-pane subprocess helpers were outside the guard set; NB8: the proof-header hook was fail-closed) and 1 adversarial blocker (B1, the same capture-pane route), plus concerns C42-C50 and N29-N31 and minors m14-m19.** The **review-repair iteration 6 patch (2026-10-09) edited all of them** (see "Repair log (Review-repair iteration 6)"): the three helpers join check (d), a tmux-subcommand literal lint and an injectable `tmuxInputSender` seam with a new fixture T-RO-48 close the class; the hook command is fail-open and its proof file is per session and outside the worktree; PR 5 is split into streams and unary; `5a -> 2a-2` is an explicit edge. **The re-review is to be re-run on this patch; nothing here is marked verified-resolved.**

## Phase 1: Discovery

### Epic 1: Spikes (PR 1, `docs:`)
**Goal**: Replace every "INFERRED" in the research with a verified fact before code depends on it. Output is edits to this plan's Unresolved Questions and, where a repro test is worth keeping, a spec that lands in Epic 5.

> **AWAITING OPERATOR (written 2026-10-09 by the agent worker for PR 1; none of the items below was run or faked).** Agent-runnable spikes 1.3b, 1.3c, 1.3d, 1.3e, 1.3f, the static part of 1.3h, Story 1.5 (history and sessions-DB part) and Task 5.1d-0 (syntactic pass) have results recorded under their tasks. Everything that needs a live manual instance or a device is below. Record each answer under its task in this plan.
>
> **Setup (once).**
> 1. Ports: `ss -ltnp | grep -E '62871|62872|62873|62874'`. At 2026-10-09 18:30 port `62871` was **already held** by pid 716335 (`STAPLER_SQUAD_INSTANCE=scroll-verify-clean`, binary `~/.stapler-squad/manual-builds/manual-1/stapler-squad`, started from another worktree): stop it yourself if it is stale, or use block #2 below. Never use `:8543`/`:8444`.
> 2. Build this branch: `make build` (generates proto and ent; this worktree has none), then `mkdir -p ~/.stapler-squad/manual-builds/manual-2 && go build -o ~/.stapler-squad/manual-builds/manual-2/stapler-squad .`
> 3. Start (fresh instance name; `claude-manual-test*` dirs already hold old state): `PORT=62873 STAPLER_SQUAD_INSTANCE=claude-notif-spike STAPLER_SQUAD_TMUX_SOCKET=ssq-manual-2 ~/.stapler-squad/manual-builds/manual-2/stapler-squad --tmux-keep-server &` and confirm the log line `Using private tmux socket` in `~/.stapler-squad/instances/claude-notif-spike/logs/staplersquad.log`. Remote access off (no `--remote-access`); if you enable it pass `--remote-port 62874`. Teardown: `kill %1; tmux -L ssq-manual-2 kill-server`.
> 4. **`scripts/ssq-notify` defaults to port 8543 (`scripts/ssq-notify:61`)**: always pass `--port 62873` (or `CS_SERVER_PORT=62873`), or the notification lands on the live instance.
>

Record the table `--type -> stored notification_type`. Unblocks Story 2.5 sizing and the Stage 2 probe policy (`--type question` must map to INPUT_REQUIRED after ADR-003).

Run the four repros (toast "View Session", tray/history row click on `/notifications`, push click with the app closed, deep link `/?session=<uuid>&tab=terminal` with a populated list), recording per repro: result, and the console line `[URL] Session not found` if any; plus the `web-app/src/app/page.tsx:243` early-return log on a cold `/?session=<hidden-uuid>` with `ListSessions` returning `[]`, and, at 390x844, whether an app Header and BottomNav render and where the memory/fork-pressure banner sits. #### Story 1.2: Radix non-modal vs xterm focus, and gesture-listener audit
**As a** engineer, **I want** to know whether the focus primitive and swipe handlers are safe with xterm, **so that** Epic 3/4 pick the right implementation.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- A throwaway harness (not committed) shows `Dialog.Root modal={false}` open/close over `XtermTerminal`: terminal textarea keeps `document.activeElement`, terminal `cols`/`rows` unchanged, no `body` style mutation.
  - *Given* a focused xterm textarea in a session page on the manual instance, *When* a non-modal Radix dialog opens then closes via Esc, *Then* `document.activeElement` is the xterm textarea and `term.cols x term.rows` are equal before and after (record the numbers).
- Mobile focus (C11): on a real phone (or Chromium mobile emulation with the keyboard var set), opening a non-modal sheet by tap leaves the xterm textarea focused, the soft keyboard visible, and sends no terminal resize.
  - *Given* the soft keyboard open and the xterm textarea focused, *When* a non-modal sheet is opened by tap with no `.focus()` call, *Then* `blur` on the textarea does not fire and no resize message is sent (record the observation; if a tap on the sheet itself blurs the textarea, record that and the `onMouseDown preventDefault` / `tabindex=-1` mitigation tested).
- `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useTerminalGestures.ts` document-level `touchmove`/`touchend` registration (reported ~L755-792) is read and the conflict rule written down.
  - *Given* a touch that starts on a toast element above the terminal, *When* it moves 80px horizontally, *Then* the audit states whether `useTerminalGestures` sees the `touchmove` (and so whether `stopPropagation` on the row is sufficient).
- Coarse-pointer focus and the expanded sheet (triad UX B2; needs a physical phone for the real soft-keyboard leg, a wall-clock blocker, operator-run; emulation with the keyboard CSS var covers the rest).
  - *Given* the keyboard CLOSED and a non-trapping expanded sheet closed by tap, *Then* record whether `document.activeElement` stays off the xterm textarea (no keyboard summoned). *Given* the keyboard OPEN and Expand tapped with no `.focus()` call and no focus trap, *Then* record that the textarea keeps focus, no `blur` fires and 0 resize messages are sent. *Given* a modal focus trap with `inert` on the background, *Then* record whether the textarea blurs and the keyboard collapses (expected yes, which is why the coarse-pointer sheet is non-trapping, Story 4.2).
**Files**: `web-app/src/components/ui/Modal.tsx` (reference), `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useTerminalGestures.ts` (read), `web-app/src/lib/window/useWindowSwipe.ts` (read), this plan.
