if the shape is not a single-answer prompt the Reply card says "Open the terminal to answer" and offers no composer, and with no question text it says "Open the terminal output to read the question"; (b) which pane state the dialog produces (`inst.GetDetectedStatus()`) while open, after it is answered and with no controller (`StatusUnknown`), and how long after the hook fires the dialog becomes visible (the confirmation poll schedule for the `candidate` to `confirmed` step, and the **promotion window in seconds** from the hook after which an unconfirmed candidate expires; the 30-minute TTL is for confirmed entries only); (c) which keystrokes answer it (option number, typed text, "Other" free-text semantics, arrows) **and the resulting `ReplyKeystrokes` mode**: if a digit key selects immediately the mode is `DigitOnly` and no Enter is ever written (a stray Enter would pick the default of the next dialog); the mode and its reply validator are spike output, not a hard-coded "content then Enter" (ADV-N11); (d) the **pane fingerprint** rule: the capture accessor (a live capture, not the cached status), the dialog-region boundaries, which **must include the question text and the option labels** (so two questions with the same option layout differ) and exclude animated lines, whether the hash is stable across idle redraws, and what happens when a second question B is **never registered** (then only the fingerprint separates A from B; it is **mandatory**, not "evaluated": a mismatch right before the first byte is `STALE_PROMPT` with 0 writes); (e) whether a second hook arrives for a queued or parallel question and what the pane shows then (the supersede rule is acceptance regardless); (f) the **detector status set** chosen for "the AskUserQuestion dialog is on screen" (`StatusInputRequired`, `StatusNeedsApproval`, or an explicit set; the detector has both, `session/detection/detector.go:93-94`, and a permission dialog is `StatusNeedsApproval`, so the set must **exclude** whatever a real permission dialog produces), always with an active controller, never the `StatusUnknown` or "no meaningful output" fallback; it is a cached pre-filter (`GetDetectedStatus` returns the controller's last result, `session/instance_state.go:326-338`), the live fingerprint is the guard; (g) every hook event that fires when a dialog appears (each is wired to supersede pending questions, ARCH-NB2), whether a **permission dialog raised right after an answered question** is detected as a different status and fingerprint, and whether the session driver's auto-answer (`shouldApprovePrompt`, `session/session_driver.go:1338-1354`, sends `"1\n"` at `:365`) can fire on an `AskUserQuestion` dialog; (h) **checked first, in this order** (review-repair iteration 3: ARCH-C31, C32, ADV-N15, N16): (h1) the real payload shape and whether a `QuestionToken` can be derived from it (the code reads `tool_input["prompt"]` at `approval_handler.go:759-762`; if the real payload nests the text under a `questions` array every question is registered not replyable and Reply never appears: the spike then reports "Reply not replyable" and the plan returns to the operator); (h2) whether each hidden kind (review, diagnose, triage, other) has an **active status controller** while its question is open (`statusManager.GetStatus(inst).IsControllerActive`, `session_service_liveness.go:83-85`, `session/instance_state.go:326-355`), otherwise `NOT_WAITING` is permanent for that kind; (h3) the longest question token that survives terminal wrapping at 80 columns (the comparison normalizes whitespace; the token length is the smaller of 40 runes and that length); (h4) the **keystroke plan per question shape**, `(question_shape, reply_text) -> ReplyKeystrokes`: for a select dialog, whether a digit selects immediately; for a dialog with an "Other" text field, whether text plus Enter is the way; a shape that is not recorded is not replyable; (h5) the **selection-glyph** normalization for the fingerprint (the cursor glyph on an option line moves when the operator arrows in the terminal); (h6) the **forged-hook negative test**: a hook whose `tool_input` token is dialog boilerplate must not promote onto a permission dialog or onto any dialog whose title region differs, and the boilerplate denylist is recorded here; (h7) the measured **supersede frequency** with a visible session in the same repository, split by cause (`question`, `permission_request`, `path_share`), before Contract PR 3 freezes the UX. (h8) the **hook-proof rollout facts** (review-repair iteration 4, ADV-N23; since iteration 6b the variable is the already-injected `STAPLER_SESSION_UUID`, so h8 checks only that a hook subprocess inherits it, not that a variable exists): whether Claude Code snapshots its hooks at start (does a rewritten `settings.local.json` reach a running agent? INFERRED yes, it does not), whether `curl -H @file` works on the operator's machines (curl 7.55 or newer; the local build here reports 8.22.0), and the per-sender table of Story 5.6 (c) (the six known senders of `X-CS-Session-ID` and which of them are proofed). **Hard gate**: Story 5.6 does not start until this item records a reliable predicate and fingerprint rule; if detection is unreliable the plan returns to the operator (ADR-010 consequences). ##### Task 1.3e: Index feed points
- Files: this plan.
- **RESULT (2026-10-09; VERIFIED by reading, line numbers at `e835b8090`)**.
  - `Hidden` is **write-once**: set only from `InstanceOptions.Hidden` in `session.NewInstance` (`session/instance.go:1188`) and on restore in `instance_serialization.go:357`; there is no setter (no `Hidden =` outside construction, no hide/unhide RPC; `proto/session/v1/session.proto` has `hidden` only on the response `Session` message, `types.proto:237`, and `include_hidden` filters). So the feed points are **construct, restore, delete/archive**, not "hide/rename/tag-change" for the flag itself; `HiddenKind` derives from tags, so tag changes still feed it (Spike 1.3h).
  - Producers of `Hidden: true` (grep): `SpawnReviewSession` (`session_service_diagnose.go:55-61`, tag `backlog:review`), `SpawnDiagnosticSession` (`:101-107`, `backlog:diagnose`) and the re-review spawn `backlog_service_triage.go:3326-3333` (`backlog:review`; **the plan's `:3266` is stale and the site is a review, not a triage**). All three go through `SessionService.CreateDirectorySession` (`session_service_diagnose.go:122`). `CreateWorktreeSession` (`:177-`) also forwards `opts.Hidden` but no current caller sets it (its caller `backlog_service_triage.go:1094` builds `spawnOpts` without `Hidden`). `session.NewInstance` callers: `session_service_diagnose.go:148,216`, `session/create_managed_instance.go:144`, `session/instance_checkpoint.go:242` (none of the last two copies `Hidden`).
  - **ASSUMPTION WRONG (ordering)**: "create upserts before the tmux pane can emit a hook" is **not** satisfied if the upsert sits where the plan expects (`AddInstance` / `NewSessionCreatedEvent`, `:166-178`): `CreateDirectorySession` calls `instance.Start(true)` at `:157`, which starts the tmux session and the agent, **before** `s.storage.AddInstance` (`:166`) and before `eventBus.Publish(NewSessionCreatedEvent)` (`:173`). The index upsert must therefore be made right after `session.NewInstance` returns (`:148`, `:216`) or inside `NewInstance` itself, before `Start`. A first hook racing the upsert is INFERRED possible (hooks fire seconds after launch; not measured).
  - Restore: `BuildRuntimeDeps` calls `storage.LoadInstances()` at `server/dependencies.go:650` (plan: `:652`); a synchronous seed after it precedes `wireCallbacks`/`StartController` for restored instances, as the plan states.
  - **tmux-name key**: `tmux.NewSessionName(d.Title, d.TmuxPrefix).String()` (`approval_handler.go:901`; sanitized title with the stored `TmuxPrefix`); also match the raw title and the UUID (identity forms in Task 1.3d).

##### Task 1.3f: Lock-holder inventory and `NewEventBus` call sites
- Files: this plan.
- **RESULT (2026-10-09; VERIFIED for the lexical scan and the three named chains, INFERRED beyond them)**.
  - `grep -rn '\.Publish(' --include='*.go' . | grep -v _test | wc -l` = **108** (matches the plan). A throwaway lexical scan (lock acquired earlier in the same function and not released before the `Publish`) flagged 6, all false positives or a different bus type: the `session/detection/ratelimit` manager has its **own** `ratelimit.NewEventBus` (`session/detection/ratelimit/manager.go:61`, a different type; its external callbacks run as `go externalCallback(det)` after `m.mu.Unlock()`, `:207-212`), `session_service_stream_terminal.go:442` (a `sendMu` that guards the stream, publishing a user-interaction event after the write), `session/workspace/registry.go:162` (a distributed notifier, not the event bus).
  - The three named chains: `ReviewQueue.Add` releases `rq.mu` (`session/queue/queue.go:292`) **before** `observer.OnItemAdded` (`:297`), so `ReactiveQueueManager.OnItemAdded` does not run under a queue lock; `fireLifecycleEvent` copies the listeners under `lifecycleListenersMu` and calls them after unlocking (`session/instance_controller.go:168-177`); `pmMu` guards only the lazy `processManager` construction (`session/instance_tmux.go:318-331`). `rqp.mu` is taken only in short accessor sections (`review_queue_poller.go:210-298,348,457,676,1002`). **No `Publish` was found under `rqp.mu`, `inst.mu` or `pmMu`**; a caller-chain proof for every one of the 108 sites was not done (stated limit). Independently, `EventBus.Publish` is non-blocking toward subscribers (`pkg/events/bus.go:86-93`, `select`/`default`) and takes only `bufMu` and `mu.RLock`, so a lock-free filter in front of it is safe under any caller lock.
  - `NewEventBus` call sites (non-test): `server/services/session_service.go:1012` and `:1026` (the plan's `:1008,1022` are stale), plus the unrelated `ratelimit.NewEventBus`. The bus reaches the runtime as `CoreDeps.EventBus = sessionService.GetEventBus()` (`server/dependencies.go:396`) and `BuildRuntimeDeps` reads `svc.EventBus` (`:646`). **Gate plumbing**: put the field on `CoreDeps` (`ServiceDeps` embeds `*CoreDeps`, `:410-412`, so `svc.Gate` resolves) or, simpler, install the gate inside the bus at construction (as Task 2.3c-a plans) so no field is needed; the plan's "expected `ServiceDeps.Gate`" works only through that embedding.

##### Task 1.3h: Crash dedupe, crash event ordering, reachable `HiddenKind` values (`kind:triage` is shown and accepted only if this spike proves it reachable), `SendNotification` callers
- Also confirm the ordering Story 2.10 depends on: `MarkCrashed` completes `transitionToLocked(Crashed)` inside `sendSyncErr` before `fireLifecycleEvent(EventExited)` (`session/instance_crash.go:40-52`, VERIFIED by opening the file), and that the asynchronous `go func()` in `sessionExitedPublisher.OnLifecycleEvent` (`server/services/session_service_events.go:109-117`, VERIFIED) reads `Snapshot().Status` only after that transition is published (INFERRED). List every other `EventExited` source (the engineering triad lens reported three: an unexpected-exit path near `session/instance.go:1491`, `MarkCrashed`, and `session/backlog_lifecycle.go`; UNVERIFIED here) and confirm none reaches the publisher with a stale non-Crashed status.
- Files: this plan.
- **RESULT (2026-10-09; static part VERIFIED by reading; the live dedupe run is AWAITING OPERATOR, O-4)**.
  - **Dedupe, by code**: `Append` collapses by `(session_id, notification_type)` regardless of read state (`server/notifications/store.go:201`, `findDuplicate` `:298-305`) into `mergeOccurrence` (`:243-262`): `OccurrenceCount++`, `LastOccurredAt`, title/message/metadata replaced, `IsRead=false`, record moved to front. So a second crash of a resumed session produces **no new history row** but resurfaces the same row as unread (live history shows this pattern: 269 of 352 rows have `occurrence_count > 1`). Toast and push are fed by the bus, not the store, and push uses per-event `shouldNotify` (`server/push/subscriber.go:122-141`: priority URGENT or type APPROVAL_NEEDED), so they re-fire (INFERRED; the subscriber also coalesces same-key events inside one 500 ms flush, `server/notifications/subscriber.go:14,89`). **Consequence for Story 2.10**: the history record is not a re-fire signal; the notification id must include the exit time only if the row must be distinguishable, otherwise the occurrence counter is the "second crash" evidence.
  - **Crash is silent today**: `MarkCrashed` has one caller (`session/health.go:479`, `handleCrashedPane`, after `respawnDeadPane` fails) and `buildStatusChangeNotification` pushes only `Stopped` (`server/push/subscriber.go:173`). Confirms plan line 418.
  - **`EventExited` sources (grep, non-test)**: `session/instance.go:1491` (control-mode exit: `sendSyncErr` to Stopped first, then fires; ordered), **`session/instance_controller.go:93` (`pty-eof`: transition via `i.send`, which does not wait, then fires immediately: a listener can observe a non-Stopped status)**, `session/instance_crash.go:54` (Crashed; ordered) and `:76` (Stopped; ordered), `session/review_queue_poller.go:542` (`reconcile-session-missing`: transition inside `sendCtx` with a 2 s timeout, fires regardless), and `session/native_process_manager.go:146` (invokes the exit callback, which lands in the `instance.go` path). **`session/backlog_lifecycle.go:820` is a listener, not a source**: the triad lens's third source is wrong. Only `MarkCrashed` can produce Crashed, so Story 2.10 depends only on its ordering, which holds. **Residual race (INFERRED, not observed)**: `MarkCrashed` first calls `KillSession()` (`:41`), which can trigger the control-mode exit callback (`instance.go:1435-1491`) to move Active to Stopped first; then `MarkCrashed`'s actor check `Status != Active` returns `ErrInvalidTransition` and no Crashed event is ever fired. Task 2.10's tests should include that interleaving.
  - **Reachable `HiddenKind` values**: `review` (`SpawnReviewSession`, and the re-review spawn `backlog_service_triage.go:3326`) and `diagnose` (`SpawnDiagnosticSession`). **`kind:triage` is NOT reachable**: `backlog:triage` appears only at `session/session_driver.go:570` (comment) and `:1131` (`HasTag` read); there is no tag constant for it (`session/backlog.go:108` defines only `TagBacklogReview`) and no `Hidden: true` producer sets it. Per this task's rule, **`kind:triage` is not shown and not accepted by Story 2.11**. `other` has no current producer; the live sessions DB nevertheless holds hidden sessions: `select hidden,count(*)`: 60 hidden = 55 `backlog:review`, 4 `backlog:diagnose`, 1 untagged (legacy). Tag changes: tags live in `session_tags`; whether the tagging engine can change a hidden session's `backlog:*` tag after creation was not traced (UNVERIFIED), so Story 2.11's feed must also hook tag mutation or document the gap.
  - **`SendNotification` callers**: the only in-tree caller of the RPC is `scripts/ssq-notify:192` (itself called by `scripts/ssq-hook-handler`); `web-app/src/app/notifications/NotificationsPage.tsx:90` mentions it in a comment only; no Go caller. **`ssq_notify_schema` does not exist anywhere in the tree** (grep over Go, TS, proto and scripts): it is a field Story 2.5 introduces, so "`ssq-notify` is the only caller omitting it" is true vacuously (it is the only caller). Also note `SendNotification` already suppresses LOW priority for hidden sessions (`notification_service.go:142`), a legacy check.

#### Story 1.4: Up-front Contract PR 1 (PR 1c): the proto edits that need neither a spike nor the focused re-review
**As an** engineer on one of the UI epics, **I want** the `session.proto`/`events.proto` edits that depend on no unrun spike and no unreviewed design merged first, **so that** Epics 2b, 3, 4 and 5 rebase onto stable generated types instead of conflicting on the same proto file and the same components (adversarial N6). The scope fields and `GetDeliveryGateStats` (Story 1.6) and `ReplyToPendingQuestion` (Story 1.7) are deliberately **not** here: they are under the pending focused re-review (items 3, 4, 1) and Spike 1.3g, and a merged proto field cannot be changed.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Additive proto edits only, with field numbers re-read from the file at commit time (numbers below were read at `013856269`).
  - *Given* `proto/session/v1/session.proto`, *Then* it gains `NotificationHistoryRecord.is_pending_decision = 14`, `ClearNotificationHistoryRequest.notification_ids = 2`, `ListSessionsRequest.hidden_only = 9` and the `PruneHiddenSessionNotifications` messages and RPC; `events.proto` gains `NotificationEvent.is_pending_decision = 10`. `make proto-breaking` (Task 1.4e; `buf breaking proto --against '.git#branch=origin/main,subdir=proto'`, config `breaking: FILE` already in `buf.yaml`) reports no breaking change. **`buf breaking` runs nowhere today** (no `Makefile` target and no workflow mentions it, searched at `013856269`; only `proto-lint`/`proto-gen` exist), so Stories 1.4, 1.6 and 1.7 could not have met that criterion before Task 1.4e.
- Minimal server behavior so UI epics can build against real values. One predicate, `IsPendingDecision(type, metadata, read)` = `IsActionableType(type)` AND NOT `auto_remediating=true`, is behind the field, `Clear`, `ClearByIDs` and the prune (ADR-008).
  - *Given* an unread WARNING record, an unread INFO record and an unread WARNING record stamped `auto_remediating=true`, *When* `GetNotificationHistory` runs, *Then* `is_pending_decision` is true, false, false; *Given* a live APPROVAL_NEEDED event through `event_converter.go:72`, *Then* the converted `NotificationEvent.is_pending_decision` is true; *Given* the stamped WARNING and `Clear`, *Then* it is deleted, and an unstamped unread WARNING is kept (a flag-independent narrowing of "Clear history", listed in the PR description). *Given* `PruneHiddenSessionNotifications`, *Then* the stub returns `CodeUnimplemented`.
  - *Given* `buildInlineNotification` for an event with `NotificationID`, *Then* `data.url` ends with `&notification=<id>`; `buildStatusChangeNotification` and `buildApprovalNotification` keep the two-parameter URL (`server/push/subscriber_test.go` asserts both forms).
- `make proto-gen` and `make registry-generate` run; generated output not committed.
**Files**: `proto/session/v1/session.proto`, `proto/session/v1/events.proto`, `pkg/events/notification_metadata.go`, `server/notifications/store.go`, `server/services/event_converter.go`, `server/push/subscriber.go`, `server/push/subscriber_test.go`, `server/services/notification_service.go` (stub), `server/services/session_service_delegates.go` (stub registration), `docs/registry/features/`.

#### Story 1.5: Baseline measurement of hidden-session noise (before PR 2a-1 ships)
**As the** operator, **I want** a measured before-picture, **so that** "zero routine hidden-session deliveries" and "at most 3 toasts" are shown as improvements of a known size instead of acceptance criteria (triad product gap 3).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- The 7-day baseline is measured from existing data before the gate ships.
  - *Given* the live notification history file (`<config dir>/notifications.json`, read-only; retention is 7 days, `server/notifications/store.go:19`, so a 7-day window is the whole store) and the sessions DB, *When* the counts are taken, *Then* the report lists per day: (a) history rows whose `session_id` or `session_name` resolves to a hidden session, by `NotificationType` and `HiddenKind`; (b) rows of types that toast (not in `HISTORY_ONLY_TYPES`), as the toast count; (c) hidden-session push estimates; (d) hidden-session failure and needs-human events (the dead-end exposure, used to check the R2/R3 order); (e) the largest burst of rows created inside any 60-second window (a proxy for simultaneous toasts).
  - *Given* pushes are not recorded in history, *Then* (c) is stated as **INFERRED**: rows that pass `shouldNotify` plus hidden Stopped transitions found in the sessions DB (UNVERIFIED method; the limits are written next to the numbers). Rows of deleted sessions are `undeterminable` and counted separately (84 of 319 in the research sample, `research/pitfalls.md`).
- The numbers live in `requirements.md` Success Metrics (baseline column) before PR 2a-1 merges; the identical procedure re-runs after the soak (Task 2.9e) and its output is the "after".
- **Operator-experience outcomes (triad repair 1, product; `requirements.md` "Outcome measurement").** The baseline also records: (f) **interruptions per day** (toast-eligible rows plus the INFERRED push estimate, hidden and visible, per day); (g) **taps to triage a burst**, counted by a script (`tests/e2e/notification-triage-walkthrough.spec.ts`, written here against the unchanged `origin/main` build on a manual instance, seeded with 6 informational, 3 pending approvals and 1 failure) from the burst arriving to an empty deck with every decision acted on or left pinned, and **time to triage** by the operator with a stopwatch on a phone (median of 3 runs, INFERRED, manual); (h) **taps to reach a failing hidden session** from its notification (not reachable on `main`; Spike 1.1 records the four repros); (i) **residual bursts**, the number of 60-second windows holding at least 4 toast-eligible rows after every hidden-session routine row is removed, per week, the largest burst, and the pinned-per-active-hour median and busiest hour (the checks of hypothesis H1 and of the post-soak decision rule).
  - *Given* the baseline is taken, *Then* each of (f) to (i) has a number or the explicit words NOT YET MEASURED with the reason, the walkthrough script is attached to the PR description (it is committed in Task 3.10b and re-run unchanged after R2 and R4), and the post-soak rule table in `requirements.md` is not yet applied (it is applied once, from Task 2.9e's figures).
**Files**: none committed (a one-off command or throwaway script; its text goes in the PR description), `requirements.md`.

The residual-burst count is the pre-registered check of hypothesis H1 and is taken before any Epic 3 or 4 code is written.

- **RESULT (2026-10-09 18:3x local; read-only; snapshot copies of the live state in the agent scratchpad, nothing written under `~/.stapler-squad`; throwaway scripts not committed)**.
  - **Source (VERIFIED)**: the live `:8543` service (pid 2040325, no `STAPLER_SQUAD_INSTANCE`) uses the preferred workspace `~/.stapler-squad/workspaces/d685c4b1a423cca3/` (`~/.stapler-squad/preferred_workspace`): `notifications.json` (352 records, 291 KB, `updated_at` 2026-10-09T18:30) and `sessions.db`. There is **no** `~/.stapler-squad/notifications.json`. Hidden resolution used `sessions.db` (`hidden`, `session_tags`) matched by UUID, title and `tmux_prefix`+title.
  - **ASSUMPTION WRONG (the history is not one row per event, and "7 days is the whole store" is only half true)**: records collapse by `(session_id, notification_type)` with `occurrence_count`/`last_occurred_at` (`store.go:201`, `:243-262`); retention is keyed on `last_occurred_at` (`:661-678`). So 47 of the 352 records have a first occurrence older than 7 days (the oldest is 2026-09-05), 269 have `occurrence_count > 1`, and the per-event day distribution is **not recoverable**: only first stamp, last stamp and count exist. Two figures are therefore reported: **rows** (records whose last occurrence is in the window: all 352) and **exact-window occurrences** (records whose first occurrence is also inside the 7 days: 305 rows, 9,509 occurrences, a lower bound on 7-day events because older-first records also have in-window events). Orphan pruning also deletes rows of deleted sessions, so hidden-session counts are **lower bounds** (60 rows, 3,579 occurrences, are `undeterminable`; 24 of those ids are UUID-shaped and match no session, most plausibly deleted hidden review sessions).

Needs-human exposure: 1 row (the question). So today's hidden noise is not "Task Complete" rows: it is a capacity WARNING that `capacity_monitor.go` publishes with no Hidden check, which the plan's Story 2.4 classification (`delivery_class`) must cover.
  - **(a)-(b) table (rows | occurrences; window = records with last occurrence in the last 7 days)**: visible 235 | 9,256; hidden 12 | 504; non-session (item IDs and system) 45 | 6,266; undeterminable 60 | 3,579. Toast-eligible (not in `HISTORY_ONLY_TYPES`, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useSessionNotifications.ts:19-26`: TASK_COMPLETE, PROCESS_FINISHED, PROCESS_STARTED, STATUS_CHANGE, INFO, DEBUG): 151 rows by `last_occurred_at` day (2026-10-03: 3, 10-04: 8, 10-05: 59, 10-06: 25, 10-07: 19, 10-08: 11, 10-09: 26; about 22 rows per day), of which 12 hidden. Exact-window toast-eligible occurrences: 1,849 (visible 1,170, hidden 443, non-session 113, undeterminable 123), an **upper bound** on toasts because the client suppresses same-session repeats inside `TOAST_DEDUP_WINDOW_MS` (`useSessionNotifications.ts:~120`); two records dominate it ("Session went stale" for one visible session: 893 occurrences; the hidden token-ceiling row: 385).
  - **(c) hidden pushes, INFERRED (method unchanged: history cannot show pushes)**: history rows passing `shouldNotify` (priority URGENT or APPROVAL_NEEDED, `server/push/subscriber.go:122-141`): **0** hidden rows. Hidden Stopped transitions: the push subscriber has **no Hidden gate** (`grep -i hidden server/push/*.go` is empty, VERIFIED), so each hidden session that stops sends a "Session Completed" push (`subscriber.go:166-190`). `sessions.db` holds 37 hidden sessions created in the last 7 days that are now Stopped (hidden: 60 total, 59 Stopped; `updated_at` is rewritten by every save and cannot date the stop): **at most 37 hidden pushes in 7 days, about 5 per day** (UNVERIFIED method; sessions deleted since are not counted, so a lower bound on creation, an upper bound if any stopped earlier than the window). This, not history, is the baseline for "zero routine hidden pushes".
  - **(d)** hidden failure and needs-human rows: 0 failure (types 7 and 9), 1 needs-human (type 2, 61 occurrences). **(e)** largest burst: 16 rows stamped inside one 60 s window (all types, first and last stamps of each record), 6 toast-eligible; both are lower bounds because merged records contribute two stamps.
  - **(f)** interruptions per day: about 22 toast-eligible rows per day (151 / 7) and at most about 264 toast-eligible occurrences per day (1,849 / 7, exact-window rows only), plus at most about 5 hidden pushes per day; the hidden share is 12 of 151 rows (8%) and 443 of 1,849 occurrences (24%). **(i)** residual bursts: 3 sixty-second windows holding at least 4 toast-eligible rows after removing hidden routine rows (greedy, row-stamp granularity, so a lower bound); largest 6. Pinned-toasts-per-active-hour median and busiest hour: **NOT YET MEASURED** (pin state is client-side and not in history). **(g)** taps and time to triage, **(h)** taps to reach a failing hidden session: **NOT YET MEASURED** (needs a manual instance and the operator's phone; see O-6; the walkthrough spec is a new test file and was not written by this docs-only slice). Spike 1.1 still owns (h).
  - **Capacity Alert type bug confirmed in live data**: the record "Capacity Alert" has `notification_type 2` (INPUT_REQUIRED) with 100 occurrences (session `stelekit-dowbolad`, classified `undeterminable`), matching `capacity_monitor.go:489-493` (see Task 1.3c).

##### Task 1.5b: Record the numbers in `requirements.md` Success Metrics and in the Stage 3 checklist; write the exact re-measurement commands
- Also (triad repair 1): fill the new rows of the `requirements.md` outcome table (interruptions per day, taps and time to triage a burst, taps to reach a failing hidden session, pinned per active hour against its target) and the H1 baseline figures, and attach the walkthrough script to the PR description.
- Files: `requirements.md`, this plan.
- **RESULT (2026-10-09)**: numbers recorded in `requirements.md` "Outcome measurement" (rows with a measured baseline) and in the Task 1.5a result above. **Re-measurement commands** (run read-only from any shell; the throwaway script of this slice was `python3 -I` over a copy of `notifications.json` and `sessions.db`; recreate it from this recipe): (1) `S=$(mktemp -d); W=~/.stapler-squad/workspaces/$(basename "$(cat ~/.stapler-squad/preferred_workspace)"); cp "$W/notifications.json" $S/n.json; cp "$W"/sessions.db* $S/` (substitute the live instance's config dir if `STAPLER_SQUAD_INSTANCE` is set; use `ss -ltnp | grep 8543` and `/proc/<pid>/environ` to find it); (2) rows: `jq '[.notifications[] | select(.last_occurred_at >= (now-604800|todate))] | length' $S/n.json`; (3) hidden set: `sqlite3 -readonly $S/sessions.db "select uuid,title,tmux_prefix from sessions where hidden=1"`, match `session_id` by UUID, title, then `tmux_prefix||sanitized title`; (4) exact-window occurrences: records whose `created_at` is within 7 days, summing `occurrence_count`; (5) toast-eligible: `notification_type` not in {4,5,6,10,11,12}; (6) hidden Stopped estimate: `sqlite3 -readonly $S/sessions.db "select count(*) from sessions where hidden=1 and status=3 and created_at >= datetime('now','-7 day')"`; (7) bursts: sort row stamps and slide a 60 s window. After PR 2a-2, `GetDeliveryGateStats` replaces (2)-(6) for the "after". Add `occurrence_count` per day to the store (a per-day counter) if the plan wants exact per-day figures: the merge in `mergeOccurrence` currently discards them.

#### Story 1.6: Contract PR 2 (PR 1d): per-kind flag scope fields and `GetDeliveryGateStats` (starts only after the focused re-review PASS on items 3 and 4)
**As an** engineer on Stories 2.8 and 2.11, **I want** the flag-scope and stats proto edits merged only after they are reviewed, **so that** the flag-mutation semantics and the stats RPC shape (including the gate-on-time fields the soak needs) cannot be frozen wrongly.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Additive edits with field numbers re-read from the file at commit time.
  - *Given* `proto/session/v1/session.proto`, *Then* it gains `UpdateFeatureFlagRequest.scope = 3` and `FlagMutation mutation = 4` (enum values **spelled with the type prefix**, because `buf.yaml` uses `STANDARD` lint, which includes `ENUM_VALUE_PREFIX` and requires the `_UNSPECIFIED` zero value: `FLAG_MUTATION_UNSPECIFIED = 0`, `FLAG_MUTATION_SET_ENABLED = 1`, `FLAG_MUTATION_SET_DISABLED = 2`, `FLAG_MUTATION_CLEAR_SCOPE = 3`, `FLAG_MUTATION_RESET_GLOBAL = 4`; today `name = 1`, `enabled = 2`; the zero value is exactly the legacy "set `enabled`" shape an old client sends and requires an empty `scope`; the set values carry the enabled state, so a set cannot write `false` because the plain `bool enabled` was omitted, ARCH-C26/ADV-N5; **precedence: for every non-zero `mutation` the server ignores the plain `bool enabled`** (so `FLAG_MUTATION_SET_ENABLED` with `enabled=false` is neither a conflict nor an error, and the proto comment says so), and only `FLAG_MUTATION_UNSPECIFIED` reads `enabled`, review-repair iteration 3, ARCH-C33a; `make proto-lint` is part of the PR's evidence, ARCH-C22), `FeatureFlag.scopes = 5` (repeated `FeatureFlagScopeOverride{scope, enabled}`; today fields 1-4) and the `GetDeliveryGateStats` messages and RPC; `make proto-breaking` reports nothing.
  - *Given* the stats response, *Then* it carries, settled **before this PR merges** because it freezes the shape (adversarial ADV-B3): `process_start`, `uptime_seconds`; a `since_process_start` section with every counter at full labels; a `buckets` list of **72** hourly buckets, each with `hour_start`, `uptime_seconds`, `gate_on_seconds` (global) and `gate_on_seconds_by_kind`, `hidden_events_seen`, `hidden_events_while_on`, **`routine_events_while_on`** (hidden events the gate **decided to suppress**: a resolved hidden session, routine class, evaluated while that scope was on; an `Unresolved` session is delivered fail-open and is **not** counted, so a broken resolver cannot accumulate streak hours while suppressing nothing; review-repair iterations 2 and 3, ADV-N19), and counters keyed `(counter, kind, class)` with `kind` in `review|triage|diagnose|other|unresolved`; `flag_history` (the last 20 `{scope, value, at}` changes of `hidden_session_gate`); `events_by_kind_24h`; a `soak` message with `gate_on_hours`, `routine_events_while_on`, `last_off_flip_at` (empty when none in the window), **`failure_delivered_while_on`** and **`needs_human_delivered_while_on`** (two separate counts, since `last_off_flip_at`, of hidden events delivered while the gate was on that traversed the real gate with class failure and needs-human respectively; the earlier merged count let one `ssq-notify --type error` satisfy both, ADV-N25), and **`probe_delivered_while_on`** (the subset of those whose title starts with the visible label `[synthetic-probe]` of the Stage 2 probe, so the Stage 3 evidence shows how many counted deliveries were synthetic; the label only feeds this counter and never changes delivery), **`explicit_off_scopes`** (repeated string: `global`, `kind:review`, ...; empty when no explicit `false` is persisted) and the single number **`soak_streak_hours`** (global, plus a per-kind map), defined as the sum over the buckets since `last_off_flip_at` (the whole window when empty) of `min(gate_on_seconds, uptime_seconds) / 3600`, counting only buckets whose `routine_events_while_on > 0` and **excluding the bucket that contains `last_off_flip_at`** (it carries up to an hour of pre-flip gate-on seconds); and `stats_file_status` (`loaded`, `quarantined`, `writable`). An off flip therefore restarts the streak at the flip instead of zeroing the figure for the whole 72-bucket window, a failure-only hour adds nothing, and a figure computed from an unwritable file is flagged by `writable=false` (Stage 3 requires it true). This settles ADV-N3 and ARCH-C25 **before this PR merges**, because changing a frozen field's meaning later is not additive. The two sections are explicit (`since_process_start` resets on restart, `buckets` do not), so the AC "totals since process start" and "not reset to zero after restart" no longer conflict.
- Stubs fail loudly rather than ignoring input (read-back principle).
  - *Given* `UpdateFeatureFlag` with `scope` or a non-zero `mutation` set while Story 2.11 has not merged, *Then* the stub returns `CodeUnimplemented` and changes no persisted value (it never falls through to the legacy global write), and `GetDeliveryGateStats` returns `CodeUnimplemented` until Story 2.8; *Given* a request with neither field set (an old client), *Then* behavior is exactly today's.
  - *Given* the validation rules that Story 2.11 implements and Contract PR 2 freezes in comments, *Then* the proto documents them: `FLAG_MUTATION_UNSPECIFIED` with a non-empty `scope` and an unknown enum value are `InvalidArgument`; a set with an empty `scope` is `InvalidArgument` and a global set takes the literal `scope = "global"`; `CLEAR_SCOPE` needs a `kind:` scope and `RESET_GLOBAL` forbids one.
**Files**: `proto/session/v1/session.proto`, `server/services/feature_flag_service.go` (stub branch), `server/services/notification_service.go` (stub), `server/services/session_service_delegates.go`, `docs/registry/features/`.

Settled choices the plan text left open: counter `kind`/`class` are strings (closed sets documented in comments, so a new kind is not a wire change); `flag_history` entries are `{scope, value, at, mutation}` - `mutation` is added to the plan's `{scope, value, at}` because a `CLEAR_SCOPE` or `RESET_GLOBAL` otherwise reads as an explicit `false`; `soak.gate_on_hours` is the global figure only (per-kind streaks are `soak_streak_hours_by_kind`); `stats_file_status` is three bools, not a string enum. Stubs: `UpdateFeatureFlag` returns `CodeUnimplemented` before any write when `scope` is non-empty or `mutation` is non-zero (checked ahead of name validation, so an unknown flag with a scope is also `Unimplemented`; Story 2.11 replaces this with the `InvalidArgument` rules above), `GetDeliveryGateStats` returns `CodeUnimplemented`. #### Story 1.7: Contract PR 3 (PR 1e): `ReplyToPendingQuestion` (starts only after Spike 1.3g, the re-review PASS on item 1 and the second review of the Reply design)
**As an** engineer on Story 5.6, **I want** the Reply request/response shape fixed only after the spike decides how the dialog is answered, **so that** the contract does not freeze option buttons versus free text before it is known (triad engineering gap).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- *Given* Spike 1.3g's recorded payload shape, answer keystrokes, "still waiting" predicate and fingerprint rule, *Then* `ReplyToPendingQuestion{session_id, question_id, reply_text, reply_id}` and its response `{outcome, retry_after_seconds}` are added additively. **Signaling channel (decided, ADR-010 decision 1): expected business outcomes are the typed `ReplyOutcome` enum in the response; Connect errors are only `InvalidArgument`, `Internal`, `PermissionDenied` and `Unauthenticated`.** The enum is complete before this PR merges, and its values carry the type prefix that `ENUM_VALUE_PREFIX` (part of the `STANDARD` lint in `buf.yaml`) requires: `REPLY_OUTCOME_UNSPECIFIED = 0`, `REPLY_OUTCOME_SENT`, `REPLY_OUTCOME_SEND_INDETERMINATE`, `REPLY_OUTCOME_NOT_SENT`, `REPLY_OUTCOME_NO_PENDING`, `REPLY_OUTCOME_SUPERSEDED`, `REPLY_OUTCOME_STALE_PROMPT`, `REPLY_OUTCOME_NOT_HIDDEN`, `REPLY_OUTCOME_NOT_WAITING`, `REPLY_OUTCOME_RATE_LIMITED`, `REPLY_OUTCOME_DISABLED` (this plan abbreviates them without the prefix). **`IN_PROGRESS` is not in the enum** (review-repair iteration 2, ARCH-C21): a second caller with the same `reply_id` waits for the first result, a different `reply_id` against a claimed question is `NO_PENDING`, and a busy write lease is `NOT_SENT`, so no path could emit it and no UX state exists for it; every remaining value has a card state in `design/ux.md` Surface 12b. `make proto-lint` and `make proto-breaking` are clean. A field exposing numbered options to the client is added **only if** the spike found answering by option number reliable (otherwise the Reply card is free text only, RP-12), and `question_shape` travels in notification metadata, not in this message. `make proto-breaking` is clean and the stub returns `CodeUnimplemented` until Story 5.6.

#### Story 1.8: `LocalWriteGuard` procedure set and peer-aware verdict (PRs 1f-a and 1f-b; moved out of Epic 5 in review-repair iteration 2, ARCH-C17/C18, ADV-N7/N12; split in review-repair iteration 4, ADV-N26)
**As an** operator, **I want** one guard and one verdict function for the destructive or terminal-writing RPCs on the unauthenticated `:8543` listener, merged before the RPCs that register in it, **so that** DNS rebinding is rejected without locking a legitimate LAN-hostname browser out of the shipped backlog Steer, and neither Reply nor the prune apply ever merges unguarded.
**Gate** (split in review-repair iteration 4, ADV-N26, ARCH-C36b): **PR 1f-a** is Task 1.8e alone: the request-record stamp on both chains, raw values only, with `auth_mode` derived from a real validator. It adds no verdict, no guard set and no refusal, so it needs no re-review PASS and no spike, and it is the **only** part of Story 1.8 that PR 2a-2 waits on (Task 2.8h needs the record's raw fields, not a verdict), which keeps the flood fix R1 off the Host-verdict and verified-hostname work. **PR 1f-b** is Tasks 1.8a-1.8d (the verdict profiles, the guard set, the `:8444` and IP-literal rules, the verified-hostname set): it starts only after the focused re-review PASS on the verdict definition (items 1 and 2), needs PR 1f-a (the verdict reads the record), no spike and no other PR, and merges **before PR 2c and PR 5u**.
**Why this shape (VERIFIED at `013856269`)**: `ProbeGuard` guards exactly one procedure path and its verdict also requires a loopback-bound listener (`server/middleware/probeguard.go:27-51`; `ListenAddrIsLoopback` treats `:8543` and wildcards as not loopback, `:125-131`). The default bind is `localhost:8543` (`main.go:320`, `config/config.go:741`) but `--listen` and `listen_address` allow a wildcard (`main.go:869-870`), so calling the same verdict from `UpdateSession` would make every backlog Steer of a hidden review session `PermissionDenied` on such a deployment and would refuse non-loopback LAN-hostname browsers even with the guards flag off, a regression of shipped behavior (ARCH-C18, ADV-N7). A Connect handler cannot read `Host` from `connect.Request`; the codebase solves that already: `services.WithRequestHost` stamps `r.Host` into the context for the `SessionService` handler (`server/server.go:430`, `server/services/capture_tap_service.go:33-38`), and `requireLoopbackCaller(peer, host, header)` (`:124-155`) is an in-handler loopback check with proxy-header detection (`proxyHeaders`, `isProxyHeader`, `:167-180`). Reply, `UpdateSession` and `PruneHiddenSessionNotifications` are all `SessionService` RPCs, so one stamp covers them (it must also wrap any other service whose RPC later joins the set).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- The guard is a **set** and `ProbeProgram` is unchanged.
  - *Given* the existing `server/server_probeguard_test.go` and `server/middleware/probeguard_test.go`, *Then* they pass unmodified for `ProbeProgram` (profile `probe`: POST only, loopback-bound listener, Host and Origin loopback or allowed, exactly as `probeGuardVerdict` today); *Given* the set, *Then* it contains only `ProbeProgram` at the end of this story (`PruneHiddenSessionNotifications` is added by Task 2.7c and `ReplyToPendingQuestion` by Task 5.6l), and a test asserts `ClearNotificationHistory` is **not** a member.
- The **rebinding gate** (profile for the steer, Prune and Reply) has no `LoopbackBound` condition and keeps a legitimate LAN-hostname browser working.
  - *Given* Host in {the loopback names (`localhost` and loopback IP literals), the **verified hostnames** (`Server.GetVerifiedHostnames()`, below), an **IP literal that is the listener's own address or any local interface address**, the host of a non-wildcard `GetAddr()`} and an Origin that is absent, loopback, in `GetOrigins()` or same-origin with that already-allowed Host, *Then* the verdict allows the request, including when the listener is bound to `0.0.0.0:8543`; *Given* Host `evil.example:8543` resolving to 127.0.0.1, a foreign Origin, or an IP literal that is not a local address, *Then* it is rejected (403 at the procedure guard, `PermissionDenied` in a handler) on every bind.
- The allowlist is the **verified** hostname set, recomputed from `validateFn` on every detector cycle and never copied from `d.networks` (review-repair iteration 3, ARCH-NB4; iteration 4, ARCH-NB5 and ADV-m12).
  - *Given* the VERIFIED facts at `013856269`: `HostnameDetector.redetect` publishes to `Server.hostnames` "every hostname discovered this cycle, verified or not" and documents that an unverified one "stays in `Server.GetHostnames()` forever" (`hostname_detector.go:240-249`; `resolveAndValidate` keeps it out of `d.networks` but returns it in `unverified`, `:284-299`), `main.go:459` publishes the raw boot-time candidate list, and candidate names come from reverse DNS of the host's own addresses, which the network controls, *Then* a hostile network that answers a PTR record for the server's IP with `attacker.example` would put that name on `GetHostnames()`, and DNS rebinding from it would pass a Host check built on it (`ProbeGuard` has the same weakness for `ProbeProgram` today, `server/server.go:1726`, `AllowedHosts: s.GetHostnames`; that rule is kept byte-for-byte and the weakness is recorded, not widened).
  - *Given* that `d.networks` is **not** a verified set (VERIFIED, ARCH-NB5): `startRemoteAccess` seeds `networks[ip] = append([]string{ip}, resolveLANHostnames(ctx, ip)...)` (`main.go:1526`), the raw boot-time candidates (reverse DNS/PTR, avahi, `hostname -f`, search-domain guesses) **plus the IP literal**; only the separate `hostnames` slice is filtered by `verifyHostnameOwnership` (`main.go:1556-1564`); the unfiltered `networks` map is returned as `remoteAccessResult.Networks` (`main.go:1653`) and handed to the detector as `InitialNetworks` (`main.go:1676,1702`); `NewHostnameDetector` copies it into `d.networks` (`hostname_detector.go:147-151`); and `resolveAndValidate` skips every name already in `d.networks[ip]` (`:287-289`), so the seeded names are **never validated**. `flattenHostnames()` (`:385-391`) therefore returns unverified PTR names and IP literals, and an earlier revision of this story that fed the verified set from it left the hostile-PTR hole open. *Then* the verified set is `Server.ReplaceVerifiedHostnames(names)` / `GetVerifiedHostnames()` (an `atomic.Pointer` to an immutable sorted slice beside `SetHostnames`, `server/server.go:1577-1599`), written **only by the detector** with the output of one computation per cycle (run after `resolveAndValidate`): candidates = every name in `d.networks` that is not an IP literal and not `localhost`, plus the boot-time `verifiedHostnames` (`main.go:1556-1564`, carried in a new `remoteAccessResult.VerifiedHostnames` and passed as `HostnameDetectorConfig.InitialVerified`); the verified set = the candidates for which `d.validateFn(cycleCtx, name)` returns true **in this cycle**. A seeded name is validated, not trusted. The IP-literal exclusion is explicit (`net.ParseIP`) because the real `verifyHostnameOwnership` returns true for a literal that is a local address (`LookupHost` of a literal returns the literal, `main.go:1342-1358`). The set is never fed from `flattenHostnames()`, `d.networks` as such or `remoteAccess.Networks`.
  - *Given* a hostname that `resolveAndValidate` returned in `unverified`, or a name seeded through `InitialNetworks` that `validateFn` rejects, or an IP literal, or `localhost`, *Then* the first two are in `GetHostnames()` (the unverified one) but **none** is in `GetVerifiedHostnames()`, and the verdict rejects a request with that Host (T-RP-65 drives the real detector with a stub resolver and a stub `validateFn`; T-RP-70 seeds `InitialNetworks` with an unverified name **and** an IP literal and asserts both are absent, with a `validateFn` that accepts everything to prove the literal is excluded by rule and not by luck; the test file is the root-package `hostname_detector_test.go`, not `server/`). Stated residual: `verifyHostnameOwnership` proves that the name resolves to a non-loopback address of this machine, so an attacker who controls the network's DNS for both the PTR and the forward record can still get a name verified (the trust model of the certificate and RPID gate, outside this rail); and the detector starts whether or not remote access is configured (VERIFIED `main.go:500-515`: `startHostnameDetector` passes an empty `InitialNetworks` when `remoteAccess == nil`), so with remote access unconfigured only the boot feeder is empty and the **recompute still admits names the detector discovers and `validateFn` accepts**; a LAN-hostname browser is refused only until such a name verifies, with the explicit message (Task 1.8a records whether the live verified set is populated; ARCH-C44b corrected the earlier sentence that said only loopback, local literals and the listen host pass).
  - *Given* the set is not add-only (m12), *Then* **the choice is re-verification on every detector cycle, not an expiry**: the default cycle is 5 minutes (`defaultHostnameRedetectInterval`, `hostname_detector.go:18`), a name whose DNS record changed drops out at the next cycle and a name that returns is re-admitted at the next, the cost is one forward lookup per candidate per cycle inside the cycle's existing 30s bound, and a name not yet checked when the cycle context expires keeps its previous state for that cycle, **and so does a name whose lookup was in flight when `cycleCtx` expired: a false result with `cycleCtx.Err() != nil` is a timeout, not a failed verification** (`verifyHostnameOwnership` returns false on any `LookupHost` error, and `forwardLookupViaKnownNameservers` can spend up to 3s per nameserver per name, `main.go:1178-1203`, so N candidates can exhaust the 30s cycle; ARCH-C43); a name previously in the set is dropped only after **two consecutive false results with the context alive**, so one transient failure does not flap the set (T-RP-71). With `STAPLER_SQUAD_HOSTNAME_REDETECT_DISABLE=true` no loop runs, so `main` seeds the set once at boot from `verifiedHostnames` and it stays static (stated limit: the operator turned re-verification off; T-RP-72).
- IP-literal Hosts (ADV-N14).
  - *Given* a Host that is an IP literal, *Then* it is allowed only when it equals the host of the request's listener address or any address of a local interface (`net.InterfaceAddrs`, cached briefly): DNS rebinding needs a name the attacker controls, so an IP literal cannot be a rebinding Host and rejecting a local one protects nothing, while an IP literal the machine does not own is not a legitimate browser address and stays rejected. `GetVerifiedHostnames()` never contains IP literals.
- The authenticated `:8444` chain is decided here, not left implicit (ADV-N14).
  - *Given* `auth_mode == required` (the `:8444` chain, and `:8543` when an `authMiddleware` is set), *Then* the verdict **skips the Host-membership test** and keeps the same-origin Origin check: the auth cookie is the boundary (a rebinding page cannot present the legitimate origin's cookie; INFERRED from cookie scoping), and `remoteChain`'s own comment says its Host is "a LAN/Tailscale name the guard would reject" (`server/server.go:1710-1711`), so applying the Host test there would take the shipped backlog Steer, and later Prune and Reply, away from a remote operator on `onyx.staplerhome.internal` or a Tailscale name. Tests (T-RO-34, T-RP-56): `:8444` with a cookie and a Host that is in no set is allowed; an IP-literal Host on `:8444` is allowed; the same without a cookie is refused by auth (401), not by the verdict.
- The new guard set is not applied when auth middleware is set (minor m9).
  - *Given* `localChain` is `auth | ProbeGuard` (`server/server.go:1696-1708`: `ProbeGuard` is installed only in the `else` branch, when `authMiddleware == nil`), *Then* the procedure-level guard set, like `ProbeGuard` today, is not installed when an `authMiddleware` is set, the in-handler verdict follows `auth_mode`, and a test covers the auth-set branch.
- Prune's dry run needs an allowed Host too (minor m10).
  - *Given* `PruneHiddenSessionNotifications` is registered as a whole procedure (Task 2.7c), *Then* a dry run from a Host outside the allowed set gets the same 403 as an apply, and the Prune how-to says so (the guard is not per-field).
- The **local-caller gate** (Reply only) refuses a remote caller when auth is off.
  - *Given* `auth_mode == none` and a peer that is not loopback, or any proxy header (reusing `isProxyHeader`, so a local reverse proxy or Tailscale Serve whose peer is loopback is treated as remote), *Then* `PermissionDenied` with outcome `refused`; *Given* `auth_mode == required` (the `:8444` chain), *Then* the gate does not apply. The steer and Prune use the rebinding gate only: the steer is shipped behavior preserved by O7, and Prune has the exposure `ClearNotificationHistory` has today; both record `peer_loopback` and `proxied` in their audit line instead of refusing on them.
- One implementation, no copy.
  - *Given* `isProxyHeader`, `proxyHeaders` and the loopback checks, *Then* they move to `server/middleware` (exported `HasProxyHeader`, `PeerIsLoopback`) and `capture_tap_service.go` imports them (`server/services` already imports `middleware`), the capture-tap tests stay green, and `make ready-complexity-gate` (`dupl`) passes.

VERIFIED why it cannot be derived from the wrapper: `middleware.Auth(nil)` returns `next` unchanged (`server/middleware/auth.go:16-20`), `SetupAuth` accepts any function (`server/server.go:1320`; `server_probeguard_test.go:71` installs an identity wrapper) and `remoteChain` skips the wrapper when it is nil (`:1712-1717`), so a chain with no real validator would otherwise read `required`, skip the Host test and be an open listener without the rebinding gate. *Given* an identity wrapper, a nil wrapper or `Auth(nil)`, *Then* `auth_mode` is `none` and the Host test applies (negative control, T-RP-80); *Given* the real `Auth(sessions)` with a non-nil validator, *Then* `required` (T-RP-80, T-RP-81).

  - *Given* the live deployment's config read once and read-only, *Then* its `listen_address` (or the absence of one, meaning `localhost:8543`) is written into this plan next to the live `GetVerifiedHostnames()` set and whether the operator's browser Host is in it and the verdict regression tests include both that bind and a wildcard bind (UNVERIFIED until Task 1.8a runs).

## Phase 2: Backend delivery gate

### Epic 2a: Gate core (PRs 2a-1, 2a-2 and 2e, `feat(notifications):`; flag default off; legacy checks untouched)
**Goal**: One policy function and one I/O-free filter installed at bus construction, proven (matrix + type-based sink guard) to cover every channel, shipped in shadow mode so that merging changes no hidden-session suppression; the flag-independent changes are listed under Stage 0. PR 2a-1 is the first shippable slice (Stories 2.1-2.6, global flag only); PR 2a-2 adds Stories 2.8 and 2.10; PR 2e adds Story 2.11.

#### Story 2.1: Pure delivery policy and flag cache
**As an** operator, **I want** one function that classifies and decides, **so that** hidden-session policy has exactly one definition and no I/O.
**Acceptance Criteria** (tests first in `server/deliverygate/policy_test.go`):
- Hidden + routine type is suppressed.
  - *Given* `VisibilityHidden` and `Facts{Type: TASK_COMPLETE}`, *When* `ShouldDeliver` runs, *Then* `Decision{Outcome: OutcomeSuppress, Class: ClassRoutine, Reason: ReasonRoutineForHidden}`.
- Hidden + failure or needs-human delivers; priority is not an input.
  - *Given* `VisibilityHidden`, `Facts{Type: ERROR}` then `Facts{Type: APPROVAL_NEEDED}`, *Then* `Class: ClassFailure` and `Class: ClassNeedsHuman`, both `OutcomeDeliver`.
- Hints.
  - *Given* hidden `Facts{Type: WARNING}` no hint, *Then* `OutcomeSuppress`; with `HintFailure`, *Then* `OutcomeDeliver, ClassFailure`. *Given* hidden `Facts{Type: ERROR, Hint: HintRoutine}` (per-tool hook error), *Then* `OutcomeSuppress`. *Given* visible session with `HintRoutine`, *Then* `OutcomeDeliver` (hints affect hidden sessions only).
- A hidden session's crash and permanent failure are failure-class through plain types (no status concept in the policy).
  - *Given* hidden `Facts{Type: FAILURE}` (the Story 2.10 crash notification) and `Facts{Type: ERROR}` (the existing PermanentlyFailed notification), *Then* both `OutcomeDeliver, ClassFailure`.
- Untrusted legacy type delivers for hidden sessions (skew-safe rule, Story 2.5).
  - *Given* hidden `Facts{Type: TASK_COMPLETE, Hint: HintUntrustedType}`, *Then* `OutcomeDeliver, Class: ClassRoutine, Reason: ReasonUntrustedLegacyType` (the class stays type-derived so `hidden_delivered{class=routine}` exposes the skew); *Given* a visible session with the same hint, *Then* `OutcomeDeliver` with the ordinary reason.
- Non-hidden always delivers; unresolved fails open.
  - *Given* `VisibilityVisible` and `AUTO_APPROVED`, *Then* `OutcomeDeliver`; `VisibilityUnresolved` and `TASK_COMPLETE`, *Then* `OutcomeDeliver, Reason: ReasonUnresolvedFailOpen`; `VisibilityNotASession`, *Then* `OutcomeDeliver`.
- Exhaustiveness: the test ranges over `sessionv1.NotificationType_name` and fails if any value lacks an explicit class.
  - *Given* a hypothetical new enum value, *When* the test runs, *Then* it fails naming the value.
- Gate flag off yields shadow outcomes at the `Gate` level (policy stays pure).
  - *Given* `Gate` with `FlagCache` false, hidden TASK_COMPLETE, *Then* `OutcomeShadowSuppress`, delivery proceeds and `would_suppress` increments.
- `FlagCache` does no I/O on read.
  - *Given* a flag loader that counts reads, *When* 10,000 `Enabled()` calls run, *Then* the loader was called 0 times after construction; *When* `UpdateFeatureFlag` flips the flag, *Then* `Enabled()` reflects it immediately; *When* the config file is edited externally, *Then* the 5s ticker (fake clock) picks it up.
- One reload path (review-repair iteration 1, ARCH-C7 / ADV C12).
  - *Given* an injected barrier that parks the 5s ticker's `Reload()` after its file read and before its swap, *When* `UpdateFeatureFlag` saves a new value and calls `Reload()`, *Then* the final snapshot reflects the save and a later ticker swap cannot publish the older value (the load happens inside `reloadMu`); *Given* a reload that fails to read the file, *Then* the previous snapshot is kept (a read error never flips the gate); *Given* `UpdateFeatureFlag` and the ticker run concurrently under `-race` with a fake clock, *Then* no data race and the snapshot equals the persisted file. `FeatureFlagService` reaches the cache through a one-method `FlagObserver.OnFlagChanged(name)`; `deliverygate` does not import `services`.
**Files**: `server/deliverygate/policy.go`, `policy_test.go`, `gate.go`, `flagcache.go`, `flagcache_test.go`, `metrics.go`, `pkg/events/notification_metadata.go`, `config/config.go`, `server/services/feature_flag_service.go`.

- Cover every criterion above plus the enum exhaustiveness test.
- Files: `server/deliverygate/policy_test.go`.

- Also (triad repair 1): the `FlagCache` reload ticker takes an injected clock and exposes a `Stop()` that joins its goroutine (BUG-089); the rate-limited logger takes an injected `*slog.Logger`, never the global default (BUG-087). Tests assert the join (T-FT-02) and use no `slog.SetDefault` (T-FT-01).
- `FlagCache` per the AC (the scope-aware snapshot is added by Story 2.11; here it holds the global value), with one `Reload()` guarded by `reloadMu` as the only swapper. The 5s ticker goroutine stops when the server context is cancelled (goleak-clean) AND is **joined** by the server shutdown path, because this package's shutdown-join test is already flaky (`docs/bugs/open/BUG-089-server-shutdown-joins-background-tickers-flake.md`: `TestServer_Shutdown_JoinsBackgroundTickers`); the new ticker must not add a second source of that flake. Log-assertion tests (the rate-limited `delivery_suppressed` lines, GL-1..GL-5) inject a `slog.Handler` through the constructor and never use the global-swapping `captureLogs` under `t.Parallel` (`docs/bugs/open/BUG-087-captureLogs-global-slog-swap-races-under-t-parallel.md`). Counters per the Observability Plan; log limiter keyed `(session,type,reason)`, 60s window, LRU-bounded at 4096 keys, injected clock.
- Files: `server/deliverygate/flagcache.go`, `metrics.go`, `gate.go`, `gate_test.go`.

- A `go/packages` scan (behind the `sinkguard` tag, run by `make test-delivery-guards`) fails if `os.Getenv`/`os.LookupEnv` appears in the gate, flag-cache or flag-service code paths that read `hidden_session_gate`, `hidden_session_readonly_guards` or `notification_tray_v2`; rollout flags are live-settable only (`feedback_rollout_flags_live_settable_no_env_vars`).
- Files: `server/deliverygate/flag_env_guard_test.go`.

#### Story 2.2: Visibility index and resolver (no I/O on the publish path)
**As an** engineer, **I want** hidden-ness answered from memory and primed by session lifecycle, **so that** a new hidden session's first hook is never leaked and no `Publish` caller blocks.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Index hit by any identity form.
  - *Given* hidden session `{uuid:"u1", title:"review:abc", tmux:"ssq_review_abc"}` upserted, *When* `Resolve` is called with each of the three strings, *Then* `VisibilityHidden` every time (hook IDs are titles, so title and tmux name are first-class keys).
- Created-before-hook ordering.
  - *Given* `SessionService` creating a hidden session, *When* the create path returns and a hook event with `SessionID=<title>` is published immediately, *Then* the resolver returns `Hidden` (the index was upserted synchronously in the create path, before the tmux pane starts). *Given* a test that publishes the hook before the upsert, *Then* the result is `Unresolved` (delivered, counted) and is **not cached**.
- Only positive results are cached.
  - *Given* an `Unresolved` lookup for `"x1"` followed by an upsert of `x1` as hidden, *When* `Resolve("x1")` runs, *Then* `Hidden` immediately (no negative TTL).
- Miss does no synchronous I/O and refreshes once.
  - *Given* a storage fake whose `ListInstanceData` blocks forever and 200 concurrent misses for 50 distinct IDs, *Then* all 200 `Resolve` calls return `Unresolved` within 1ms of each other (no blocking), exactly 1 background refresh is in flight (singleflight), and it is abandoned after its timeout.
- `NotASession` is defined positively.
  - *Given* `""`, metadata `item_id=item-77`, and `"fork-pressure"` (member of the closed set from Spike 1.3d), *Then* `NotASession`; *Given* `"deadbeef-0000"` found nowhere, *Then* `Unresolved` (a real session title is indistinguishable from any other string, so only the closed set and `item_id` count).
- Deleted hidden sessions are remembered.
  - *Given* hidden `h1` deleted, *When* a routine event arrives for `h1` within the tombstone TTL (24h, fake clock), *Then* `VisibilityHidden`; after TTL, `Unresolved`. Tombstones are LRU-bounded (10,000).
- Seeding and readiness.
  - *Given* a fresh `Gate`, *Then* `Seeded()==false`, the filter fails open and `notification_gate_index_miss_total` counts; *When* `SeedFromInstances(instances)` runs on an already-loaded `[]*session.Instance` (reading each via `Snapshot()`), *Then* `Seeded()==true` with zero storage or poller calls (a fake that fails the test on any call), and a seeded hidden session resolves `Hidden` by UUID, title and tmux name. The async `ListInstanceData` reconciler may later add entries but is not required for readiness.
- Reads are lock-free; writers cannot invert (architecture C1).
  - *Given* 8 goroutines calling `Resolve` while one calls `Upsert` and another calls `Upsert` while holding a fake `Instance.mu`, under `go test -race` with a 5s deadlock watchdog, *Then* no race, no deadlock, and `Resolve` acquires no mutex (asserted by the `atomic.Pointer` implementation plus a benchmark showing no contention; the copy-and-swap cost for 1,000 sessions is recorded, INFERRED to be microseconds).
- Refresh is rate-limited (architecture C2).
  - *Given* a counting storage fake and 500 misses over 100 distinct unknown IDs across 20s of fake time, *Then* `ListInstanceData` is called at most once per 5s window (at most 4 calls), including when the previous attempt failed, timed out or returned nothing; a miss inside the back-off window returns `Unresolved` immediately and increments `refresh{result=skipped_min_interval}`.
- Rename and tag changes keep the index current.
  - *Given* hidden `h1` renamed to `h1b`, *When* the rename feed point runs, *Then* `Resolve("h1b")` is `Hidden` and `Resolve("h1")` stays `Hidden` for the alias TTL (running hooks carry the title they started with; INFERRED, confirm in Spike 1.3e); *Given* a tag change from `backlog:review` to none, *Then* the entry's `Kind` becomes `other`.
- Title reuse cannot make a visible session hidden (pre-mortem #8; the failure direction would be a lost routine push of a visible session).
  - *Given* hidden `h1` deleted (tombstone) or renamed (alias) and then a **visible** session created with the same title, *When* `Upsert` of the visible entry runs, *Then* the tombstone/alias with that key is evicted and `Resolve(title)` is `Visible`. (`Hidden` is only set at creation, VERIFIED by the pre-mortem: no `SetHidden` writer outside the ent repository, so un-hide staleness cannot occur; title reuse is the remaining path.)
- `HiddenKind` is derived from tags.
  - *Given* hidden sessions tagged `backlog:review`, `backlog:triage`, `backlog:diagnose` and an untagged hidden session, *Then* `Kind` is `review`, `triage`, `diagnose`, `other`.
- `item_id` does not hide a session (PermanentlyFailed shape).
  - *Given* an event whose `SessionID` is a hidden session's UUID and whose metadata has `item_id=<the same UUID>` (what `Notify(inst.UUID, ...)` produces), *Then* `Hidden`, not `NotASession`; *Given* `item_id=item-77` with an unknown `SessionID`, *Then* `NotASession`.
- Capacity-monitor-style events still resolve.
  - *Given* hidden `h1` and an event with `NotificationMetadata=nil`, *Then* `Hidden` (resolution never depends on `session_scoped`).
**Files**: `server/deliverygate/index.go`, `index_test.go`, `resolver.go`, `resolver_test.go`, `server/services/session_service_diagnose.go` (`CreateDirectorySession`, the primary feed), `server/services/session_service_crud.go`, `server/services/session_service_update.go` (rename and tag change) and the other sites named by Spike 1.3e, `server/dependencies.go`.

##### Task 2.2a: Failing index/resolver tests with fakes
- Files: `server/deliverygate/index_test.go`, `resolver_test.go`.

##### Task 2.2b: Implement the copy-on-write `VisibilityIndex` and `SessionVisibilityResolver`
- Decision (architecture C1): `atomic.Pointer` to an immutable struct (maps for UUID, title, tmux name, aliases and tombstones); writers take a private writer-only mutex that guards copy-and-swap and calls nothing, so reads are lock-free and `Upsert` under `Instance.mu` is safe. A batch `Replace(entries)` is used for the seed so seeding is O(n), not O(n^2). Rejected: keep the `RWMutex` and reword the ADR (a reader still takes a lock that a writer holding `inst.mu` contends on). Add the 1,000-session copy-cost benchmark.
- Singleflight refresh with a bounded timeout and a 5s minimum interval between attempts (success or failure). Consumer-defined narrow interface `InstanceDataLister` (refresh only). Package doc states the lock-order rule: the writer mutex is never held while calling out.
- Files: `server/deliverygate/index.go`, `resolver.go`.

##### Task 2.2c: Feed the index: `CreateDirectorySession` first, then the other lifecycle sites
- Primary feed (architecture C8): `SessionService.CreateDirectorySession` (`session_service_diagnose.go:122`) upserts synchronously after the `Instance` exists and before it is started, because every `Hidden: true` producer found so far (diagnose, triage, `SpawnReviewSession`) goes through it; Spike 1.3e must confirm there is no bypass, and the answer gates the "hidden before first hook" guarantee. Then the interactive create path, hide/tag toggles in `UpdateSession`, rename (add the new key, keep the old as an alias), and tombstone on delete/archive. The restore case is the synchronous seed (Task 2.3c-b), with the async `ListInstanceData` reconciler only as a backstop.
- Files: the sites from Spike 1.3e.

##### Task 2.2d: Closed system-ID set
- Constant set from Spike 1.3d with a test that every `NewNotificationEvent` producer ID prefix is either in the set, an `item_id` stamp, or a session identity (a scan test, so a new producer fails loudly).
- Files: `server/deliverygate/system_ids.go`, `system_ids_test.go`.

##### Task 2.2e: Characterize `SendNotification` resolution; leave it untouched in 2a (adversarial N4)
- 2a does NOT swap the poller/storage lookup at L96-129: it supplies `sessionName` and `resolvedSessionID`, which View Session depends on, and the hidden check there is a legacy check that stays until 2b. Add a flag-off characterization test with an **unseeded** index: for a hidden and a visible session the response and the published event (name, UUID, metadata) are byte-identical to `main`. In 2b (Task 2.9c) only the hidden branch and the LOW check are deleted; name/UUID resolution stays and may read the index first with a fallback to the existing lookup on `Unresolved`.
- Files: `server/services/notification_service_test.go`.

#### Story 2.3: EventBus publish filter installed at bus construction
**As an** operator, **I want** a suppressed notification to never get a sequence number and no producer to publish before the filter exists, **so that** replay cannot resurrect it and startup ordering cannot leak.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- A rejected event is dropped before `Seq` assignment.
  - *Given* a bus with a filter returning false for `EventNotification` where `NotificationType==4`, *When* one rejected and one accepted event are published, *Then* the accepted event has `Seq==1`, the subscriber receives one event, and `EventsSince` replay contains only it (`pkg/events/bus.go:72-94` assigns `Seq` first today; the filter goes before it).
- Filter is race-safe.
  - *Given* 8 goroutines publishing while `SetPublishFilter` is called 100 times, under `go test -race`, *Then* no race or panic.
- Installed before any producer, and the Gate owns its index from construction (architecture C3).
  - *Given* the two constructors (`session_service.go:1008`, `:1022`), *When* a notification is published immediately after construction and before seeding, *Then* it is delivered (fail open), `notification_gate_index_miss_total` increments and exactly one WARN is logged; after `SeedFromInstances`, hidden routine events are filtered. There is no `Bind` step to forget.
- Restore-time producers are covered (architecture C3).
  - *Given* a hidden instance persisted in storage, gate on, *When* `BuildRuntimeDeps` runs (`server/dependencies.go:652` loads, `:876` wires, `:930` starts) and `onColdRestoreLostHistory` fires for it, *Then* history gets 0 rows for it and the gate saw `Hidden` (the seed ran synchronously right after `LoadInstances`, before any instance was wired or started). The same holds for `onRateLimitDetected` on restore.
- No lock inversion (new design constraint).
  - *Given* a test that holds `ReviewQueuePoller.mu.Lock`, a `Instance.mu.Lock` and the poller's `pmMu` in separate goroutines while 8 other goroutines `Publish` hidden and visible notifications, under `-race` with a 5s deadlock watchdog, *Then* all publishes complete (the filter takes no lock: atomic loads only), including when `Upsert` is called while holding `Instance.mu`.
- Non-notification events never reach gate logic.
  - *Given* an `EventSessionUpdated`, *Then* the filter returns true without touching the index.
- A panic inside the filter cannot break a producer (pre-mortem #12; the filter runs on the caller goroutine of 108 `Publish` sites).
  - *Given* a filter whose index lookup panics, *When* `Publish` is called, *Then* the panic is recovered inside the filter wrapper, the event is delivered (fail open), `notification_gate_filter_panic_total` increments and exactly one rate-limited ERROR is logged; the producer goroutine never sees the panic.
- Hidden TASK_COMPLETE rejected with flag on; hidden ERROR accepted and logged `hidden_delivery_allowed`; flag off delivers both and counts `would_suppress` for the first.
**Files**: `pkg/events/bus.go`, `bus_test.go`, `server/deliverygate/gate.go`, `gate_test.go`, `server/services/session_service.go`, `server/server.go`.

#### Story 2.4: Push status-change and auto-approved history through single-method gates
**As an** operator, **I want** the hidden "Session Completed" push and hidden auto-approval rows closed, **so that** the screenshot bug is fixed on the paths the bus filter cannot reach.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Hidden Stopped push is suppressed (gate on); visible still pushes.
  - *Given* a hidden `Instance` and an `EventSessionUpdated` with `UpdatedFields=[status]`, status Stopped, gate on, *When* `buildStatusChangeNotification` runs through `StartDeliverySubscriber`, *Then* the recording `Notifier` receives 0 deliveries; visible: 1; gate off: 1 (shadow counted).
- Hidden auto-approvals: allow rows dropped, deny rows kept (audit trail).
  - *Given* hidden session `h1`, gate on, *When* `approval_handler` resolves an auto-**allow** (`approval_handler.go:477`), *Then* `AppendAutoApproved` is not called (0 rows); *When* it resolves an auto-**deny** (`:488`), *Then* `AppendAutoApproved(..., "deny")` is still called (1 row), because it is the only record that a rule blocked a tool in a background session. Visible session: both append. Gate off: both append.
  - *Given* the analytics path `h.analyticsStore.RecordFromResult`, *Then* it is unchanged for hidden sessions in all cases.
- Consumer-side interfaces.
  - *Given* `push.SessionDeliveryGate` (`AllowStatusChange(sessionID string) bool`) and `services.AutoApprovedGate` (`AllowAutoApprovedRow(sessionID, decision string) bool`, declared beside the handler in `server/services/approval_handler.go`, `package services`; there is no `approval` package) each have exactly one method, *Then* each test fake is a single func literal and `server/push` does not import `server/deliverygate` (an import-graph test; the `services` package constructs the Gate, so the criterion is scoped to `push`).
**Files**: `server/push/subscriber.go`, `subscriber_test.go`, `server/services/approval_handler.go`, `approval_handler_test.go`, `server/server.go`.

#### Story 2.5: Fix `ssq-notify` type enum and stamp hook classes (ADR-003; first commit of the PR)
**As an** operator, **I want** hook events to carry real proto type numbers and honest classes, **so that** a type-keyed gate classifies them correctly and per-tool errors do not count as failures.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Parity test fails on today's script and passes after the fix.
  - *Given* the script, *When* a Go test runs `ssq-notify` in a dry-run mode that prints the numeric type (preferred over parsing the bash `case` table) for each name, *Then* `info->10, approval_needed->1, question->2, error->7, warning->8, task_complete->4, task_failed->9, custom->100` (fails before the fix with `info->1`).
- Hook-handler stamps.
  - *Given* `scripts/ssq-hook-handler` `post-tool` (L528, `--type error -p high`) and subagent `task_failed` (L472, L475), *When* they run, *Then* the request carries `delivery_class=routine`; main Stop `task_failed` (L455, L458) carries no routine stamp. A Go/bash test asserts the stamps from the script's recorded `curl` payload.
  - *Given* a hidden session and a post-tool error hook after the gate is on, *Then* history gets 0 rows; a main Stop `task_failed` gets 1 FAILURE row.
- Version skew is detectable AND safe (adversarial N4).
  - *Given* the new script sends metadata `ssq_notify_schema=2`, *When* `SendNotification` receives a request without it, *Then* `notification_rpc_unversioned_total` increments and a WARN `legacy_ssq_notify_detected` is logged at most once per hour. The Stage 2 checklist requires this counter at zero and the installed script's hash to match the repo (ADR-003).
  - *Given* gate on, a **hidden** session and an unversioned request carrying `notificationType=6` (an old script's `task_failed`, which the proto reads as PROCESS_FINISHED, routine), *Then* the server stamps `HintUntrustedType`, the event is delivered (fail open, `ReasonUntrustedLegacyType`), `hidden_delivered{class=routine}` increments and a WARN is logged; *Given* a versioned request of the same number, *Then* it is suppressed as routine; *Given* a visible session, *Then* behavior is unchanged. The failure direction is over-delivery, never a lost failure, and the rule disappears from the data once the counter is zero.
- A visible session's idle hook is stored as INFO and routed through `HISTORY_ONLY_TYPES` (no toast).
**Files**: `scripts/ssq-notify`, `scripts/ssq-hook-handler`, `server/services/ssq_notify_enum_parity_test.go`, `server/services/notification_service.go`, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useSessionNotifications.ts` (test only).

#### Story 2.6: Prove no channel bypasses the gate (type-based)
**As an** operator, **I want** a test that fails when someone adds a new delivery path, **so that** this bug class cannot regrow a fourth time.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Matrix test with real subscribers.
  - *Given* hidden session `S`, real `notifications.StartSubscriberWithInterval` (fake appender), real `push.StartDeliverySubscriber` (recording `Notifier`), a `WatchSessions`-style subscriber, gate on, *When* one event per `NotificationType` plus a Stopped `session.updated` plus a crash (the Story 2.10 FAILURE producer) is published, *Then* routine rows produce 0 records in all three sinks and each failure/needs-human type (and the crash notification) produces exactly 1 in each that handles it.
  - *Given* the same hidden session and the remaining two `DeliveryChannel` values (validation T-MX-02, T-PS-08), *Then* the matrix also covers the **Slack** sink (a real `ReactiveQueueManager.OnItemAdded` with a Slack fake: a hidden routine reason reaches Slack 0 times, a hidden `TestsFailing` reaches it once as today) and the **auto-approved** sink (`AppendAutoApproved`: hidden allow 0 rows, hidden deny 1 row), so all four label values (`bus`, `push_status`, `auto_approved`, `slack`) are exercised, not three.
- Sink-enumeration guard matches types, not names, and does not slow `make test` (adversarial M5).
  - *Given* the guard lives behind the build tag `sinkguard` and runs through a dedicated `make test-delivery-guards` target that `make ci` and `make ready` depend on (so CI enforces it and the inner `make test` loop does not pay for `go/packages` type-checking), *When* the non-test Go packages are type-checked with `go/packages`, *When* the guard test collects (a) every concrete type implementing `server/push.Notifier` (or the interface the push service uses), (b) every function whose parameter or receiver subscribes to the bus and switches on `events.EventNotification` (via `go/types` on `Subscribe` call sites in packages that reference `events.EventNotification`), and (c) every call to a method on `*notifications.NotificationHistoryStore` named `Append*`, *Then* each is in a reviewed allowlist with a reason; adding a scratch `Notifier` implementation or a new `EventNotification` consumer with a different name makes it fail. **The negative control is automated, not "verified once by hand"** (validation T-MX-06): a fixture package under `server/deliverygate/testdata/` adds an unlisted `Notifier`, a differently named `EventNotification` consumer and an unlisted `Append*` caller, and the test asserts the scan fails on each, so the guard cannot silently rot. Name-based greps are not used.
- Stragglers are asserted, not just documented (validation T-MX-11).
  - *Given* a gate-on run with (a) a hidden session deleted before the "restart" (tombstone absent), (b) a hidden session created by a path the index feed does not cover, (c) a hidden session renamed before the feed point runs, *When* a routine event arrives for each, *Then* each is delivered (fail open) and increments `notification_delivery_unresolved_total{class=routine}` by exactly 1, and `notification_unresolved_resolved_later_total` stays 0 for (a) and moves only for (b) and (c) after the refresh, which is the defect signal.
- No raw `.Hidden` reads in notification code outside the allowlist.
  - *Given* `server/` non-test files, *When* the guard scans selectors of type `session.Instance` field `Hidden`, *Then* matches only in the allowlist (which shrinks to empty after Story 2.9).
- e2e: hidden session completion leaves UI empty.
  - *Given* the isolated e2e server (confirm `tests/e2e/helpers/session-client.ts` can create `Hidden: true` sessions in Spike 1.3; if not, add it here), gate on, *When* the session finishes, *Then* the Notifications page and the panel show 0 rows for it and no toast (`toHaveCount(0)`).
**Files**: `server/deliverygate/matrix_test.go`, `sink_guard_test.go`, `straggler_test.go`, `testdata/`, `server/services/slack_gate_test.go`, `tests/e2e/hidden-session-quiet.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/pages/NotificationPanel.ts`, `tests/e2e/helpers/session-client.ts`.
