#### PR 2a-1 implementation record (2026-10-09; branch `notification-tray-hidden-session-gate`)
Stories 2.1-2.6 are implemented with the flag default off and every legacy check intact. What the code does, where it deviates from the text above, and what is still open:

- **Landed**: `server/deliverygate` (policy, `FlagCache`, copy-on-write index, resolver with singleflight refresh, `Gate` with the bus filter, the two consumer-side gates, in-process counters, rate-limited logger); `EventBus.SetPublishFilter`; the gate built with the bus in `newGatedSessionService` and seeded in `BuildRuntimeDeps` (so no `ServiceDeps.Gate` field exists, as Spike 1.3f suggested); feed points in `CreateDirectorySession`/`CreateWorktreeSession` (before `Start`), `CreateSession`, fork, rename, tag change and `removeFromAllPollers`; `push.WithSessionDeliveryGate`; `services.AutoApprovedGate`; the seven legacy counters; `ssq-notify` enum fix, `--dry-run` and schema stamp; hook-handler `delivery_class=routine` stamps; `SendNotification` unversioned counter, hourly WARN and server-only untrusted stamp; matrix, sink guard (type-based, fixture negative control), no-env-var scan, flaky-hazard scan, straggler, startup and Slack-cell tests; the filter benchmarks.
- **Plan assumptions found wrong or refined**:
  1. The `ssq_notify_schema` stamp goes in `scripts/ssq-notify`, not the hook handler's `send_notification`: the Stage 2 probe (and any other direct caller) runs `ssq-notify` itself, so a hook-handler-only stamp would have marked the probe unversioned.
  2. `HintUntrustedType` cannot ride on the client-writable `delivery_class`; it is the separate server-only key `ssq_untrusted_type`, stripped from every client request before the server decides whether to set it.
  3. `HintRoutine` demotes failure-class events only. A stamp must not be able to hide a needs-human event, and the plan text allowed it.
  4. `Remove` takes any identity key (UUID, title, tmux name): the delete path (`removeFromAllPollers`) only has the title.
  5. A single refresh primes the whole storage snapshot into the index, so straggler counting is per snapshot (each straggler still counts `unresolved` once; `resolved_later` moves for sessions the snapshot contains).
  6. `CreateWorktreeSession` also builds `Hidden`-capable instances (no caller sets it today) and gets the same pre-`Start` upsert.
  7. The consumer scan is package-level (a `Subscribe` call in any package that references `EventNotification`), as the plan said, which lists seven `server/services` functions that subscribe for other event types; each is allowlisted with a reason.
  8. The duration histogram is sampled one in 32 notification decisions: on the benchmark machine a clock read (about 0.7 us) costs more than the rest of the visible-session path.
- **Measured latency** (`go test -bench`, AMD Ryzen 9 7900X, `-count 5`, medians; table in `docs/reference/notification-delivery-gate.md`): filter-only 3 ns (non-notification), about 220 ns and 0 allocs (visible session), about 450 ns (hidden session decision), about 570 ns and 2 allocs (unresolved); `Publish` itself about 6.2 us; index upsert about 130 us at 1,000 sessions. The 5% ratio is recalibrated once to: visible and non-notification events within 10% of `Publish`, any hidden-session decision under 1 us.
- **Deviations and skipped**: Task 2.6c (e2e) is not written: Spike 1.3 recorded that no seam creates `Hidden: true` sessions in the e2e server. T-MX-04 (per-kind override in the matrix) belongs to PR 2e. The Slack cell lives in `server/slack_gate_test.go` (the review-queue manager is package `server`). The "regression test that the benchmark stays within the ratio" is not asserted in CI (a timing assertion is the flake class `deterministic-fast-tests` forbids); the ratio is recorded here instead. `FlagObserver` is defined and implemented by `FlagCache`, but the call from `FeatureFlagService.UpdateFeatureFlag` waits for PR 2a-2/2e, when the flag is registered (until then only a `config.json` edit can set it, picked up by the 5s ticker). The Task 2.6h before/after `go test -race ./server/services` wall time is not taken: a baseline needs a second checkout built against the regenerated proto output; the new tests' own cost is measured instead: the 13 new `server/services` tests take 3.2 s wall under `-race` (5.7 s summed, run in parallel), `server/deliverygate` 1.4 s under `-race` (the `sinkguard` scans add about 15 s, behind the tag), the `server` startup test 1.9 s. Whole-package wall times after this PR, measured once: `go test ./server/services` 92 s, `go test -race ./server/services` 162.0 s (4 min 8 s with compile; already above the 150 s `-race` ceiling BUG-067 records, with the new tests contributing at most 3.2 s of it); no before figure.
- **Awaiting operator**: Task 1.3a (the live `ssq-notify` curl). The enum fix, the class stamps and the version rule were implemented from code reading and are pinned by the parity and stamp tests, not by a live run. The installed `~/.local/bin/ssq-notify` must be redeployed (`make install`) before the gate flag is turned on; `notification_rpc_unversioned_total` is the detector.

#### PR 2a-2 implementation record (2026-10-09; branch `notification-tray-hidden-session-gate`)
Stories 2.8 (Tasks 2.8c-2.8h), 2.10 and the registration of `hidden_session_gate` are implemented. Reference doc: `docs/reference/notification-delivery-gate.md` (operator checklist, stats file, audit file, crash producers).

- **Landed**: `deliverygate.Stats` (72 hourly buckets, gate-on time credited from the flag snapshot via a `FlagCache` swap hook, soak figures), `StatsStore` interface and no-op default, `services.FileStatsStore` and `StatsWriter` (atomic write, checksum, quarantine, foreign-writer merge, bounded final flush deferred at the top of `Server.Shutdown`), the `GetDeliveryGateStats` handler, `AuditSink` (`hidden_reply_audit.go`) with `flag_change` requested/result lines, persisted `boot_seq`, a 64-entry non-blocking tightening queue and the fallback WARN, true previous value plus rollback-by-delete in `UpdateFeatureFlag`, `FlagObserver` called after every persisted change, `AddStatusDetailSource`, the enable-only guard, crash FAILURE from `sessionExitedPublisher` with the 3-per-minute limiter and in-place summary, the Settings status line, and (last commit) the `knownFeatureFlags` entry.
- **Deviations and refinements**:
  1. Persisted counter names are six, not three (`suppressed`, `would_suppress`, `hidden_delivered`, `unresolved`, `delivered_while_on`, `probe_delivered_while_on`): `failure_delivered_while_on`, `needs_human_delivered_while_on` and the probe figure are "since the last off flip" and cannot be derived from `hidden_delivered` alone. Key ceiling is 6 x 5 kinds x 3 classes x 72 = 6,480 (the plan's "4 classes" has no fourth class in `DeliveryClass`).
  2. `since_process_start` entries carry the extra label dimensions in the counter name (`would_suppress{channel=bus,...}`) because `DeliveryGateCounter` has only `kind` and `class`.
  3. `process_start` in the response is the current process start; the previous window's start is reloaded into `Stats` (`PreviousProcessStart`) but the frozen proto has no field for it.
  4. Events are bucketed by the accumulator's last tick (no clock read on the publish path, hidden decision stays under 1 us); an event within a minute of an hour boundary may land in the earlier bucket.
  5. `flag_history` is derived from `FlagCache` swaps, so a `RESET_GLOBAL` reads as `SET_DISABLED` until Story 2.11 carries the mutation through.
  6. `UpdateFeatureFlag` with `scope` or `mutation` is still the contract-PR `Unimplemented` stub, so "RESET_GLOBAL and CLEAR_SCOPE are never refused by the precondition" is structurally true (only the enabling branch consults the guard) but cannot be exercised until Story 2.11.
  7. The final-flush-on-`httpServer.Shutdown`-error path is proven structurally (AST test that `defer s.finalStatsFlush()` precedes the call) and by ordering, not by forcing a 10s shutdown timeout.
  8. The sink resolves its directory and takes `boot_seq` lazily on first use, so a server that never flips a gate flag creates no `audit/` directory.
  9. `flag_change` lines go to the single sink file named by ADR-010 (`hidden-session-replies.jsonl`).
  10. `server/deliverygate/system_ids_test.go` gained the reviewed dynamic id form `uuid` (the crash producer).
- **Plan assumptions found wrong**: (a) Task 2.8g's "tests: unknown before registration" cannot be a test in the same tree as the registration; it is shown by the commit order (the gate tests failed with `unknown feature flag` until the last commit). (b) "Hook Stop `task_failed`" is not stamped routine: only post-tool and subagent errors are; a main-session Stop with an error reason is failure class. (c) `Autonomous fix stuck` and the rate-limit hard stops are not reachable for hidden sessions before PR 2b because the legacy `!inst.Hidden` checks still drop them.
- **Stage 0 list for the PR description**: stats RPC, `delivery-gate-stats.json`, `<config dir>/audit/` created on first gate flip, crash FAILURE for visible and hidden sessions (own commit, revertible alone), the true-previous-value and delete-on-rollback change to `UpdateFeatureFlag` for every flag, status_detail composite.
- **Awaiting operator**: (1) the three Stage 1b backlog items (placeholder table in the reference doc; created only after PR 2a-2 **and** PR 2e merge, ids and dates to be filled in); (2) the live Stage 2 probe and soak; (3) `make install-service` redeploy is not done here by design.

#### PR 2e implementation record (2026-10-09; branch `notification-tray-hidden-session-gate`)
Story 2.11 (Tasks 2.11a-2.11g) and the 2a-2 leftovers that depend on scope and mutation. Reference: `docs/reference/notification-delivery-gate.md` ("Per-kind overrides").

- **Landed**: `config.FeatureFlagScopes` (`feature_flag_scopes`) with scoped get/set/delete; `deliverygate.ScopableKinds` (closed set `review`, `diagnose`, `other`), `FlagSettingsFromConfig`, `FlagMutationObserver`; `UpdateFeatureFlag` handling all four mutations under `updateMu` with scope validation, scope read-back in the response and in `GetFeatureFlags`, guard on enabling only, rollback by delete for scoped keys through an optional `ScopedFeatureController`; `flag_history` and `flag_change` audit lines carry the mutation; per-kind explicit-false WARN (`Gate.WarnExplicitOffKinds`), a `status_detail` source and `soak.explicit_off_scopes`; Settings > Features disclosure, global Reset to default, explicit `global` scope on the gate toggle.
- **Tests**: T-FL-01..06, 10, 13, 15 (scoped half), 16, 21 (per-kind half), 22, 23, T-FL-11/19 (context), T-FL-12 (page), T-FL-05 (`TestGate_ShouldSuppressReviewAndShadowTheInheritingKind_...`), T-MX-04 (matrix, with triage seeded in the index to prove it inherits). T-CT-08 now asserts `InvalidArgument` instead of the contract-PR `Unimplemented`.
- **Deviations**: (1) The context keeps `setFlag(name, boolean | FlagChange)` rather than the plan's `setFlag(name, mutation, enabled?, scope?)`, so the ten existing callers and the mocks in other test files are untouched. (2) Task 2.11d needed no gate change: PR 2a-1 already routes `decideHidden` through `EnabledFor(res.Kind)`. (3) `"global"` is accepted as a set scope on every flag, not only the scopable one (it names the global key, not a scope); kind scopes on a non-scopable flag are `InvalidArgument`. (4) `RESET_GLOBAL` is valid on any known flag and drives the controller to the registered default. (5) The Task 2.9d global half (global explicit-false WARN and status line) stays in PR 2b; only the per-kind half (2.11g) is here. (6) No registry JSON change: the page's `// +feature: settings-features` marker already covers the new component, `make registry-generate` produced no diff.
- **Plan assumptions found wrong**: (a) T-FL-15's scoped half has no failing step in production: the gate flag has no controller, so rollback-by-delete is only reachable through the `ScopedFeatureController` seam the tests use. (b) A `RESET_GLOBAL` from an explicit false to a default-off flag moves no effective value, so history must record the operator's mutation as a hint, not infer it from the snapshot swap; the hint is applied under `reloadMu` with the swap.
- **Awaiting operator**: the three Stage 1b backlog items (both PR 2a-2 and PR 2e are now on the branch; create them when the branch merges, due 7, 10 and 14 days later); the live Stage 2 probe; a live click-through of the override disclosure on a deployed build.

#### Story 2.8: Observability verification
**As an** operator, **I want** suppressed counts visible after the fact, **so that** I can confirm nothing needed was dropped.
**Gate**: Tasks 2.8c-2.8h and the stats handler start only after Contract PR 2 (Story 1.6, whose response shape carries the gate-on-time fields) and the focused re-review PASS on item 4 ("Pending focused re-review"); **Task 2.8h additionally needs PR 1f-a (Task 1.8e, the raw request record; review-repair iteration 4, ADV-N26)**, which supplies the `listener`, `peer_addr`, `host`, `origin`, `user_agent` and `auth_mode` fields of the `flag_change` lines (review-repair iteration 3, ADV-N20).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Suppression emits one rate-limited log and one counter increment with the closed label set.
  - *Given* 166 identical suppressed events for session `h1` within 60s, *Then* 1 log line is emitted (then one carrying `suppressed_since_last=165` after the window) and the counter reads 166; the limiter map never exceeds 4096 keys under 10,000 distinct keys.
- Hidden failure delivery is logged, not suppressed.
  - *Given* hidden FAILURE, *Then* `hidden_delivery_allowed` is logged with `channel=bus` and delivered.
- The soak is observable without OTel (pre-mortem #2, validation T-OB-07..T-OB-09).
  - *Given* telemetry is not initialized (`telemetry.GetMeter()` returns the no-op meter, `telemetry/telemetry.go:274-279`), *When* the operator calls `GetDeliveryGateStats` (reachable on `:8543` **without auth** and on `:8444` behind auth: `SetupAuth` has no non-test caller, so the earlier wording "authenticated by the same global middleware as `ClearNotificationHistory`" was false; the RPC exposes aggregate counters only, with no session id, title or reply text as a label, which is why the exposure is accepted; not exposed over MCP), *Then* it returns the `since_process_start` totals for every counter in the Observability Plan (suppressed, would_suppress, hidden_delivered with the `kind` label, unresolved by class, index_miss, refresh results, legacy_hidden_suppressed by site/type/class, rpc_unversioned, filter_panic, reply and backlog-steer outcomes) plus the persisted `buckets`, `flag_history`, `events_by_kind_24h` and `soak` messages of the Story 1.6 shape.
  - *Given* the gate is on for a kind and hidden traffic flows (gate-on-time evidence, adversarial ADV-B3), *When* the writer ticks each minute (fake clock) and on every `OnFlagChanged`, *Then* the elapsed seconds are credited to `gate_on_seconds` (global when `EnabledFor` is on at the global scope, and per kind when the kind's effective value is on) and to `uptime_seconds` of the current bucket, using the snapshot that was in force during the interval (a flip mid-interval splits it); `hidden_events_seen` counts hidden-session events the Gate evaluated, `hidden_events_while_on` those evaluated while that kind's effective value was on and `routine_events_while_on` those of them whose class is routine **and that the gate decided to suppress (an `Unresolved` session is delivered fail-open and does not count)**; `flag_history` records each change of `hidden_session_gate` (global and `kind:*`, last 20, also persisted). The server-computed **`soak_streak_hours`** (formerly `soak_gate_on_hours_with_traffic`; review-repair iteration 2, ADV-N3, ARCH-C25) = for the buckets since `last_off_flip_at` (the whole window when none), the sum of `min(gate_on_seconds, uptime_seconds) / 3600` over the buckets whose `routine_events_while_on > 0`, **excluding the bucket that contains `last_off_flip_at`**; a per-kind figure is computed the same way for the Stage 1b review-only flip. Hours with no routine traffic neither count nor break the streak, an off flip restarts the streak at the flip (it does not lock the figure out for 72 hours), and a process-down hour contributes nothing because `uptime_seconds` caps it.
  - *Given* a soak that spans a restart, a mid-soak off flip (explicit false then Reset to default), a review-only phase followed by a global phase, and a zero-traffic day, *Then* the figure counts only gate-on time in buckets with routine traffic, restarts from the off flip (24 gate-on routine hours after the flip read 24 within the window, T-OB-22), and never shows "gate on, zero traffic" for a bucket in which the process was down or the gate was off (T-OB-15..T-OB-18). *Given* `stats_file_status.writable == false` (a figure that a restart would lose), *Then* the response says so and the Stage 3 prerequisite requires `writable == true` (T-OB-23).
  - *Given* the failure path (review-repair iterations 3 and 4, ADV-N19, ADV-N25), *Then* the response carries `soak.failure_delivered_while_on`, `soak.needs_human_delivered_while_on`, `soak.probe_delivered_while_on` and `soak.explicit_off_scopes`, and T-OB-26 asserts that a window with 24 routine gate-on hours and zero failure-class deliveries has `failure_delivered_while_on == 0` (so the Stage 3 prerequisite rejects it), that an `Unresolved` routine event adds no streak hour, that the flip bucket adds none, and that a labelled live probe that traversed the gate moves the matching delivered counter and `probe_delivered_while_on` by one; T-OB-28 asserts that one `error` delivery does not satisfy the needs-human counter and that the probe is counted separately; T-OB-29 asserts the probe row carries the `[synthetic-probe]` prefix, is not pushed (`shouldNotify` false at medium priority) and traversed the real gate, by calling `NotificationService.SendNotification` end to end **with the legacy checks present** and a hidden resolved session (a probe that the legacy check drops would fail it).
  - *Given* one helper increments a counter, *Then* the OTel instrument and the in-process mirror both move by the same amount (a test increments through the helper and reads both, so they cannot drift).
  - *Given* a restart, *Then* `buckets`, `flag_history` and `process_start` of the previous window reload from `<config dir>/delivery-gate-stats.json` while `since_process_start` restarts at zero: the soak is defined on `buckets` only, so "totals since process start" and "not reset on restart" no longer conflict (adversarial B3(c), ARCH-C12).
  - *Given* the stats file (Task 2.8f; triad engineering gap and re-review ARCH-C11/C12, ADV C17), *Then*: (placement) file I/O, `fsync`, quarantine and the writer goroutine live in a `StatsStore` adapter (`server/services/gatestats_store.go`) behind `StatsStore{Load, Save}` declared in `server/deliverygate`, which keeps only in-memory mirrors with `Snapshot() Stats`; the default store in unit tests is a no-op so no test touches disk or leaks a goroutine (goleak, as for the `FlagCache` ticker); the config directory is injected once at construction and **is the test-mode directory when `STAPLER_SQUAD_TEST_DIR` or the e2e `--test-dir` is set**, so e2e runs never write to `~/.stapler-squad/`. (Write semantics) it is written atomically (temp file `delivery-gate-stats.json.<pid>.tmp` in the same directory, `fsync`, rename; mode 0600) at most once a minute and from the shutdown path below; a crash loses at most the last minute (stated); a crash between the temp write and the rename leaves the previous file intact and stale `.tmp` files are removed on start (an undeletable one is logged and ignored). (Bound and schema) it holds exactly the rolling 72 hourly buckets (overwritten, never appended; older dropped on load), persists **only non-zero keys** and only the reduced `(counter, kind, class)` labels (full type/channel/reason labels exist in the in-memory `since_process_start` only), so the key count is at most 3 counters x 5 kinds x 4 classes x 72 buckets = 4,320 and the key-count test asserts both that ceiling and a realistic fixture far below it; the reader `Stat`s the file first and refuses one over 1 MiB (quarantined, start empty). (Integrity) the `checksum` field is the SHA-256 hex of the canonical JSON of the document with `checksum` set to the empty string (Go `encoding/json` of a struct with fixed field order and sorted map keys); it detects accidental corruption, not tampering. *Given* an unknown schema `version`, invalid JSON, a failed checksum or an oversize file, *Then* the server starts with empty buckets, logs one WARN `delivery_gate_stats_corrupt`, renames the bad file to `delivery-gate-stats.json.corrupt-<UTC>` and keeps at most one such file. (Failure never blocks) *Given* an unwritable directory, a full disk or a failing rename, *Then* the write logs one WARN per 10 minutes, sets `stats_file_status.writable=false` in the RPC, and never blocks, panics or fails startup. (Two processes) the file records `writer_pid`; two processes sharing one config directory is last-writer-wins (stated), and a process that finds a different pid that wrote within the last 2 minutes logs WARN `delivery_gate_stats_foreign_writer` and keeps its stats in memory without writing until that pid is gone (the macOS restart orphan case, `service-restart-orphan-process`). **Merge on resume** (minor m5; the buckets are not discarded and the file is not blindly replaced): when the foreign pid is gone, the process reloads the file and merges per `hour_start`: counters are summed, `gate_on_seconds` and `uptime_seconds` are summed and clamped to 3600 per bucket, `flag_history` is merged by `at` and truncated to 20, and `writer_pid` becomes its own. **`Save` is serialized by a mutex inside the adapter** (the writer goroutine and the shutdown flush share the pid-named temp file, ARCH-C23).
  - *Given* the writer lifecycle (ARCH-C12), *Then* the writer goroutine is started by the server (not by `BuildRuntimeDeps`, which has no server context) next to `notifications.StartUrgencyDecaySweeper(serverCtx, ..., &srv.backgroundTasksWG)` (`server/server.go:308`), is joined through `backgroundTasksWG`, and a **final flush is registered with `defer` at the top of `Shutdown`** (`server/server.go:1503`), so it runs after `httpServer.Shutdown` returns **including on its error path**, where `Shutdown` returns early (`:1541-1544`, `return err`; a flush placed "as the last step" would be skipped): `shutdownHooks` run first (`:1521`) and the join second (`:1534`), both before HTTP handlers stop, so a flush placed in a hook would miss the last publishes; the flush is attempted even if the join timed out, which is why `Save` is serialized by a mutex inside the adapter (the writer goroutine and the flush both write the pid-named temp file). Publishes after the final flush (for example session stop events during storage close) are lost by design and said so in the reference doc. **The deferred flush is bounded** (review-repair iteration 3, ARCH-C33e): `Save` runs in a helper goroutine and the `defer` waits at most `StatsFlushTimeout` (INFERRED 5s, inside the systemd stop window), so a stalled `fsync` cannot extend `Shutdown` past the stop window; the abandoned write can only leave a stale `.tmp`, which the start-up cleanup removes. Tests: a publish after cancellation appears in the final file; `httpServer.Shutdown` returning an error still flushes; a tick and the final flush racing under `-race` leave a valid file (T-OB-21, T-OB-24).
  - *Given* Settings > Features, *Then* under `hidden_session_gate` the status line reads "Shadow: N hidden events would have been suppressed in the last 24h; M unresolved fail-open; K unversioned ssq-notify" from `GetDeliveryGateStats` (UX FG-7).
**Files**: `server/deliverygate/gate_test.go`, `metrics.go`, `metrics_test.go`, `statsstore.go` (the `StatsStore` interface and no-op default only), `server/services/gatestats_store.go`, `gatestats_store_test.go`, `server/services/notification_service.go`, `server/services/feature_flag_service.go` (Task 2.8g), `server/services/hidden_reply_audit.go` and `hidden_reply_audit_test.go` (Task 2.8h), `server/server.go` (writer start and the `defer`red final flush), `web-app/src/app/settings/features/page.tsx`, `docs/reference/notification-delivery-gate.md`. (The RPC and its stub land in Contract PR 2, Story 1.6.)

##### Task 2.8h: Audit sink core: `flag_change` lines, the non-blocking tightening queue, the degraded-mode fallback record (first PR that can flip the gate)
- Build the audit sink with the invariants of ADR-010 decision 6 here (moved from Task 5.2e, which now extends it; ADV-N5: `flag_change` must exist from the first PR in which the gate can flip, not from Epic 5): process-wide mutex around append and rotate, `fsync` of a pre-write line, 0700 directory and 0600 file, config directory resolved once at construction, rotation failure is `audit_failed` with 0 writes, 5 MiB rotation keeping 3. **Two paths**: (1) the blocking, durable `Append(ctx, line)` for Reply, the steer, the prune apply and **loosening** flips; (2) the **tightening path** (`hidden_session_reply` to off, `hidden_session_readonly_guards` to on, every `hidden_session_gate` flip): the flip is persisted first, the line goes on a bounded queue (64 entries) drained by one goroutine that takes the sink mutex, and the request **never waits on the sink mutex or an `fsync`** (ADV-N6a: a stalled `fsync` holds the shared mutex and the best-effort append of a kill switch would otherwise wait unbounded under `updateMu`); a full queue or a failed drain writes one WARN record carrying the same fields to the main log (`flag_change_audit_degraded`) and increments `hidden_session_audit_degraded_total{mode=flag_change_queued|flag_change_fallback_log}`. Loosening flips keep "audit before persist, refuse with `Internal` if the append fails" **with the append performed before `updateMu` is taken (`feature_flag_service.go:400-401`) and bounded by a 2s timeout (the append runs in a helper goroutine; a timeout returns `Internal` with nothing persisted; no sink call is ever made while `updateMu` is held), so a stalled `fsync` cannot make the kill switch queue behind a loosening flip** (ADV-N17), **except** `hidden_session_readonly_guards` to off and `terminal_write_lease` to off (Story 5.0), which are the escape hatches for a sink fault and are persisted with only the fallback log record when the sink is down (ADR-010 decision 6, degraded modes). `flag_change` lines carry `ts`, `kind=flag_change`, the flag, scope, previous and new value, `listener`, `peer_addr`, `host`, `origin`, `user_agent`, `auth_mode`; the request-context fields come from the Story 1.8 record (Task 1.8e, **PR 1f-a**, which carries raw values only and no verdict, so this task no longer waits on the Host-verdict work; review-repair iteration 4, ADV-N26, replacing the earlier dependency on the whole of PR 1f, ADV-N20). **Ordering and the previous value** (review-repair iteration 4, ADV-N24; VERIFIED `previousEnabled := cfg.GetFeatureFlag(name)` is read under `updateMu` (taken at `server/services/feature_flag_service.go:400`) at `:407` while the loosening append runs before the lock, so a line appended before the lock can carry a previous value and an order that disagree with the persisted state, and a failed persist or controller rollback would leave a line for a change that never happened): a flip writes **at most two lines, linked by a `change_id`**. A **loosening** flip appends a durable `phase=requested` line **before `updateMu`**, carrying the *requested* value only (an intent, never a previous value). **Every** flip, tightening included, writes a `phase=result` line after the persist, built under the lock and enqueued by a `defer` registered before the lock is taken so that it runs after the unlock: `outcome` in `applied`, `aborted_persist_failed`, `aborted_controller_failed` or `aborted_rolled_back`, the **true previous value** read under `updateMu`, the new value, and a `seq` taken from a counter incremented **inside** `updateMu`, so lines are ordered by `seq` (the persisted order), never by file position. A reader never takes a lone `requested` line for "the guard was turned off": the effect is the `result` line with `outcome=applied`, and a `requested` line with no `result` (the process died between them, or the sink went down) reads as indeterminate and its fields are also in the fallback WARN record. The result line takes the tightening path (a bounded queue push, never a wait on the sink mutex or an `fsync`). **Boot id and crash windows** (review-repair iteration 6, ARCH-C50, ADV-m16): `seq` is a process-local counter that restarts at 0, so every line also carries a random `boot_id` (identity), a `boot_ts` (process start, a **label only**) and a **persisted monotonic `boot_seq`**, and readers order by `(boot_seq, seq)`, never by `ts` or `boot_ts` (review-repair iteration 6b, ARCH-C54 / ADV-m20: wall-clock `boot_ts` misorders boots across a backward clock step or two starts within the timestamp's resolution, for example a crash loop). `boot_seq` is a small counter file next to the audit file (`<config dir>/audit/boot_seq`, same 0700 directory), incremented and written atomically (tmp `O_EXCL`, `fsync`, rename) when the sink opens, before any line is written; a counter that cannot be persisted is the same fault as an unwritable sink and takes the sink's degraded path (the fallback log record carries `boot_id` and `boot_seq=unknown`). A crash between the persist and the queue drain leaves only the fsynced `requested` line while the guard may really be off, and a loosening append that the 2s helper bound abandons can still complete **later** and land after the caller already got `Internal` for a flip that never happened: both read as "indeterminate", and the reader rule is to read the persisted config (`GetFeatureFlags`) for the actual state, never the log. The atomics (`GuardsFlag`, `LeaseFlag`, the Reply flag) are updated by one `FeatureController` per flag, not by a new callback.
- **True previous value and rollback by delete live here, not in PR 2e** (review-repair iteration 6b, ARCH-C53 / ADV-m23). VERIFIED `UpdateFeatureFlag` reads `previousEnabled := cfg.GetFeatureFlag(name)` (`feature_flag_service.go:407`), and `GetFeatureFlag` returns `false` for an absent key (`config/config.go:1776-1781`); the controller-failure rollback (`:416-440`, write at `:428`) writes that `false` back as an explicit value. `terminal_write_lease` (PR 5a) and `hidden_session_readonly_guards` (PR 5u) are default **on** and ship before PR 2e, so without this change a first flip of either would record `previous=false` and a controller failure would persist an explicit `false`, leaving a safety flag off. This task therefore changes `UpdateFeatureFlag` for the **global** value: the previous value is read from `GetFeatureFlagOverride` (existence flag) plus the registered default, and a controller failure on a key that was absent **deletes** the key (`DeleteFeatureFlag`) instead of writing `false`; Story 2.11 extends the same rule to scoped keys. PR 5a and PR 2e both already depend on PR 2a-2, so no new edge. **The `5a -> 2a-2` wait is stated plainly:** PR 5a (the lease, and with it R3's first step) waits on the audit sink core and this fix, which gives the lease the gate chain's calendar; that is the price of the audited `terminal_write_lease` flip (T-WL-11). The alternative, if the lease is wanted earlier, is to ship PR 5a with only the fallback log record and defer T-WL-11's audit assertion until PR 2a-2 merges; **not taken** (default applied; the operator may override).
- Tests (fake filesystem and clock, no sleeps): a loosening flip stalled in `fsync` neither delays a concurrent tightening flip nor holds `updateMu`, and itself returns `Internal` after the bound with nothing persisted (T-RP-58 extended); `Sync` precedes the caller's return for the blocking path; a stalled `fsync` does not delay a tightening flip (the flip returns and persists, the line is queued, T-RP-58); a full disk makes the loosening `hidden_session_reply` flip fail with `Internal` and 0 persisted change, while the guards-off flip succeeds with the fallback record; every `hidden_session_gate` flip from this PR appends a `flag_change` line (T-FL-24). Two concurrent flips each carry their own `change_id`, `seq` and true previous value, and a persist failure and a controller rollback each produce an `aborted_*` result line with no `applied` line (T-RP-79); a `requested` line that completes after its caller returned `Internal`, with no `result`, is read as indeterminate and two process starts order by `(boot_seq, seq)` even when the fake clock steps backward between them (T-RP-79); and, for a default-on flag with no persisted key, the first flip records the true previous value (`true`) and a controller failure leaves **no key** in the persisted file, not an explicit `false` (T-RP-79, review-repair iteration 6b, ARCH-C53).
- Files: `server/services/hidden_reply_audit.go`, `hidden_reply_audit_test.go`, `server/services/feature_flag_service.go`, `feature_flag_service_test.go`.

#### Story 2.10: A session crash is a failure-class notification (decision O3)
**As an** operator, **I want** a session that crashes to notify me, hidden or not, **so that** a failure in a session the review-queue poller skips is not silent.
**Why this shape (adversarial N2, VERIFIED)**: `buildStatusChangeNotification` returns false unless `Status == Stopped` (`server/push/subscriber.go:173`), so a Crashed transition produces no push today; and there is no bus event for a crash: `MarkCrashed` (`session/instance_crash.go:40-52`) fires `EventExited` and the only server listener, `sessionExitedPublisher` (`server/services/session_service_events.go:109-117`), publishes a bare `session.updated`. So instead of a second crash builder on the push path (which would also need a status concept in the policy), the crash gets **one new bus producer**: a FAILURE(9) `EventNotification`. That flows through the existing gate, history, toast and push paths with no change to `buildStatusChangeNotification`, and the policy needs no `StatusKind`. `PermanentlyFailed` already has a bus producer (`markSessionPermanentlyFailed`, `session/session_driver.go:1004`, type ERROR), so it needs a test only.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Crash publishes one FAILURE notification.
  - *Given* an `Instance` whose `Snapshot().Status` is `Crashed` with `ExitReason` "pane exited 137", *When* `sessionExitedPublisher.OnLifecycleEvent(EventExited, ...)` runs, *Then* exactly one `EventNotification` is published with `NotificationType=FAILURE`, urgent+important priority, `SessionID=inst.UUID`, id `session-crashed-<uuid>-<exit unix seconds>`, title "Session crashed", message naming the session title and the truncated, sanitized exit reason, `SessionScopedMetadata`; the existing `session.updated` publish still happens once. *Given* the same event with status `Stopped`, *Then* no notification.
- Ordering: the status is Crashed before the asynchronous publisher reads it (triad engineering gap).
  - *Given* `Instance.MarkCrashed`, which completes `transitionToLocked(Crashed)` inside `sendSyncErr` and only then calls `fireLifecycleEvent(EventExited)` (`session/instance_crash.go:40-52`, VERIFIED), and the publisher's `go func()` (`server/services/session_service_events.go:109-117`, VERIFIED), *When* the publisher runs, *Then* `Snapshot().Status == Crashed` already holds and exactly one FAILURE is published. *Given* an `EventExited` whose `Snapshot().Status` is not Crashed when the publisher reads it (another exit source, Spike 1.3h), *Then* no crash notification is published and one DEBUG line is logged. The test uses a fake instance whose snapshot is updated after the event and runs under `-race`, reading only through `Snapshot()` (T-CR-06).
- Hidden crash delivers; hidden Stopped does not.
  - *Given* a hidden session, gate on, *When* it crashes, *Then* the bus event is delivered (FAILURE is failure-class, Story 2.1), history gets 1 FAILURE row, and the recording `Notifier` gets 1 push (URGENT passes `shouldNotify`, `subscriber.go:130`); *When* it exits normally (Stopped), *Then* 0 pushes (Story 2.4).
- Visible crash also notifies (documented flag-independent behavior change).
  - *Given* a visible session that crashes, *Then* the same one notification is delivered and the PR description lists it under Stage 0; it is its own commit so it can be reverted alone. Rationale: `PermanentlyFailed` already notifies the same way and a crash is a real failure; failure direction is over-delivery.
- Repeat crashes re-fire.
  - *Given* a session that crashes, is resumed (`ResumeFromCrash`) and crashes again, *Then* the second crash produces a new notification id (the notification store coalesces by `(sessionID, type)`, `server/notifications/store.go:298`, so history shows one record with a higher occurrence count and the toast/push re-fire; confirmed or corrected by Spike 1.3h).
- PermanentlyFailed is covered by its existing producer.
  - *Given* a hidden session and `markSessionPermanentlyFailed`, gate on, *Then* exactly one ERROR is delivered and the resolver returns `Hidden` by UUID although the event carries `item_id=<session UUID>` (Story 2.2).
- Background activity can list it.
  - *Given* a hidden crash, *Then* a FAILURE history record exists for Story 5.4's join (the join's failure-class list is `ERROR`, `FAILURE`, `APPROVAL_NEEDED`, `INPUT_REQUIRED`, `CONFIRMATION_NEEDED`; there is no STATUS_CHANGE crash record).
- The producer enumeration is recorded.
  - *Given* the Hidden-reachable failure producers table in the PR description (hooks Stop `task_failed`, autonomous driver `Autonomous fix stuck`, rate-limit/capacity hard stops, PermanentlyFailed ERROR, crash FAILURE), *Then* each has a test or an explicit "not reachable" note; `ReviewQueuePoller` ErrorState/TestsFailing are recorded as **unreachable for hidden sessions** (`shouldSkipSession`), not relied on.
- The storm risk is mitigated by minimal coalescing, built here (decision of triad iteration 1; this commit changes behavior for VISIBLE sessions regardless of the flag, so it must not be left unmitigated; pre-mortem #9).
  - *Given* 10 sessions crashing within 60s (a tmux or host fault), *Then* the first 3 publish individually and the other 7 are folded into one summary notification (`session-crashed-summary-<window start, unix seconds>`, FAILURE, "7 more sessions crashed", at most 5 titles listed plus a count) updated in place by id, `notification_crash_coalesced_total` rises by 7, every coalesced crash is still logged, and the `session.updated` publish for each session is unaffected. *Given* a hidden and a visible crash in the same window, *Then* both count toward the same global limiter. The earlier bound still applies: `MarkCrashed` only runs after respawn fails and outside the restart grace window (`session/health.go:441-490`, per the pre-mortem; not re-opened here).
**Files**: `server/services/session_service_events.go`, `session_service_events_test.go`, `server/services/session_service_events_hidden_test.go` (new), PR description. No change to `server/push/subscriber.go`.

#### Story 2.11: Per-kind override for `hidden_session_gate` (decision O1)
**As an** operator, **I want** to enable or disable the gate for one kind of hidden session (for example review sessions only) from the feature-flag panel, **so that** I can roll it out and roll it back without a deploy and without flipping every hidden session at once (requirements.md "Risk Control": global + per-scope override).
**Scope model, and why this one**: scope = `HiddenKind` (`review`, `triage`, `diagnose`, `other`), derived from the tags a session carries (`backlog:review`, `backlog:triage` and `backlog:diagnose`; `session/backlog.go:106-108` for the first, `session_driver.go:1112` for the triage/review pair, `session_service_diagnose.go:100-110` for diagnose). It is the smallest scope that serves "enable the gate for review sessions only". Rejected: per-session UUID (sessions are ephemeral and created at tens per day, so an override is stale before it matters, and the panel could not list them); per-workspace/instance (one server instance is one operator, and `STAPLER_SQUAD_INSTANCE` already isolates state dirs, so it is already "global" for that state). Only `hidden_session_gate` is scopable; `notification_tray_v2` is a per-viewer UI flag.
**Gate**: this story starts only after Contract PR 2 (Story 1.6), **PR 2a-2 (which registers `hidden_session_gate`, Task 2.8g)** and the focused re-review PASS on item 3 ("Pending focused re-review"). It is inside R1 (the Stage 1b review-only flip needs it).
**Precedence (most specific wins)**: kind override (explicit on or off) > explicit global value > registry default (`GetFeatureFlagWithDefault`, `config/config.go:1793`). A session with no resolvable kind (`Unresolved`, `NotASession`) uses the global value (stated in the panel help text). **Kind resolution is total and defined** (ARCH-C9, ADV C13): a session carrying several backlog tags resolves `review` > `diagnose` > `triage` > `other`; tags are mutable, so the index re-derives the kind on every tag change; **no producer creates a `backlog:triage` Instance** (the literal appears only in a read at `session/session_driver.go:1112`), so `kind:triage` is an inert control and is offered, validated and shown only if Spike 1.3h proves it reachable, and the panel shows per-kind counts of resolved events for the last 24h (`events_by_kind_24h`) so a dead scope is visible.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Precedence table.
  - *Given* a registry default of off, no persisted values, *Then* `EnabledFor(review)` is false. *Given* global true and a `review` override false, *Then* `EnabledFor(review)` false, `EnabledFor(triage)` true. *Given* global false and `review` override true, *Then* `EnabledFor(review)` true, others false. *Given* the `review` override cleared, *Then* review follows global again. After the PR 2b default flip (default true), a `review` override false makes review sessions deliver everything (documented).
- Validation and back-compat.
  - *Given* `UpdateFeatureFlag{name:"hidden_session_gate", scope:"kind:review", mutation:SET_ENABLED}`, *Then* the response `FeatureFlag.scopes` lists `{scope:"kind:review", enabled:true}` read back from the persisted config (not echoed). *Given* `scope:"kind:bogus"` or a scope on `notification_tray_v2` or on the two safety flags, *Then* `CodeInvalidArgument`. *Given* a request without `scope` or `mutation`, *Then* behavior is exactly today's (the existing test is unchanged), a config file written by the previous version loads unchanged, and **an old client (no new fields) never deletes a persisted value**.
  - *Given* the `FlagMutation` rules (ADR-004 decision 4; review-repair iteration 2, ADV-N5, ARCH-C22/C26), *Then*: `CLEAR_SCOPE` with an empty or non-`kind:` `scope` and `RESET_GLOBAL` with a non-empty `scope` are `InvalidArgument`; **a set (`SET_ENABLED` or `SET_DISABLED`) with an empty `scope` is `InvalidArgument`** and a global set must carry the literal `scope = "global"`, so a client that drops `scope` on a per-kind click is refused instead of writing the global flag, and a test asserts the persisted global value is byte-identical afterwards; `FLAG_MUTATION_UNSPECIFIED` with a non-empty `scope` and an unknown enum value (a newer client) are `InvalidArgument`; the set values carry the enabled state, so `enabled` is ignored and a set can never write `false` because `enabled` was omitted; `CLEAR_SCOPE{scope:"kind:review"}` deletes only that override (the global value and other kinds are unchanged) and `RESET_GLOBAL` deletes only the explicit global key. Disabling, `CLEAR_SCOPE` and `RESET_GLOBAL` are never refused by the Task 2.8g precondition (T-FL-22, T-FL-23).
  - *Given* (the global-key half is built in Task 2.8h, PR 2a-2, because default-on safety flags ship before this PR; this story extends the same rule to scoped keys) a controller failure on a scoped or global change whose key was absent before, *Then* the rollback **deletes** the key (`DeleteFeatureFlagScope` or `DeleteFeatureFlag`, chosen from `GetFeatureFlagOverride`'s existence flag) instead of writing an explicit `false` (the current rollback, `previousEnabled := cfg.GetFeatureFlag(name)` at `feature_flag_service.go:407,428`, converts an absent key into `false`); a test asserts the persisted file has no key afterwards. The scopes map is read-modified-written under the same `updateMu` as the global map (whole-config load/save stays a pre-existing limit, documented).
  - *Given* a client that sent a scoped change (a newer client against an older server that drops `scope`), *When* the response has no `scopes` entry for it, *Then* the client reports an error, not success (a test with a stub server that ignores `scope`); the displayed state is always the server read-back (FG-3).
- FlagCache keeps the publish path I/O-free.
  - *Given* an override change via `UpdateFeatureFlag`, *Then* the `FlagCache` snapshot reflects it on the next `Resolve` through the single mutex-guarded `Reload()` (no restart, no 5s wait, and no stale ticker swap, Story 2.1); *Given* 10,000 `EnabledFor(kind)` calls, *Then* 0 config reads; *Given* an external edit of `feature_flag_scopes` in the config file, *Then* the 5s ticker (fake clock) picks it up; *Given* an `UpdateSession` tag edit that changes `backlog:review` to `backlog:diagnose` on a hidden session, *Then* the index kind (and so the resolved flag scope) changes.
- Gate decisions follow the kind.
  - *Given* global off and a `review` override on, gate wired, *Then* a hidden review TASK_COMPLETE is suppressed and a hidden triage TASK_COMPLETE is `ShadowSuppress` (delivered, counted).
- Panel UI.
  - *Given* Settings > Features, *Then* `hidden_session_gate` shows an "Overrides by session kind" disclosure with a control per reachable kind (Inherit / On / Off, >= 44px, labelled; `triage` only if Spike 1.3h proved it reachable) and, beside each, the count of events resolved to that kind in the last 24h; each change calls `updateFeatureFlag` with `scope:"kind:<kind>"` and `mutation = SET_ENABLED` or `SET_DISABLED` (the global toggle sends `scope:"global"`), the displayed state is the server read-back (FG-3) and a missing read-back entry is an error; Inherit sends `mutation = CLEAR_SCOPE` with the kind's scope; a global "Reset to default" sends `mutation = RESET_GLOBAL` with an empty `scope`, deletes the explicit global key and the read-back shows the registry default (so a soak-time off cannot linger as an explicit `false`). `notification_tray_v2` shows no override controls. The help text says unresolved and not-a-session events use the global value.
**Files**: `config/config.go`, `config/config_test.go`, `server/services/feature_flag_service.go`, `feature_flag_service_test.go`, `server/deliverygate/flagcache.go`, `flagcache_test.go`, `web-app/src/lib/contexts/FeatureFlagsContext.tsx`, `web-app/src/app/settings/features/page.tsx`, `page.test.tsx`, `docs/reference/notification-delivery-gate.md`. (Proto fields land in Contract PR 2, Story 1.6, after the focused re-review PASS on item 3.)

### Epic 2b: Legacy-check migration (PR 2b = Story 2.9, after the Stage 2 soak passes) and history prune tool (PR 2c = Story 2.7)
**Goal**: Delete the seven legacy suppressions in the same commit that makes the gate default-on, and give the operator a safe, dry-run-first tool for the stored rows. (Story 2.9 ships as PR 2b; Story 2.7 ships afterwards as PR 2c and is not part of release boundary R1; stories are listed in number order.)

#### Story 2.7: Operator-triggered history prune (dry-run first)
**As an** operator, **I want** existing hidden-session routine rows removed on my command, **so that** the tray stops showing them without ever deleting a pending decision.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Dry run by default and lists what it would remove.
  - *Given* a store with rows for hidden `h1` (WARNING read, INFO, TASK_COMPLETE), hidden `h2` (WARNING **unread**), deleted-session `d1`, visible `v1` (WARNING), *When* `PruneHiddenSessionNotifications{}` (no `apply`) runs, *Then* it returns the ids it would delete (h1's read WARNING, INFO, TASK_COMPLETE), deletes nothing, and reports `kept_unread_actionable:1`, `undeterminable:1` (d1), visible kept.
- Never deletes an unread actionable row unless explicitly opted in.
  - *Given* `apply=true` without `include_unread_actionable`, *Then* h2's unread WARNING remains; with `include_unread_actionable=true` it is removed (O4).
- Undeterminable rows are always kept.
  - *Given* rows resolving `Unresolved` or `NotASession`, including rows of sessions deleted before the feature, *Then* they are kept in every mode.
- Refuses while the index is unseeded.
  - *Given* `Seeded()==false`, *Then* `FailedPrecondition`.
- Backup and re-runnable.
  - *Given* `apply=true`, *Then* a **timestamped** backup `notifications.json.pre-prune-<UTC yyyymmddThhmmssZ>.bak` exists (a second apply leaves the first backup intact), the removed ids and the caller's remote address are logged at INFO, and running again removes nothing more. There is no restart hook and no marker key.
- Authorization is stated and tested (adversarial M4).
  - *Given* an unauthenticated request when auth is enabled (the `:8444` chain), *Then* the auth middleware rejects it (`server/middleware/auth.go`); on `:8543` there is no auth (`SetupAuth` has no non-test caller), so **`PruneHiddenSessionNotifications` is registered in the `LocalWriteGuard` procedure set, whole procedure (dry run and apply), with the rebinding gate** (Story 1.8): *Given* Host `evil.example:8543` or a foreign Origin, *Then* 403 before the handler, *Given* a detected LAN hostname on a wildcard bind, *Then* the request is allowed (no `LoopbackBound` condition). `ClearNotificationHistory`, which has the same exposure today, is **not** added to the set (a follow-up, out of scope). The RPC has no MCP tool; the log line records the remote address and listener.
- Every apply is audited before it deletes (review-repair iteration 2, ARCH Prune recommendation).
  - *Given* `apply=true`, *Then* one `kind=prune` line is appended and `fsync`ed to the audit sink (Task 2.8h) **before** any row is deleted, carrying `ts`, `matched_count`, `include_unread_actionable`, the per-reason counts, `listener`, `peer_addr`, `host`, `origin`, `user_agent`, `auth_mode` and `peer_loopback`/`proxied` and **no row content, title or session id**; an outcome line follows; *Given* the append fails, *Then* `Internal` with 0 deletions and no backup written; *Given* a dry run, *Then* no audit line (INFO log only). A flaw in the sink never blocks the dry run.
- ID form is documented.
  - *Given* the callback `isPrunable(sessionID string, ...)`, *Then* its doc states that the store holds the ID form each producer used (UUID or title) and the index resolves both.
- ID-form mismatch cannot misclassify a row as hidden (pre-mortem #10, P3).
  - *Given* store rows whose `session_id` is a title and others whose `session_id` is a UUID for the same hidden session, plus a visible session whose title equals a hidden session's alias key, *When* the dry run groups its result, *Then* the output is grouped by **resolved session title and `HiddenKind`** (not by raw id), each row shows the id form it was matched by (`uuid`, `title`, `tmux`, `alias`), and a row matched only through an alias or tombstone is reported as `matched_by_alias` so the operator can see it before Stage 4 applies. A row that matches both a hidden and a visible entry is `undeterminable` and kept.
**Files**: `server/notifications/store.go`, `store_test.go`, `server/services/notification_service.go`, `server/deliverygate/index.go`.

#### Story 2.9: Staged removal of legacy checks with default-on
**As an** operator, **I want** the legacy checks removed only when the gate is already on, **so that** no merge re-floods.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Characterization tests written first and green on both flag states before any deletion (Task 2.9a): hidden `ReasonTaskComplete/Idle/Stale` never reach `OnItemAdded` (poller `shouldSkipSession`; startup scanner `Determine()` gate); hidden review-role autonomous run emits exactly its role-specific notification and nothing generic.
- Single commit: default flips on and legacy checks go away.
  - *Given* the commit, *Then* `knownFeatureFlags` default for `hidden_session_gate` is true and `rg 'inst\.Hidden|\.Hidden\b' server/services/session_service_events.go server/review_queue_manager.go server/services/notification_service.go server/services/autonomous_orchestration_service.go` finds no raw reads; the Story 2.6 raw-`.Hidden` allowlist is empty.
- Behavior with the gate on (default) matches the soak.
  - *Given* hidden session, gate on, *When* `onRateLimitRecoveryFailed` fires, *Then* one FAILURE(9) reaches history; `onRateLimitRecoverySucceeded` (INFO) 0; `onColdRestoreLostHistory` and `onRateLimitDetected` follow the Spike 1.3c classification (stamped failure or routine).
  - *Given* hidden session and a LOW `task_complete` request to `SendNotification`, *Then* `Success:true` and no event delivered; with the flag off, one event is delivered (documented escape hatch).
- `suppressForHidden` is deleted, not rewired.
  - *Given* hidden session with `ReasonTestsFailing` via the startup scanner, *When* `OnItemAdded` runs, *Then* the bus event and the Slack fake each receive 1 as today; there is no `Gate` call in `review_queue_manager.go` and no `EvaluateReviewItem`.
- Autonomous duplicate check.
  - *Given* hidden non-review run that ends "Autonomous fix stuck", gate on, *Then* one FAILURE is delivered (volume change called out in the PR description and soak notes).
- The newly delivered volume is reviewed before the commit (adversarial N1).
  - *Given* the Stage 2 soak output for `legacy_hidden_suppressed{class=failure|needs_human}` by `type` and `site`, *Then* the 2b PR description contains that table with a per-day volume and an accept-or-stamp-routine decision per row (the events the legacy checks swallow today that the policy will now deliver, e.g. `onRateLimitRecoveryFailed`, "Autonomous fix stuck").
- An explicit `false` is surfaced, not silently overridden (adversarial M2).
  - *Given* `config.json` with `hidden_session_gate: false` persisted before the commit, *When* the server starts after 2b, *Then* a WARN `hidden_session_gate_explicit_false` is logged once and `GetFeatureFlags` returns `status_detail` "Gate is OFF: hidden sessions deliver everything"; *Given* no persisted key, *Then* the flag is on and `status_detail` is empty. The 2b PR description and `docs/reference/notification-delivery-gate.md` name the caveat.
  - *Given* (asserted once Task 2.11g has merged) a persisted per-kind override `false` (for example `feature_flag_scopes: {"hidden_session_gate": {"kind:review": false}}`) with the global value unset or true, *When* the server starts after 2b, *Then* one WARN per overridden kind is logged and `status_detail` reads "Gate is OFF for kind review: hidden review sessions deliver everything" (pre-mortem #3: a soak-time rollback via the panel is exactly how these get written). The Stage 3 prerequisite "`status_detail` is empty" is therefore mechanical.
**Files**: `server/services/session_service_events.go`, `server/review_queue_manager.go`, `server/services/notification_service.go`, `server/services/autonomous_orchestration_service.go`, `server/services/capacity_monitor.go`, `config/config.go`, matching `_test.go`.

## Phase 3: Web notification UX

### Epic 3: Toast stack (PR 3, `feat(web):`, behind `notification_tray_v2`)
**Goal**: At most 3 toasts visible with a "+N more" chip and one one-tap bulk control, "Move all to tray", that clears the deck without deleting anything; toasts never cover the terminal input or soft keyboard. Prerequisite: Contract PR 1 (Story 1.4) supplies `is_pending_decision` on `NotificationHistoryRecord` and on the live `NotificationEvent` (additive proto, computed server-side by `IsPendingDecision`). **PR 3 merges behind the default-off `notification_tray_v2` flag; the operator's device checks (Task 3.7d) gate turning the flag on, not the merge.**

#### Implementation status: PR 3 (recorded after the build; the story text below is unchanged)

Stories 3.1 to 3.10 are implemented behind `notification_tray_v2` (default off) in the order 3.1, 3.2, 3.6, 3.3, 3.4, 3.5, 3.7, 3.9, 3.8, 3.10 (3.6 before any live role, 3.9 before 3.8 so the moved message has a sender and a receiver). Commits are listed in the PR 3 hand-off; every one carries "Plan slice: PR 3 (Story 3.x)".

**AWAITING OPERATOR (not run, not faked)**
- [ ] **Spike 1.2** (Radix non-modal vs xterm focus, and the `useTerminalGestures` document-listener audit). Story 3.8 was built on the documented default: touches that start on a toast row stop at the row (`useSwipeToDismiss.ts`), vertical drift of 10px cancels, a release past 40% of the width or a 60px fling at 0.3 px/ms dismisses. The default needs the DV-6 real-device confirmation.
- [ ] **Task 3.7d DV-1..DV-9** on a real Android Chrome and iOS Safari (browser tab and installed PWA), recorded in the PR 3 thread before the flag is turned on. Playwright covers only the CSS-variable path (`setKeyboardOpen` overrides `visualViewport.height`), so DV-3 (the soft keyboard), DV-4 (URL bar), DV-5 (cutouts), DV-6 (swipe vs pull-to-refresh) and DV-7 (screen readers) rest on the operator.
- [ ] **Task 3.7g MC-1** manual contrast of the toast, chip and handle over the xterm canvas (bright and dark xterm themes, coloured ANSI behind, light and dark app theme; text >= 4.5:1, non-text >= 3:1).
- [x] **The five committed e2e specs ran green** (10 tests, `chromium` project, isolated server on a free port with `STAPLER_SQUAD_TMUX_SOCKET` set, headless shell 1208 because the pinned 1200 was not installed). Re-run once in CI with the real toolchain: `cd tests/e2e && npm install && npm test`. Of the pre-existing specs touched, 28 of 35 passed; `notifications-responsive.spec.ts:117` (onboarding modal plus a collapsed Recent activity section) and the six `backlog-session-steer.spec.ts` tests (the item detail never opens from the list) fail before reaching anything this PR changed.
- [ ] **Task 3.2d** (stamp auto-remediating WARNING producers with `auto_remediating=true`): not done. `session.Notifier.Notify(itemID, title, message, type, urgent, important)` has no metadata parameter, so stamping `session/backlog_lifecycle_pr.go:1317` needs a Notifier interface change that reaches `server/`. Until then that WARNING is a pending decision and pins.
- [ ] **Server stamp of `risk_level` on the approval notification** (`server/services/approval_handler.go` `broadcastApprovalNotification` builds the metadata without it; `PendingApproval.RiskLevel` exists). Until it is stamped the phone approval card treats every approval as unrecorded risk and asks "Approve this command?" first (the documented fail-safe).

**Plan assumptions found wrong while building**
- Task 3.7c names `PaneSplitRenderer.tsx` as the publisher site. The row the wireframe means ("Terminal Diff VCS Files ...") is `SessionDetailView`'s `tabsWrapper`; `PaneSplitRenderer` only owns the multi-pane "[Window 3] [+]" strip. `--mobile-stack-top-offset` is published from `SessionDetailView` (`usePublishStackTopOffset`).
- Task 3.7f assumes a right-hand xterm scrollbar. The app draws its own left-hand scrollbar track (`XtermTerminal.tsx`), so `--terminal-scrollbar-width` defaults to 0 and nothing publishes it.
- Story 3.1 says `NotificationToast` timers move into `ToastStack`; the unmodified `NotificationContext.test.tsx` mocks `NotificationToast` to `null` and advances five minutes expecting toasts to stay. Per-toast timers therefore start when the card mounts (`onPresent`), which is also the right behavior for an overflow toast with no card yet.
- Story 3.10 says a flag toggle applies "without reload (`useFeatureFlags` refetch)". `FeatureFlagsProvider` fetches once on mount and after its own `setFlag`, so a toggle made in the same page (Settings > Features) applies on the next render, while a change made from another tab or an RPC needs a reload.
- Live events had no stable client id, so the cross-tab id-set message could never match another tab's toast. Toasts now take the server's `notificationId`.
- The info auto-close in the story examples is 5s; the shipped `DEFAULT_TOAST_MS` is 8s. Tests use the shipped value.
- Task 3.10b says to commit the Story 1.5 walkthrough "unchanged"; no such script was ever committed (Story 1.5 recorded none). `notification-triage-walkthrough.spec.ts` is new and covers the burst-to-empty-deck leg only; the read-only-output leg waits for Epic 5.
- Task 3.10f is already reconciled: `CLAUDE.md` and `web-app/.jscpd.json` both say 0.14%. Only `scripts/check-jscpd-threshold-doc.sh` (validation T-JS-01) was added.
- TD-7 "a toast for the session in view is suppressed" had no client concept of the viewed session; `lib/utils/viewedSessions.ts` plus `SessionDetailView` now provide it.

**Deviations**
- `isActionable` was renamed `hasLongToastLifetime` (staleness only), per Task 3.2c's "renamed for its remaining meaning".
- `clearAll()` now keeps pinned toasts for every flag state (ADR-004 decision 9).
- Review-queue toasts built by `showSessionNotification` carry no `isPendingDecision` (it is a server field), so they are not pinned; they still expire by `hasLongToastLifetime`.
- The mocked-history e2e specs (`notifications-needs-decision`, `approval-*`, `accessibility`) now send `isPendingDecision`, and two specs that located toasts by `role="alert"` use the toast testid (the Announcer owns the only live roles).

#### Story 3.1: Extract `ToastStack` and the timer registry (refactor-first, no behavior change)
**As an** engineer, **I want** toast rendering and timers out of `NotificationContext.tsx`, **so that** the new behavior lands in a small unit.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Characterization tests lock current behavior first.
  - *Given* the existing `web-app/src/lib/contexts/__tests__/NotificationContext.test.tsx`, *When* `ToastStack` and `useToastTimers` are extracted, *Then* every existing test passes unmodified.
- Timers are cancellable.
  - *Given* `showActionToast("Saved","success","k1")` then `clearAll()`, *When* 5s of fake time elapse, *Then* no `setNotifications` call occurs after clear (registry cancelled the handle) and unmounting leaves 0 pending timers.
- One timer owner.
  - *Given* `NotificationToast.tsx`, *Then* it contains no `setTimeout`/timer `useEffect` (its auto-close/minimize effects at L99-135 moved into `ToastStack` via the registry) and a grep-test asserts it; pause/resume/cancel reach every toast through the registry.
- Context split and shrink.
  - *Given* `NotificationContext.tsx`, *Then* it is below its current 532 lines and its public surface is split into a state context and a command context, with toast queue logic in a pure `useToastQueue` reducer; `ToastStack` consumes only the queue and commands it renders (recount members by command; the plan baseline is ~25).

#### Story 3.2: One server-sent pending-decision field; stack policy
**As an** operator, **I want** exactly one definition of "pending decision", **so that** bulk actions, the stack and the server can never disagree (WARNING is the case that disagrees today).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Server sends the field.
  - *Given* a store with an unread WARNING, an unread APPROVAL_NEEDED, a read ERROR and an unread INFO, *When* `GetNotificationHistory` runs, *Then* `is_pending_decision` is true, true, false, false. The Go table test iterates every `sessionv1.NotificationType` and asserts `is_pending_decision == (IsActionableType(t) && !auto_remediating && !read)` (the single server predicate `IsPendingDecision`, Task 1.4d).
- **An auto-remediating WARNING is informational, not a decision** (triad UX "needs a decision" semantics; the operator's own screenshot toast, "PR needs attention ... An automated fix attempt will run on the standard backoff schedule", is a WARNING from `session/backlog_lifecycle_pr.go:1317` that needs no human action and would otherwise pin forever, inflate the count and be undismissable).
  - *Given* that WARNING stamped `auto_remediating=true` (Task 3.2d), *Then* its live event has `isPendingDecision` false, the toast auto-closes at the WARNING timeout (not pinned), its tray row is dismissible and sits in its session group (not in the "Needs attention" group), and it is excluded from the "N need attention" count and the handle's dot; *When* the automated fix fails and the producer publishes its escalation, *Then* that new event is an unstamped ERROR/FAILURE and pins. (Whether the producer already has an escalation notification is UNVERIFIED; Task 3.2d checks and, if there is none, adds one.) The tray group is named **"Needs attention"** (not "Needs a decision") because the pinned set includes errors and failures.
- Duplicate predicates are gone.
  - *Given* `web-app/src`, *Then* `ACTIONABLE_TYPES`/`isActionableNotification` (`notificationMapping.ts:124,132`), `isActionable` (`notification-policy.ts:29`) and any `isInformational` no longer decide deletion or bulk scope; their call sites (`NotificationsPage.tsx:154,158,167`, `NotificationPanel.tsx:252`, `notificationMapping.ts:147,175`) read `record.isPendingDecision`. `isActionable` is renamed for its remaining meaning (toast staleness, `ACTIONABLE_TOAST_STALE_MS`). A grep test fails if a new `ACTIONABLE`-style type set appears in `web-app/src/lib`. If a duplicate must survive, a parity test fed by a shared fixture (`pending-decision-types.json`) asserts it equals `IsActionableType` for every type.
- `isPinned` is presentational only.
  - *Given* toasts `[approval, info, info, error, info]` (newest last) and cap 3, *When* `partitionToasts` runs, *Then* `visible=[approval, error, newest-info]`, `overflow=2`. *Given* 4 pinned toasts and cap 3, *Then* `visible` has 3, `overflow=1`, `pinnedCount=4`. A test shows `isPinned` is never imported by the bulk-clear code path.
- Live toast and record agree by construction (architecture C6, adversarial N5).
  - *Given* a live unstamped WARNING `NotificationEvent` (the toast arrives before any history record exists), *Then* `event.isPendingDecision` is true (stamped server-side with `IsPendingDecision` in `server/services/event_converter.go:72`) and the toast is pinned; *Given* the matching history record merged later, *Then* the same value, so a toast bulk action never removes a toast whose record the server keeps. `isPinned(toast)` reads only that field (a grep/unit test fails if it lists notification types), so there is no fourth client predicate.
- The Notifications page call sites that this story rewires (`NotificationsPage.tsx:154,158,167`) also handle a `kept` list in a `ClearNotificationHistory` response; that behavior is specified once, in Story 4.4 (Task 4.4f), and shared by the tray and the page (validation G-5).
**Files**: `server/notifications/store.go`, `store_test.go`, `server/services/event_converter.go`, `web-app/src/lib/notification-policy.ts`, `web-app/src/lib/utils/notificationMapping.ts`, `web-app/src/app/notifications/NotificationsPage.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationPanel.tsx`, `web-app/src/lib/__tests__/notification-policy.test.ts`.

#### Story 3.3: Cap, "+N more" chip, single-container layout
**As an** operator, **I want** at most 3 toasts and a chip for the rest, **so that** the terminal stays visible.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Cap and chip.
  - *Given* 10 simultaneous non-routine toasts (e.g. 10 `error` for distinct sessions), *When* rendered with `notification_tray_v2` on, *Then* `getAllByTestId("toast")` has length 3 and the chip reads "+7 more" with `aria-label="7 more notifications, open tray"`; clicking it calls `togglePanel`.
- Cap per viewport (ADR-009): desktop 3; mobile portrait 1 card plus chip; landscape 1; keyboard open 0 (chip only).
  - *Given* 10 non-routine toasts at 390x844 portrait, *Then* 1 toast and a chip "+9 more" render (the desktop case above still shows 3 and "+7 more").
- Routine types never toast under v2.
  - *Given* a `TASK_COMPLETE` event for a visible session, *Then* it is added to history only (existing `HISTORY_ONLY_TYPES`) and the toast count is unchanged.
- One container, no `:nth-child` offsets.
  - *Given* `NotificationToast.css.ts`, *Then* it contains no `:nth-child` selector and the stack container is one flex column with `gap`.
- Connectivity (triad UX B3, one source: `selectConnectionState`, Glossary `ConnectivityState`).
  - *Given* the connection state is not `connected`, *Then* the deck shows no banner of its own and no misleading empty state (the existing `ConnectionIndicator` and the tray's offline banner, Story 4.3, carry the status), existing toasts stay, Approve and Deny are disabled with an accessible reason (`aria-disabled`, visible hint "Offline"), and the client-only controls (close or Move to tray, Move all to tray) stay enabled.
- Flag off renders the legacy list.
  - *Given* `notification_tray_v2` off and 10 toasts, *Then* all 10 render as today.
**Files**: `web-app/src/components/ui/ToastStack.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationToast.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationToast.css.ts`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationToast.stories.tsx`, `web-app/src/lib/contexts/FeatureFlagsContext.tsx`.

#### Story 3.4: One bulk control, "Move all to tray", that never clears or deletes anything
**As an** operator, **I want** one tap to get the toasts off the screen, **so that** I can reach the terminal without losing an approval or a history row.
**Why one control (triad UX)**: the earlier header slot flipped between "Dismiss all" and "Move all to tray (N)" depending on deck contents, a mode error, and "Dismiss all" was almost a dead path because routine types never toast (TD-7) so nearly every toast is pinned. The deck now has a single bulk semantic; dismissal applies only to one non-pinned toast at a time (the close button or a swipe). The tray keeps "Clear informational" and "Clear history" (Story 4.4), which are the only bulk deletions.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- One header control, one label.
  - *Given* the deck holds at least 2 toasts in total (visible plus overflow), *Then* a button `data-testid="toast-move-all-to-tray"` reads "Move all to tray (N)" with N the total, min-height 44px; on mobile portrait, where one toast is visible, it is a secondary button on the chip row. *Given* 1 or 0 toasts, *Then* it is absent (never a no-op button). The label never changes with deck contents.
  - *Given* toasts `[approval, error, info, info]`, *When* it is tapped, *Then* 0 toasts remain, all 4 history rows remain unread and visible in the tray, `MarkNotificationRead` and `ClearNotificationHistory` are called 0 times, and an undo control reads "Moved 4 to tray - Undo".
  - *Given* 10 pinned toasts (approval_needed/error), *When* it is tapped, *Then* 0 toasts remain, the tray still holds the 10 rows with `isPendingDecision` true and unread, and Undo restores the same 10 toasts in order (TB-8: from a deck of 10, one tap reaches an empty deck).
  - *Given* `moveAllToTray` is wired, *Then* a grep test shows it never calls the history-mutating commands and never imports the bulk-clear path (same guard as `isPinned`).
- Single-toast dismissal is the only dismissal on the deck.
  - *Given* an informational toast, *Then* its close button (or a swipe) dismisses that toast and its row stays in history (Story 3.8); *Given* a pinned toast, *Then* the same control and swipe move it to the tray, never delete it.
- Undo placement is specified for every form factor (triad UX).
  - *Given* a phone (deck cap 1, or 0 with the keyboard open), *When* Move all runs, *Then* the undo control **replaces the chip row in place** (same docked position, same >= 44px height, visible with the keyboard open) for the UndoWindow; *Given* desktop, *Then* the undo control occupies the deck's header slot; the same toasts return in order on Undo; tested at V1, V2 and V4 (TB-10). Undo is announced through the Announcer (Story 3.6). The window is the per-device UndoWindow (default 8s, paused while the control is hovered or focused; Task 4.4g), not a hard-coded 5s.
- `clearAll` can no longer clear pinned toasts. This is a deliberate flag-independent bug fix (the old behavior can remove an unread approval toast, violating #738); ADR-004 decision 9 states it.
  - *Given* a `question` toast and `clearAll()` called with `notification_tray_v2` off, *Then* the question toast remains.

#### Story 3.5: Timers pause on hover, focus and open tray
**As an** operator, **I want** a toast to stay while I read or swipe it, **so that** it does not vanish mid-gesture (WCAG 2.2.1).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Pause on hover/focus-within.
  - *Given* an `info`-class toast with 5s auto-close and fake timers, *When* pointer enters at 3s and leaves at 10s, *Then* the toast is still present at 10s and removed after 2s more (remaining time preserved).
- Pause while the tray is open; pinned toasts never auto-expire.
  - *Given* `isPanelOpen=true`, *Then* no auto-dismiss fires; *Given* an `approval_needed` toast, *Then* it is still present after 10 minutes of fake time.
- A pinned card on a phone cannot cover the first terminal lines indefinitely (triad UX).
  - *Given* a pinned toast on mobile portrait with no interaction, *When* `PINNED_COLLAPSE_MS` (a named constant, default 8s, also the default of the per-device setting 8s / 15s / 30s / Never, Task 4.4h; an approval card with Approve or Deny in flight or its confirm step showing never collapses, TC-14) elapses, *Then* the card collapses to a one-line 44px chip ("1 needs you - <title>") in the same dock position; the notification stays pinned and unread in the tray; tapping the chip expands the card and it then stays expanded until the user collapses or acts (no collapse loop); the timer pauses on touch, hover and focus; with the keyboard open the deck is already a chip; the compact card is at most 96px tall; desktop cards never auto-collapse (TD-14).
**Files**: `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useToastTimers.ts`, `web-app/src/components/ui/ToastStack.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/__tests__/ToastStack.test.tsx`. (`NotificationToast.tsx` is presentational after Story 3.1.)

##### Task 3.5a: Failing pause/resume tests
- Files: `web-app/src/lib/hooks/__tests__/useToastTimers.test.ts`.

##### Task 3.5b: Wire pointer/focus handlers on the stack's per-toast wrapper and panel-open pause through the registry
- Files: `web-app/src/components/ui/ToastStack.tsx`, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useToastTimers.ts`.

The fallback if DV-1 shows 8s is too short to read and act on an approval is changing that default to 15s, a one-line change with no other effect. Tests: the default, each setting value, Never, storage-throws, the in-flight and confirm exemption (TD-14, TC-14).

#### Story 3.6: Accessible live regions: one Announcer owns every notification announcement
**As a** screen-reader user, **I want** new notifications, banners and receipts announced once, in priority order, without each surface fighting for the live region, **so that** a burst is intelligible (WCAG 4.1.3).
**Why an owner (triad UX B1)**: the earlier text asked for exactly one persistent `role=status` region (TD-8) while RO-8, RP-4, TM-10, the tray announcements and the pinned toasts each defined their own `role=status` or `role=alert`. One provider now owns the live regions and every surface calls `announce(message, politeness, key?)`. This story lands before any story adds a live role or status line (Stories 4.4, 5.3, 5.6 call it). It builds on the existing `web-app/src/components/ui/LiveRegion.tsx` primitive (which already takes a `role`), not on a new mechanism.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- One owner and no stray live roles.
  - *Given* the toast deck, tray, Background section, read-only banner mounted (and the Reply card once Story 5.6 has shipped; its own announcer cases are asserted by Task 5.6f), *Then* exactly one `AnnouncerProvider` renders exactly one always-mounted polite region (`role="status"`, `data-testid="announcer-polite"`, mounted even at 0 toasts) and one always-mounted assertive region (`role="alert"`, `data-testid="announcer-assertive"`); no toast card, deck, tray, banner, receipt or kept-line renders its own `role="status"` or `role="alert"`, and no element has both a live role and `aria-live="polite"`. Unrelated pre-existing `LiveRegion` users (`ConnectionIndicator.tsx`, `InputDropBadge.tsx`, tables) are out of scope and unchanged; the test scopes to the notification surfaces by testid.
- **Coalescing versus `role=alert`, decided: coalescing wins inside a 500ms window per channel, and the assertive channel is reserved for the single highest-priority pinned item.** Reason: five assertive interruptions in one burst make the burst unintelligible, while a lone decision is time-sensitive and must interrupt.
  - *Given* 5 informational toasts within 500ms, *Then* the polite region says "5 new notifications" once. *Given* one pinned toast alone, *Then* the assertive region announces its title once. *Given* 5 pinned toasts within 500ms, *Then* one assertive message "5 notifications need attention, newest: <title>". *Given* 1 pinned and 3 informational in the same window, *Then* one assertive message "4 new notifications, 1 needs attention" and no separate polite message.
- Priority queue.
  - *Given* a queued polite message and a new assertive one, *Then* the assertive one is announced first; the polite queue holds at most 5 messages, deduped by `key`, oldest dropped; a message is cleared from the region after it has been announced so an identical later message is announced again.
- Surfaces use the API, not their own regions.
  - *Given* the read-only banner mount (polite "Background session - read-only"), the Reply receipt (polite) and a Reply error (assertive) (both asserted by Task 5.6f once Story 5.6 ships, not by Story 3.6), the "N kept: still needs attention" line (polite), "N marked read, 1 still needs attention" (polite) and the deck's undo (polite), *Then* each is produced by `announce()`; the visible element carries the same text with no live role.
- Axe passes on the stack story.
  - *Given* `NotificationToast.stories.tsx` rendered in the a11y sweep (`tests/e2e/a11y-route-sweep.spec.ts` pattern), *Then* 0 WCAG AA violations.
**Files**: `web-app/src/components/ui/Announcer.tsx`, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useAnnounce.ts`, `web-app/src/components/ui/LiveRegion.tsx` (reuse), `web-app/src/components/ui/ToastStack.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationToast.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/__tests__/Announcer.test.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/__tests__/ToastStack.test.tsx`.

- Files: `web-app/src/components/ui/__tests__/Announcer.test.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/__tests__/ToastStack.test.tsx`.
