#### Story 3.7: Mobile and keyboard positioning (top dock on mobile session pages, ADR-009)
**As a** mobile operator, **I want** toasts to stay clear of the terminal input, page-keys row and soft keyboard, **so that** they never cover what I am typing.
**Overlay, not layout (triad UX)**: the mobile stack is `position: fixed` and takes no layout space; it **covers** whatever is under it. On the Terminal tab that is the first terminal lines and the "Connected / Redraw / Hist" status row; on the Diff, VCS, Files, Logs and Info tabs it covers the top of that tab's content, with the same cap of one card. The wireframe in `design/ux.md` Surface 1 therefore shows the status row overlaid while a toast is up; DV-1 records whether that row is needed while a toast is showing, and the pinned auto-collapse (Story 3.5, TD-14) bounds how long a card covers it.
**Placement (operator decision, ADR-009)**: mobile session pages dock the stack at the TOP, directly under the pane tab row and below the memory banner; desktop (>= 900px) stays bottom-right; the bottom-offset math below applies only to desktop and to mobile pages that have no terminal. Fallback to a bottom dock above the page-keys row happens only if device check DV-1 fails.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Published top offset (C3).
  - *Given* a mobile session page with the memory banner shown, dismissed, and wrapped to two lines, *When* the layout settles, *Then* `--mobile-stack-top-offset` equals the tab row's `getBoundingClientRect().bottom` within 1px in all three cases (measured by a `ResizeObserver` publisher beside `--mobile-pane-tab-strip-height`, `PaneSplitRenderer.tsx:442-449`), and the stack top is `max(var(--mobile-stack-top-offset), env(safe-area-inset-top)) + 8px`.
- Portrait, keyboard closed (390x844): at most 1 compact toast (<= 96px) plus a 44px "+N" chip.
  - *Given* 3 toasts, *Then* the stack's top is >= the tab row bottom and below the memory banner, and its bounding box does not intersect the terminal input row, the page-keys row or the connection status pill (Playwright bounding boxes; TD-2).
- Keyboard open collapses to a 1-line top chip.
  - *Given* `isVirtualKeyboardOpen=true` and 3 toasts, *Then* no toast card renders, a single-line chip of >= 44px reading "3 notifications" (plus " - N needs you" when pinned exist) is docked at the top anchor, the xterm textarea keeps focus (no blur) and no terminal resize is sent, and pinned toasts still use `role="alert"` when they are in the DOM before collapse (TD-3).
- Landscape (844x390): chip or 1 compact card, stack `max-height` `calc(var(--viewport-height) * 0.4)` scrolling internally; the rest fold into the chip (TD-4); the chip or card's close control is visible without scrolling (TL-2). This story owns TD-1..TD-4, TD-12, TD-13 and TL-2 (ux.md Surfaces 1 and 8); the tray-side landscape criteria are owned by Story 4.2.
- Desktop (>= 900px) and mobile pages with no terminal: bottom offset clears nav, tab strip and safe area.
  - *Given* a no-terminal mobile page with `--bottom-nav-height: 72px`, `--mobile-pane-tab-strip-height: 0px`, `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` 0, viewport 390x844, *Then* the stack's bottom is at least 72px above the viewport bottom; *Given* desktop 1280x800, *Then* the deck is bottom-right, 360px wide, max 3 visible, and sits >= 16px left of an open tray (TD-12).
- Desktop overlap guard (triad UX; TD-15).
  - *Given* 1280x800 with the xterm cursor on the last row inside the right 360px (where Claude Code prints its input line), *When* the bottom-right deck is shown, *Then* the deck's bounding box intersects neither the cursor cell rectangle nor the xterm scrollbar (the deck's right offset is the scrollbar width plus 16px); if the deck would cover the cursor cell it switches to a top-right anchor (same width, below the header) until the cursor moves away; if the cursor rectangle cannot be read, a fixed top-right anchor is used (decided in Task 3.7f).
- Device verification gates enabling the flag, not the merge (adversarial N8).
  - *Given* PR 3 merged with `notification_tray_v2` default off, *Then* the operator (the only person with the Android and iOS devices) records ADR-009 DV-1..DV-9 results and screenshots in the PR 3 thread before turning the flag on; DV-1 failing switches to the documented bottom-dock fallback (a CSS/variant change) before the flag is enabled. Agents cannot run these checks, so no PR 3 task blocks on them.
**Files**: `web-app/src/components/ui/ToastStack.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationToast.css.ts` (bottom rule at L33 becomes desktop/no-terminal only), `web-app/src/components/pane/PaneSplitRenderer.tsx` (publish the new var), `web-app/src/components/providers/ViewportProvider.tsx` (read), `tests/e2e/toast-stack-mobile.spec.ts`.

#### Story 3.8: Swipe-to-dismiss hook
**As a** touch user, **I want** to swipe a toast away, **so that** dismissal is one gesture, with a button as the non-gesture path.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Horizontal swipe past threshold dismisses; vertical movement cancels.
  - *Given* an `info` toast row, *When* a touch moves 90px right within 200ms with <10px vertical drift, *Then* `onDismiss` fires once; *When* it moves 90px right but 60px down, *Then* it does not fire.
- Pinned toasts swipe to tray (demote), never dismiss.
  - *Given* an `approval_needed` toast, *When* swiped, *Then* the toast leaves the deck, the history row remains unread and visible in the tray.
- Does not leak to the terminal.
  - *Given* a document-level `touchmove` spy (stand-in for `useTerminalGestures`), *When* a swipe starts on a toast row, *Then* the spy is not called (row calls `stopPropagation`), or the spike recorded why it is unnecessary.
- Button alternative present.
  - *Given* any toast, *Then* a visible close button of at least 44x44 exists and is keyboard reachable; a pinned toast shows a **tray icon plus the visible text "Tray"** (>= 44x44, `aria-label` "Move to tray") instead of an x, so a touch user can see the control is not a dismissal; an informational toast keeps the x (`aria-label` "Dismiss notification"); `Delete`/`Backspace` on a focused card performs the same action.
- In-drag feedback distinguishes the two outcomes (triad UX).
  - *Given* an informational toast dragged past 24px, *Then* a reveal behind it shows an icon and the text "Dismiss"; *Given* a pinned toast, *Then* the reveal shows the tray icon and "Move to tray"; releasing before 40% of the card width springs back and nothing happens; the reveal text meets 4.5:1 (TC-11).
- "Focus Window" is not offered on touch (C13).
  - *Given* `pointer: coarse` (390px), *Then* a toast shows at most 2 action buttons and none is "Focus Window"; *Given* a desktop pointer and a toast with more than 2 actions, *Then* "Focus Window" lives in the card's "..." overflow.
- Phone approval card (triad repair 1, G1; `design/ux.md` D11, TC-12 to TC-14).
  - *Given* an `approval_needed` toast at 390x844, *Then* Approve and Deny are both visible, each >= 44px high with >= 24px between their hit areas (stacked, Deny first, when the card is under 360px), neither in the overflow, exempt from the two-action cap ("View output" or "View Session" moves into the overflow), the command text wraps (4 lines, no one-line ellipsis) with a >= 44px "Show full command" disclosure that shows all of a 600-character command; *When* Approve or Deny is tapped, *Then* both disable at once ("Approving..." or "Denying..."), collapse and swipe are blocked, a double tap within 300ms sends one RPC with one idempotency key, and a failure shows "Could not approve - Retry" with both buttons re-enabled; *Given* a `high` or `critical` (or unrecorded) risk level from the same classifier `ApprovalCard` shows (`SeverityBadge`, `web-app/src/lib/sessions/riskLevel`), *Then* the first Approve tap opens the inline "Approve this command?" confirm and sends 0 RPCs until "Confirm approve"; `low` and `medium` approve in one tap; there is no Undo for an approval.
- Reduced motion.
  - *Given* `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, *Then* no fling animation runs.
- **Sequencing note (no cut line, operator decision O8)**: until this story merges, toasts keep the 44px close / "Move to tray" button and `Delete`/`Backspace` (the non-gesture paths already required here), and Story 4.3's swipe rows (Task 4.3c) use per-row buttons first and gain the gesture when this hook merges. Nothing else depends on the hook.
**Files**: `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useSwipeToDismiss.ts`, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/__tests__/useSwipeToDismiss.test.ts`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationToast.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationToast.css.ts`.

#### Story 3.9: Cross-tab bulk dismissal
**As an** operator with two tabs open, **I want** a bulk action in one tab ("Move all to tray" on the deck, "Clear informational" in the tray) to update the same toasts and rows in the other, **so that** state agrees.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Id-set message is idempotent and exact.
  - *Given* tab B holds toasts `a,b,c`, *When* tab A broadcasts `NOTIFICATIONS_BULK_DISMISSED {ids:["a","b"]}`, *Then* tab B keeps only `c`; replaying the same message changes nothing; a toast `d` that arrived in B after the click is unaffected.
- "Moved to tray" is a separate kind and never touches history.
  - *Given* tab A broadcasts `{kind:"moved", ids:["a"]}`, *Then* tab B drops toast `a` from its deck but its history row `a` is unchanged and still unread.
- Sender does not receive its own message; fallback when `BroadcastChannel` is missing.
  - *Given* `BroadcastChannel` undefined, *Then* the `storage` event path delivers the same message.
- **Sequencing note (no cut line, operator decision O8)**: Story 4.4's "other tab drops exactly the cleared ids" and Task 3.4e's `moved` cross-tab message depend on this story; until it merges they fall back to the shipped per-id `NOTIFICATION_DISMISSED` broadcast (one message per cleared id; `NotificationContext.tsx`, ADR-008 context) or the next history refresh. Single-tab behavior, undo and the server `ClearByIDs` guard are unaffected.
**Files**: `web-app/src/lib/contexts/NotificationContext.tsx` (message union), the sync-channel module used at `createNotificationSyncChannel`, `web-app/src/lib/contexts/__tests__/NotificationContext.test.tsx`.

#### Story 3.10: Flag, e2e, registry, docs for the toast stack
**As an** operator, **I want** the new stack behind a live flag with end-to-end coverage, **so that** I can roll it out and back safely.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Flag registered, default off until the device checks are recorded, and toggles behavior live.

### Epic 4: Notification tray (PR 4, `feat(web):`, behind `notification_tray_v2`, stacked on PR 3)
**Goal**: Triage and bulk-manage notifications from a non-modal side tray reachable on desktop and mobile without remounting or resizing the terminal.

#### Implementation status: PR 4 (recorded after the build; the story text below is unchanged)

Stories 4.1 to 4.5 and Tasks 4.2a-4.2e, 4.2g, 4.2i, 4.3a-4.3g, 4.4a-4.4h and 4.5a-4.5d are implemented behind `notification_tray_v2` (default off; flag off keeps the legacy modal panel and the uncapped toast list unchanged). Task 3.2d (stamp the auto-remediating WARNING) is done as a small change: an optional `session.AutoRemediatingNotifier` extension implemented by `EventBusNotifier`, so the `Notifier` interface did not change. `ClearByIDs` replaces the PR 1c `CodeUnimplemented` guard.

**AWAITING OPERATOR (not run, not faked)**
- [ ] **Spike 1.2** (Radix non-modal versus xterm focus; the `useTerminalGestures` document-listener audit). Built on the documented default: a hand-rolled `role="complementary"` tray with explicit capture and restore of `document.activeElement` (`useTrayFocus.ts`), no Radix. Tasks 4.1b, 4.2g and 4.2e are implemented against that default and unit and Playwright tested; a real Android Chrome or iOS Safari run decides whether Radix would change anything and whether the default holds. Steps: DV-1..DV-9 in ADR-009 on a real phone with the soft keyboard (open the tray by tap with the keyboard up, Expand, close; the keyboard must stay up and the page must not jump).
- [ ] **Task 4.2h (C14, TY-10), real session at 1280x800.** Open a session with output in the last columns, then: (1) does the 44px handle band at 35% from the top hide needed text; (2) can the xterm scrollbar thumb still be grabbed under the band; (3) does the bottom-right deck ever cover the cursor row (the deck now sits 60px from the right edge, clear of the handle). O6 is decided (fixed handle): if (1) or (2) fails, stop and report; do not switch to a draggable handle.
- [ ] **Task 4.2f hotkey chord spike.** Repository half done (VERIFIED by reading): the shortcut registry is one bubble-phase `document` keydown listener (`web-app/src/lib/shortcuts/shortcutRegistry.ts:88-112`), `Alt+W` is already the window leader (`lib/window/useWindowShortcuts.ts:198`; its follow-up `n` is "next window" only while armed), and xterm's custom key handler only intercepts Ctrl/Cmd+C and Ctrl/Cmd+A (`XtermTerminal.tsx:776-829`), so every Alt chord reaches the PTY unless a capture-phase listener stops it. Not done and needed: a byte probe on macOS Safari, macOS Chrome and Linux Chrome (Option+N is a dead key on macOS, so match `event.code`, not `event.key`), plus the browser's own reserved chords. **No hotkey ships**; TH-3 and TY-4's hotkey half record "no safe chord" until the probe is run. The `how-to/manage-notifications.md` Hotkey section says so.
- [ ] **Device checks for the tray** (extend Task 3.7d): TS-2 and TK-1 on a real soft keyboard (the Playwright run drives it through the `visualViewport` height only), TS-8 hardware Back on Android, DV-7 screen readers on the tray, and MC-1 contrast of the handle, entry chip and tray over the xterm canvas (Axe passes on the DOM; it cannot see canvas pixels).
- [ ] **Operator review of the "What changed" copy and the phone landscape layout.** At 844x390 the app chrome leaves the terminal about one row (pre-existing; `cols` reads 3 in the DOM renderer), so TL-3's "terminal unchanged" is asserted on cols and rows only.

**Plan assumptions found wrong while building**
- Task 4.4b says the `kept` list needs only the request field from Story 1.4. `ClearNotificationHistoryResponse` had no field for it; `repeated string kept_ids = 3` was added (additive proto).
- TY-11 gives tray left edges of 540, 600 and 660px at 900, 1000 and 1100px. `min(400px, 40vw)` gives 540, 600 and **700**; the spec asserts the formula.
- The handle count of "99+" at 120 unread could not hold: the context counted unread groups in the one loaded page (50 rows) and showed 49. The count now floors at the server's unread total (`history.unreadCount`).
- ADR-006 and the plan say the tray uses `zIndex.slideOver`. That token (700) is below the BottomNav (1050) and the toast deck (1080). The bottom sheet therefore sits above the BottomNav by offsetting its `bottom`, and the deck steps aside (`visibility: hidden`, node still mounted) while the tray is open, because a deck above a 25%-85% sheet covered its header and close button.
- The desktop deck's old 16px right inset put its cards over the 44px handle; it is now 60px (and `DESKTOP_DECK.inset.right` matches, so the cursor-overlap guard measures the same footprint).
- Axe `color-contrast` failed on the toast type label and "View Session" (white on `#f44336` 3.68:1), the tray type label and the unread badge (3.76:1). All four get a darkening gradient layer; the toast labels are Epic 3 CSS, fixed here because the matrix spec now guards them.
- A 25% peek sheet is 211px at 844 and 160px at 640: the header, search and filters used all of it and the virtualizer rendered 0 rows. The search bar, filters and the settings and What-changed cards now scroll with the list (the virtualizer takes their height as `scrollMargin`), the peek height has a 220px floor and the footer link shows only when expanded.
- `useClearResultLine` (plan: `useNotificationHistory.ts`) lives in its own module so the Notifications page and its test do not pull in the ConnectRPC transport.
- `useCoarsePointer` already existed from PR 3 and is reused; the plan's "track pointer type" needed only the keydown/pointerdown input-method tracker.
- Task 4.2i says the entry chip is rendered by `ToastStack`'s dock; that holds, but on a phone session page the dock is now never null (it renders the bell with no toasts), and the floating no-terminal case reuses the bottom dock rather than a separate floating element.

**Deviations**
- Stories 4.1-4.4's UI landed as one integrated commit (the panel file serves all four); the hooks, the Go change, Task 3.2d, Quiet mode/Pin/variants and the e2e specs are separate commits.
- The BottomNav "More" row (Task 4.2d file list) was not added; a phone page with no terminal shows the one entry chip in the bottom dock.
- The `top-sheet` (keyboard open) and `landscape-panel` variants and Pin tray are built to the plan's defaults, ahead of Spike 1.2. The plan's "no `top-sheet` until Task 4.2e" is therefore superseded: with the keyboard open the phone gets the top-anchored sheet, not a capped bottom sheet.
- Row dismiss, swipe and `x` go through the undo window and `ClearNotificationHistory{notification_ids}` (the shipped `removeFromHistory` was local-only). Session-group headers carry "Dismiss N informational".
- XA-16 asserts bare controls and the handle border, not a focus-ring contrast; XA-17 asserts no clipped controls and a reachable last row, not 200% zoom. The "Q" quick control the What-changed card mentions in ux.md was not built; the card names Quiet mode only.
- `@axe-core/playwright` was not installed under `tests/e2e` when the matrix spec was written; it was run with the package installed in a scratch directory. Run `cd tests/e2e && npm install && npm test` once in CI with the real toolchain.

#### Story 4.1: Non-modal, layout-neutral conversion (invariants first)
**As an** operator, **I want** opening the tray not to touch my terminal, **so that** scrollback, focus and size survive.
**Acceptance Criteria** (e2e written first, red against the current modal panel):
- Terminal untouched.
  - *Given* a session page with an xterm showing 80x24 and scrollback marker text, *When* the tray opens then closes, *Then* `cols/rows` equal before and after, the terminal root element is the same DOM node (`elementHandle` identity / a `data-instance-id` marker unchanged), the marker text remains, and `document.body.style.overflow` is unchanged.
- Focus rules by input method (D6, C11, triad UX B2). Restoring focus to the xterm textarea with `.focus()` re-summons the soft keyboard on a phone and votes a terminal resize, so it is restricted:
  - *Pointer/touch open never moves focus.* *Given* the xterm textarea focused and the tray opened by pointer or touch, *Then* `document.activeElement` is still the textarea and no `blur` fired.
  - *Coarse-pointer close never moves focus into xterm when the keyboard was closed.* *Given* `pointer: coarse` and the keyboard closed (the textarea was not focused) when the tray opened, *When* it is closed by tap, Back or the handle, *Then* no `.focus()` is called on the textarea and the soft keyboard stays closed. *Given* the keyboard was open at open time, *Then* the textarea kept focus throughout (nothing blurred it), so the keyboard stays open and no resize message is sent.
  - *Keyboard-initiated open and close restore focus.* *Given* the tray opened by keyboard (or the hotkey), *Then* focus moves to the `#notification-tray` heading, and a keyboard-initiated close (Esc, hotkey) returns focus to the stored element, the xterm textarea when that was the opener. This and the desktop fine-pointer Esc-from-inside-the-tray path are the only callers of `.focus()` on the textarea.
  - *Given* a fine pointer, the xterm textarea focused, the tray opened by the handle and closed by Esc with focus inside it, *Then* `document.activeElement` is the xterm textarea (TY-2).
- Not modal.
  - *Given* tray open on desktop, *Then* the tray element has no `aria-modal`, there is no backdrop element, and the terminal still accepts typed input.
- z-index tokens.
  - *Given* `NotificationPanel.css.ts`, *Then* no numeric 9998/9999 literal remains; it uses `zIndex.slideOver`.
**Files**: `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationPanel.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationPanel.css.ts`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationPanel.test.tsx`, `tests/e2e/notification-tray-terminal-invariants.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/pages/NotificationPanel.ts`.

- Files: `tests/e2e/notification-tray-terminal-invariants.spec.ts`.

- Radix `modal={false}` or hand-rolled per Spike 1.2 (ADR-006).
- Files: `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationPanel.tsx`.

##### Task 4.1c: Token z-index; confirm only `transform` animates; reduced-motion
- Files: `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationPanel.css.ts`.

#### Story 4.2: Handle, chip and variants (the hotkey and the extra variants are sequenced last in this story)
**As an** operator, **I want** a persistent entry point that fits desktop and phone, **so that** I can reach the tray from a terminal page.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Desktop edge handle with capped count.
  - *Given* viewport 1280x800 and 120 unread, *Then* `data-testid="tray-handle"` is visible at the right edge, shows "99+", has `aria-expanded="false"` and `aria-controls="notification-tray"`, and meets contrast (Axe).
- Mobile portrait chip; bottom sheet with peek/expanded; soft keyboard handled by height.
  - *Given* viewport 390x844 on a session page (no header bell), keyboard closed, *Then* the `TrayEntryChip` (ux.md D10: a 44x44 bell-and-count button at the top anchor when the deck has no overflow) is visible and reachable and covers neither the page-keys row nor the input line (bounding-box assertion, TH-2); there is no floating bottom-right chip on a session page; tapping opens a **peek** sheet (22-28% of `--viewport-height`, TS-1); tapping "Expand" (>= 44px) or dragging the grabber shows 84-86%. *Given* `isVirtualKeyboardOpen=true`, *Then* the same `TrayEntryChip` element becomes the 1-line top chip (Story 3.7), and the sheet is capped to `--viewport-height` with its bottom edge at `var(--keyboard-height)` so the action row is not covered (TK-2; no separate variant until Task 4.2e adds the top-anchored one). On a phone page with no terminal the entry is the floating chip bottom-right (16px above `--bottom-nav-height`, or above the safe-area inset with no BottomNav) plus the BottomNav "More" row.
- Landscape and variant switches (ux.md Surfaces 7 and 8: the capped bottom sheet serves until Task 4.2e lands).
  - *Given* 844x390 (below 900px), *Then* the capped bottom sheet renders (no `landscape-panel` until Task 4.2e); opening and closing it does not reflow or resize the terminal and its controls are >= 44px with an internally scrolling list (TL-3, TL-4). `landscape-panel` width and safe-area criterion TL-1 apply when Task 4.2e lands; TL-2 (toast landscape cap, visible close) is owned by Story 3.7.
  - *Given* a rotation, a keyboard dismiss or a width change across 900px while the tray is open, *Then* the variant switches without losing scroll position, search text or collapse state (TK-5, Task 4.2b/4.2g), and closing restores focus to the xterm textarea only if the tray was the thing that took focus (TK-4, Task 4.2g).
- Mobile sheets never take focus on pointer open (D6, C11).
  - *Given* the keyboard open and the xterm textarea focused, *When* the chip or "+N more" opens the sheet, *Then* the textarea keeps focus (no `blur`, keyboard stays up, no resize sent: TK-1). The search field is never auto-focused on touch (TK-3); closing restores focus only if the tray had taken it (TK-4).
- Gesture ownership (C12).
  - *Given* the bottom sheet, *Then* only the grabber drags the sheet (list content never drags it: dragging a row 80px does not move the sheet, TS-3); side overlays and the landscape panel have no swipe-to-close (they close via x, handle, Esc or hotkey); no edge-drag-to-open exists (touchstart within 30px of the edge does not open the tray, TH-6); row swipe regions are the row surface only (Story 4.3).
- Expanded sheet, by pointer type (triad UX B2). On `pointer: coarse` the expanded sheet is **non-trapping**: no focus trap, no `inert`, no `aria-modal`, no focus move on open (the strip above it stays tappable); Back and a hardware Esc close it; closing by tap or Back never moves focus into xterm when the keyboard was closed at open, and when the keyboard was open at open time the textarea keeps focus through Expand and close (no blur, keyboard stays up, no resize vote). On `pointer: fine` (a narrow desktop window) the expanded sheet is modal with a focus trap, `inert` background and a defined trap exit: Esc, the close button, a scrim click or Back each close it and focus returns to the opener (the xterm textarea only when it was focused at open). Peek is non-modal everywhere (TS-4, TS-5, TS-8).
- Keyboard-open Expand (TS-9).
  - *Given* V4 (keyboard var set) and the sheet open at Peek, *When* Expand is tapped, *Then* the textarea keeps focus, no `blur` fires, 0 resize messages are sent and the sheet grows only to the cap (`--viewport-height`, bottom edge at `var(--keyboard-height)`). Real soft-keyboard behavior is the DV-3 device check; the CSS-var path is what Playwright proves.
- Header bell is an `aria-expanded` toggle; handle hidden when the flag is off.
- One stable phone tray entry, never relocated, never zero (adversarial M6; triad UX; rewritten in triad repair 1, G2, because the earlier three-element hand-off between a floating chip, a "+N more" chip and a keyboard chip flickered and could race).
  - *Given* 390x844 on a session page, *Then* exactly one tray entry exists in the DOM and is visible in every state: the single `TrayEntryChip` (`data-testid="tray-entry"`), one mounted node docked at the top anchor (right-aligned, the same anchor as the toast dock) whose content changes in place: a 44x44 bell-and-count button with no overflow (**including with exactly 1 toast and 0 overflow**), the "+N more" row (>= 44px) while the deck has overflow, and the 1-line chip while the keyboard is open and notifications exist; it is never unmounted, never remounted and never moved more than 1px between states (stable React key; a `MutationObserver` records 0 removals and 0 insertions of the entry across the TH-8 transition table, TH-9), so no frame shows two entries or none and there is no relocation race. `trayAffordanceVariant(overflowCount, toastCount, keyboardOpen, hasTerminal)` is a pure function that returns the entry's **content** (`bell`, `more-row`, `keyboard-chip`) or, for a page with no terminal, `floating-bottom`; it never returns a position change on a session page. The accessible name follows the content ("Notifications, 7 unread" or "6 more notifications, open tray"); all states open the same tray. Desktop is unaffected (handle plus bell). TH-2, TH-7, TH-8, TH-9.
**Late-sequenced within this story (in scope, operator decision O8; each keeps its dependencies and gating spike)**: Task 4.2e `top-sheet` and `landscape-panel` variants (keyboard-open top-anchored sheet; landscape width `min(360px, 50vw)` honoring `env(safe-area-inset-left/right)`; after Task 4.2g and Spike 1.2); Task 4.2f hotkey (C4: `Alt+N` is a **candidate only and is NOT verified free**; on macOS `Option+N` is a dead-key that types a character, and in Linux/Windows terminals `Alt+N` is sent to the shell as `ESC n` (readline/emacs word-motion-style sequences), so xterm would normally receive it. **Spike first**: grep the shortcut registry, probe the xterm key path with a byte probe on macOS Safari/Chrome and Linux Chrome, and pick a chord from the result; if no chord is safe the hotkey is not shipped and TH-3/TY-4's hotkey half record "no safe chord" as a finding. Capture-phase `keydown` with an IME guard and a shortcut-dialog entry; AC: with xterm focused the tray toggles and the terminal receives 0 input bytes). Until the hotkey lands, the handle, header bell, chip and "+N more" are the entry points.
**Files**: `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationPanel.tsx`, `NotificationPanel.css.ts`, `TrayHandle.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/layout/Header.tsx`, `BottomNav.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/providers/ViewportProvider.tsx`, `tests/e2e/notification-tray-variants.spec.ts`.

#### Story 4.3: Per-session grouping, virtualization, swipe rows
**As an** operator with hundreds of rows, **I want** grouped, virtualized rows I can swipe, **so that** the tray stays fast and triage is quick.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Virtualized list.
  - *Given* 500 history rows, *When* the tray opens, *Then* fewer than 60 row nodes are in the DOM and opening does not issue a synchronous full-history fetch (network spy shows 0 `GetNotificationHistory` calls beyond the initial hydrated slice; next page loads on scroll via `loadMoreHistory`).
- Pinned "Needs attention" group (C9, D4, ADR-008; renamed from "Needs a decision" in triad iteration 1 because the set includes errors and failures, not only approvals). Above all per-session groups, a group headed "NEEDS ATTENTION (N)" holds every row with `is_pending_decision` (never an auto-remediating WARNING, Story 3.2), with actionable buttons (Approve/Deny/Open), no dismiss control and no swipe action while unread; the tray header leads with "N need attention" (hidden at 0; when 0 need attention and other rows exist the group shows the one line "Nothing needs attention"). The group is excluded from virtualized-group collapse and from every bulk action.
  - *Given* 3 unread rows with `is_pending_decision` among 40 rows from 5 sessions, *Then* the first rendered group is "NEEDS ATTENTION (3)", those rows expose no dismiss button and no swipe (TR-2), they also appear nowhere else in the per-session groups, and the header reads "3 need attention" (the count equals the handle's dot number). *Given* an unread WARNING stamped `auto_remediating=true`, *Then* it is in its session group, dismissible, and in neither count (TR-11).
  - *Given* the user resolves a decision (Approve), *Then* the row leaves the pinned group and appears in its session group as read.
- Collapsible per-session groups from `groupNotifications()`; per-viewer collapse state, not synced.
  - *Given* session `s1` with 12 rows, *Then* the group header shows the count and collapses via `Collapsible.tsx`; a second tab is unaffected.
- Swipe regions (C12): swipe lives on the row surface only; not the sheet grabber, the terminal, the tab strip or within 30px of the screen edge; `touch-action: pan-y`; every swipe has a >= 44px dismiss button.
- Swipe row dismiss reuses `useSwipeToDismiss`.
  - *Given* an informational row, *When* swiped 90px, *Then* it is dismissed; a row whose group contains a row with `is_pending_decision` refuses group dismissal and offers "Dismiss N informational".
- Rows do not reorder under the user's finger while open.
  - *Given* the tray open and a duplicate arriving for a visible group, *Then* the group count increments in place and a "N new" affordance appears only if order would change.
- Keyboard: roving tabindex, arrows move, `Enter` opens, `x` dismisses.
- Empty, loading, error and offline states are **owned by Tasks 4.3e-4.3g** (triad UX B3; the earlier "by reference, no task" gap is closed): one connectivity source, a tray state machine and one offline policy, in the three bullets below.
- One connectivity source (decided and justified).
  - *Given* `selectConnectionState` (`web-app/src/lib/store/sessionsSlice`, the store behind `ConnectionIndicator.tsx:5-6`, VERIFIED) is `stale` or `disconnected`, *Then* the tray shows the banner "Offline - showing cached, updated <time>" derived from that same store (not a second connectivity implementation, and consistent with the existing indicator the operator already sees); *Given* it is `connected` and `GetNotificationHistory` fails, *Then* the tray shows "Could not load notifications." with Retry and cached rows under "Showing cached - updated <time>" (a load error, not offline). The two conditions have different remedies (reconnect versus retry), which is why they are separate states and why a WebSocket-down signal and `historyError` are not merged into one flag. "All caught up" shows only when connected, the last fetch succeeded and there are 0 rows (TE-1, TE-7).
- One offline policy: server-mutating actions are disabled offline; nothing is queued.
  - *Given* offline, *Then* every server-mutating control (row Dismiss and swipe-dismiss, Mark activity read, Clear informational, Clear history, Approve, Deny, and Reply once Story 5.6 ships) is `aria-disabled` with the visible reason "Offline"; there is **no offline queue and no "pending sync" state**, which resolves the conflict with TM-9 ("never silently queue destructive operations") and "dismiss is permanent"; client-only controls (collapse, search, filters, the deck's Move to tray) stay enabled; on reconnect the controls re-enable and the banner clears (TE-2, TE-4).
- Every state has an exit.
  - *Given* the states initial loading, empty healthy, empty filtered, needs-attention empty, load error, offline, load more and bulk RPC failure, *Then* each shows at least one visible control or exits automatically (TE-6), and loading skeletons use `aria-busy` without announcing each row (TE-5).
- ux.md Surface 14
- **Sequencing note (no cut line, operator decision O8)**: rows keep the 44px dismiss button and `x` key until Story 3.8 merges (then they gain the swipe), and Task 4.3b-a may land the paginated `loadMoreHistory` list first and the virtualized list as its follow-up commit; the "fewer than 60 row nodes" criterion (TR-1, T-TY-08) is asserted when virtualization lands. The pinned "Needs attention" group, grouping and the bulk actions do not depend on either.
**Files**: `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationPanel.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationItem.tsx`, `web-app/src/components/history/VirtualHistoryList.tsx` (reference) or `@tanstack/react-virtual` directly, `web-app/src/components/ui/Collapsible.tsx`, `web-app/src/lib/utils/notificationGrouping.ts`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationPanel.test.tsx`.

#### Story 4.4: Bulk actions with server guard and undo
**As an** operator, **I want** Mark activity read, Clear informational and Clear history that never delete a decision, **so that** I can reset the tray safely.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- "Mark activity read" (shipped label, C8: `NotificationsPage.tsx:304`; not "Mark all read", because it excludes unread decisions) is visual only and excludes pending decisions from flipping back (reuses the shared bulk-read helper at `notificationMapping.ts:~140`, now keyed on `is_pending_decision`).
  - *Given* 5 unread rows including 1 pending approval, *When* "Mark activity read" (header icon button, `aria-label` and visible/tooltip text "Mark activity read") is clicked, *Then* `MarkNotificationRead` is called with the 4 non-decision ids and the approval stays unread; the badge shows 1.
- Clear informational lists what is kept and is guarded server-side. The client selects rows by `is_pending_decision == false` (server-sent; no client predicate).
  - *Given* 20 informational rows and 2 rows with `is_pending_decision` (one unread WARNING, one unread APPROVAL_NEEDED), *When* Clear informational (20) is chosen, *Then* the confirm line reads "2 awaiting decision kept", `ClearNotificationHistory{notification_ids:[20 ids]}` is sent after the UndoWindow (default 8s, Task 4.4g), and the server's `ClearByIDs` test shows that passing a pending-decision id deletes nothing for it (returns `deleted=20`, `kept=[that id]`).
  - *Given* the client was stale and sent a pending-decision id anyway, *When* the response lists it in `kept`, *Then* the client restores that row (no vanish-then-reappear) and the confirm line is corrected.
- Undo.
  - *Given* Clear informational confirmed, *When* Undo is tapped within the UndoWindow, *Then* no RPC is issued and the rows reappear.
  - *Given* the UndoWindow setting (per-device, 5/8/15/30s, default 8s, Task 4.4h) and an undo control that is hovered or focused, *Then* the countdown pauses and resumes with the remaining time (WCAG 2.2.1: a screen-reader or switch user can extend it), and the undo is announced through the Announcer (Story 3.6).
  - *Given* the page is hidden or closed (`visibilitychange` to hidden, `pagehide`) while a Clear informational awaits the end of the window, *Then* the deferred RPC is sent immediately with `fetch` `keepalive` (undo can no longer be offered, so the action completes instead of silently never happening) and the other tab is notified; if the transport cannot honor `keepalive` (INFERRED yes, verified in Task 4.4g) the pending clear is cancelled and the rows return on the next load. Either way no row is lost or half-deleted (TM-12).
  - *Given* the clear was initiated in the tray, *Then* its undo control renders as a sticky bar **inside the tray** (visible whenever the tray is open, including the mobile sheet), not in the deck's chip row.
- The overflow menu separates the undoable from the irreversible (triad UX; TM-11): "Clear informational (N)" with the caption "Undo available" sits above a divider; below the divider "Clear history..." carries destructive styling that is not colour alone (an icon plus the text "cannot be undone"), then a second divider and Collapse/Expand all groups.
- Clear history moved into the overflow menu with an **inline confirm**, not native `window.confirm` (C10; the shipped code uses `window.confirm` at `NotificationPanel.tsx:124` and `NotificationsPage.tsx:206`): text "Clear N read notifications? This can't be undone. M awaiting decision kept.", buttons [Cancel] [Clear N], focus starts on Cancel, Esc cancels; on RPC failure the confirm stays open with "Could not clear - Retry" and the list unchanged. Still excludes pending decisions (existing `Clear` guarantee). The Notifications page keeps its own shipped confirm unless PR 4 also converts it (not required).
  - *Given* the overflow menu "Clear history...", *When* chosen, *Then* `window.confirm` is called 0 times (spy), the inline confirm names the kept count, and Cancel leaves the list untouched.
- Failure rolls back visibly.
  - *Given* the RPC fails, *Then* rows reappear and an error toast "Could not clear notifications" is shown.
- A `kept` response is handled the same way on the tray and on the Notifications page (validation G-5, ux.md TM-10).
  - *Given* `ClearNotificationHistory{notification_ids}` returns `deleted=18` and `kept=[a, b]` (rows the client had optimistically removed but the server refused because they are unread pending decisions), *Then* the tray **and** `NotificationsPage` restore rows `a` and `b` in their original positions, show the line "2 kept: still needs attention" (visible until the next user action, announced once through the Announcer; the line itself has no `role`), and issue no further RPC; the Notifications page does not keep a private copy of the logic (both call the shared helper from Task 4.4f). *Given* an empty `kept`, *Then* no extra line is shown.
- Cross-tab: other tab drops exactly the cleared ids (Story 3.9 message; if Story 3.9 is cut, the shipped per-id `NOTIFICATION_DISMISSED` broadcast or the next history refresh does it, see Story 3.9).
**Files**: `server/notifications/store.go`, `server/notifications/store_test.go`, `server/services/notification_service.go`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationPanel.tsx`, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useNotificationHistory.ts`, `web-app/src/lib/contexts/NotificationContext.tsx`, `tests/e2e/notification-tray-bulk.spec.ts`.

#### Story 4.5: Docs and registry, and Quiet mode (in scope, sequenced last in Epic 4)
**As an** operator, **I want** finished docs and a Quiet mode, **so that** others can find how this works and I can quiet the toasts on one device.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Quiet mode (sequenced last in Epic 4) demotes every non-pinned toast to the tray and persists per device.
  - *Given* Quiet mode on, *When* a `custom` (type 100) notification arrives, *Then* no toast appears and the tray count increments; *When* any **pinned** type arrives (`warning`, `error`, `failure`, `approval_needed`, `input_required`, `confirmation_needed`, i.e. the server-pinned set, `IsActionableType` at `server/notifications/store.go:55-62`), *Then* it still toasts (Quiet never demotes a pending decision); reload keeps the setting; if `localStorage` throws, it renders with Quiet off and no crash. (Phase 4 fix: the earlier criterion used `warning` as the demoted example, but WARNING is a server-pinned type, TD-5. `custom` is used because `info` and `task_complete` are `HISTORY_ONLY_TYPES` that never toast, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useSessionNotifications.ts:19-26,134`, so they cannot show Quiet demoting anything.)
- Docs and registry.
  - *Given* the PR, *Then* `docs/how-to/manage-notifications.md` (toasts, tray, bulk actions, Quiet mode, and the hotkey when the Task 4.2f spike finds a safe chord) and `docs/reference/notification-delivery-gate.md` exist, `make registry-generate` is committed, and `pnpm run lint:duplicates` passes at threshold 0.14.

## Phase 4: Hidden-session view

### Epic 5: Read-only hidden sessions and Background activity (PRs 5a, 5, 5u, 5b, 5c and 5r, `feat(sessions):`; the read-only guarantee in PR 5 does not wait on Reply, PR 5r)
**Goal**: Every hidden-session notification that fires opens the session read-only 100% of the time; no UI path can write to a hidden session **except two**: an audited, rate-limited reply to a question that is actually outstanding (decision O2, Story 5.6, ADR-010) and the shipped backlog "Steer" of a live, backlog-linked review session (decision O7, default applied, Story 5.2, ADR-005). That guarantee rests on **typed capabilities** (`TerminalWriter`, the passed `*HeldLease`, the `steerAuthorization` token, the two UI-exception capabilities) plus **four checks** (literal lints, the RPC-descriptor classification, a short pinned-caller table of the pane-typing primitives, and the presence-and-use rule; ADR-005 decision 3, simplified in review-repair iteration 5 from a whole-tree scan), with **two UI exceptions** (the Reply and the O7 steer) and one **token-gated** steer writer that a handler may call only with a `steerAuthorization` built by the access-decision block; hidden sessions are listed in a Background activity section without being un-hidden. **Reply (Story 5.6) is sequenced last in Epic 5 and nothing else in Epics 1-5 depends on it** (review-repair iteration 3, operator decision to keep Reply in scope): the write lease (Story 5.0), the audit sink core (Task 2.8h), the guard set (Story 1.8) and the guard checks (Story 5.1d) stand alone for the steer and prune paths.

#### Story 5.0: Per-instance `TerminalWriteLease` as a passed capability (own PR 5a; independent of Reply; merges before PR 5u)
**As an** operator, **I want** every automated or unary writer to a session's pane to be serialized by one lease that is passed down the call chain, **so that** the steer, the nudges, the drivers and (later) a Reply never interleave bytes, and so that the lease depends on neither the Reply feature nor its spike (review-repair iteration 3: ARCH-NB3, ADV-N13, ARCH-C28, C29; this story took over the lease from Task 5.6m, which sat behind Spike 1.3g although the steer in Story 5.2 needs it; review-repair iteration 4: ARCH-C34, C35, C36c, C40, ADV-N21, m13).
**Gate**: starts only after the focused re-review PASS on item 1 (it is the lease design that ARCH-NB3 reopened); it needs no spike and no Contract PR and does not wait for Spike 1.3g, but it **needs PR 2a-2** (the audit sink core, Task 2.8h: every flip of its flag is a `flag_change` line; edge `5a -> 2a-2`, ARCH-C42, ADV-N31). It merges **before PR 5u** because Story 5.2 and the unary half of Story 5.1d take `*HeldLease` parameters. It ships behind the live-settable `terminal_write_lease` flag (default on, below).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- The lease is a capability, not a scanned reference (ARCH-NB3).
  - *Given* `Instance.TryTerminalWriteLease(writer string) (*HeldLease, bool)` and `Instance.AcquireTerminalWriteLease(ctx, writer string, maxWait time.Duration) (*HeldLease, error)` in `session/instance_write_lease.go`, *Then* they are the **only** constructors of `*HeldLease` (unexported fields: instance UUID, writer name, acquisition time, a `sync.Once` release), there is **one exclusive, non-reentrant lease per instance**, and a nil pointer, a zero value or a lease for another instance is refused by every write primitive with `ErrNoLease` or `ErrLeaseMismatch` and 0 writes (the same zero-value rule as the steer token). A held lease makes `Try` return `false` and `Acquire` return `ErrLeaseBusy` after `maxWait`.
  - *Given* `paneSubmitter` and `keySender` (`session/pane_submit.go:33-45`) are interfaces without an instance identity, *Then* they and the new `replySender` gain a `LeaseOwnerUUID() string` accessor (an `Instance` method over the immutable UUID) so the primitives can compare it with the lease's UUID; every fake in existing tests gains it (Task 5.0c; ARCH-C40d).
  - *Given* the classified write primitives that run under a lease, *Then* each takes a `*HeldLease` for the instance it writes to and never acquires: `SubmitDriverContent`, `SubmitContentWithEnter` and `SendKeysWithTimeout` (`session/pane_submit.go:95,141,170`), `SubmitReplyOnce` (Task 5.6k) and the server's `steerAuthorized`.
  - *Given* a call chain, *Then* **exactly one function per chain acquires**, the outermost writer: the session driver's answer-key closure, initial-prompt tick and nudge, the autonomous turn, `performNudge`, the MCP tool functions, the compactor closure, `WriteToSession`, `steerUnderLease`, `steerHiddenReviewViaBacklogLink`, `steerInternal` and, later, `replyToPendingQuestion`; everything below it receives the `*HeldLease`. A nested acquire is a programming error, not "busy". **What is and is not caught** (review-repair iteration 4, ARCH-C35; narrowed in iteration 5, when the static rule L3 was removed with the whole-tree scan; the earlier "marker in the context" could not be built, because `Try` takes no context and `Acquire` returns no context): (a) every **real** chain is caught at runtime by T-RO-43 (each writes once on a free lease and returns the retryable busy result on a held one without any link reporting busy to itself) and a **fixture chain that nests** is caught by T-WL-02 (the nested `Try` returns `false` at once and the nested `Acquire` returns `ErrLeaseBusy` after its bounded wait: it never hangs or deadlocks); (b) the **set of acquirers is closed** and pinned (Story 5.1d check (c), T-RO-45), so a new acquirer, the place a nested acquire would be written, fails the pinned-caller test until a reviewer reads it; (c) **not caught**: direct or transitive nesting in code no chain test runs, nesting through a function that has no lease parameter, or across goroutines; it appears as a spurious busy result on that chain. No context marker, no goroutine id and no production `panic` are used.
- Release ownership is one rule (ARCH-C40a-c, ADV-N13b, ARCH-C29a).
  - *Given* a function that holds a `*HeldLease`, *Then* it either releases it itself before returning or passes it to **one** callee that takes it over, and never does both and never `defer`s a release after the hand-off; the **innermost function that issues the write releases the lease exactly once on every return path, in the goroutine that issues the write**: `SendKeysWithTimeout`'s goroutine (`pane_submit.go:175`) releases when `SendKeys` returns, `SubmitDriverContent` releases on its early returns (`:96-104`, the cancelled context and the pane-owner refusal) and its error returns as well as on success, `SubmitContentWithEnter` hands the lease to `SubmitDriverContent` inside its goroutine (`:152`) and does not release it, `steerAuthorized` releases after `SendCommandImmediate` returns on its autonomous branch and hands the lease to `SubmitContentWithEnter` on the other, and an acquirer that fails between acquire and hand-off (for example the `steerHiddenReviewViaBacklogLink` link re-check) releases. `Release` is idempotent (a `sync.Once`) as a backstop, not as the design. *Given* `SubmitContentWithEnter` and `SendKeysWithTimeout`, which return `DeadlineExceeded` while their goroutine may still be inside `SendKeys` (`pane_submit.go:141-165,170-182`), *Then* the lease stays held until that goroutine's write returns; a test blocks the fake pane's write past the caller's timeout and asserts that a `Try` fails in between and succeeds after (T-WL-03; this is the old T-RP-51 case, moved here).
  - *Given* a leaked lease is a permanent busy with no forced release, *Then* a helper `session.AssertLeaseFree(t, inst)` (a `Try` that must succeed immediately) ends the test of **every acquirer** in the Story 5.1d table on every exit path (success, busy, write error, cancelled context, early return before the hand-off), so an acquirer that returns early without releasing fails its own test (T-WL-10); this is a per-acquirer assertion, not a global goroutine-leak check.
- Busy behavior is specified per writer, and a busy or lease-API result never consumes an attempt (ARCH-C29b, c; ARCH-C34, ADV-m13).
  - *Given* the lease is busy, *Then*: the driver's `sendAnswerKey` (`session/session_driver.go:365`) tries the lease inside the closure and returns the typed `ErrLeaseBusy`; **`answerDialogOnce` does not count `ErrLeaseBusy` as a send attempt** (VERIFIED: it increments `state.attempts` for any `send()` error and sets `dialogGaveUp` at `maxDialogAnswerAttempts = 3`, `session_driver.go:75,1160-1174`, sticky until the dialog hash changes, so three 2s ticks against a lease held by a Reply or a `SubmitContentWithEnter` (up to `3*DefaultPaneSettleMaxWait + 2s` = 8s) would abandon a startup or approval dialog for good): it returns the latch status unchanged and logs at debug, so the dialog is answered on the first tick after the lease frees (T-WL-04: three busy ticks, then a free lease, the dialog is answered once and the latch never reaches `dialogGaveUp`); the idle nudge (`:884`) uses `Try` and skips the tick; the one-shot initial prompt (`:706`) takes the lease **before** `*sendAttempts++` (`:686`) and consumes no attempt when busy (VERIFIED at `:686-720`: a failure leaves `sentInitial` false and retries on the next tick, but `*sendAttempts >= 3` calls `markSent()` and drops the prompt). A lease-API error from `SubmitDriverContent` (`ErrNoLease`, `ErrLeaseMismatch`: a programming error, because the acquirer just acquired the lease) is also non-counting there: it logs ERROR once, does not increment the attempt counter and does not call `markSent()` (T-WL-09). A `DeadlineExceeded` from an abandoned write still counts one attempt, and the retry after the lease frees can type the prompt twice; that is pre-existing and the lease does not change it (stated). The autonomous turn (`session/autonomous_driver.go:394`) takes a bounded blocking `Acquire` (`AutonomousTurnLeaseWait`, INFERRED 15s) and treats a timeout as its existing submit-error path (a Reply holds the lease for at most `ReplySendTimeout`); `performNudge` (`server/mcp/tools_diagnose.go:266`) and the MCP write tools return a retryable "a write is in progress" result, and `performNudge` takes the lease **before** `claimNudgeAttemptForWrite` (or releases the attempt on busy) so a busy lease never consumes the nudge attempt; `WriteToSession` and the steer return a retryable `FailedPrecondition`; `replyToPendingQuestion` uses `Try` with no wait and returns `NOT_SENT` (one rule, stated once; Story 5.6 step 6); the compactor returns an error its caller already logs; the rate-limit recovery input (`session/detection/ratelimit/manager.go:284`, through the `session`-package `SessionAccessor` wrapper that does the `Try`, because `ratelimit` cannot import the lease type) returns a send error (Task 5.0d verifies that the recovery loop retries an error and adds a bounded retry if not).
- A wedged write, its operator remedy and its notification (review-repair iteration 4, ADV-N21; default applied: the wedge raises a tray-level warning).
  - *Given* a PTY write that never returns (a wedged pane), *Then* the lease stays held (a forced release is deliberately not offered: it would allow exactly the interleave the lease exists to stop), the gauge `hidden_session_write_lease_held_seconds{writer}` (longest current hold) rises, a WARN `terminal_write_lease_wedged` (writer, session, held duration) is logged after `LeaseWedgeWarnAfter` (INFERRED 30s) and then at most once a minute, and every driver tick, steer, nudge and Reply gets the retryable busy result. Counter `hidden_session_write_lease_busy_total{writer}`. **Without a notification the driver's initial prompt and answer key would stop silently for a hidden backlog session, which then looks like a stuck review with nothing said**; so **once per wedge episode** (one acquisition that outlives `LeaseWedgeWarnAfter`) the server raises **one tray-level warning notification** for that session: type WARNING, priority medium (below `priorityUrgent` and not an approval, so `shouldNotify` does not push it, `server/push/subscriber.go:128-136`), stamped `delivery_class=failure` so the gate delivers it for a hidden session, title "A write to this session is stuck", message naming the remedy; it reaches toast, tray and history, never push (T-WL-08; Task 5.0f also checks that no Slack path forwards it, INFERRED none).
  - *Given* the remedy, *Then* it is the existing **Pause** or **Delete** of the session (also Hibernate and Stop), named in the notification and in `docs/how-to/manage-notifications.md`. No "unstick" control is built, because a forced release is exactly what the design refuses. **VERIFIED by reading** that none of those lifecycle actions can queue behind the wedged write: `TmuxSession.SendKeys` takes no lock around its bare `file.Write` (`session/tmux/tmux.go:1349-1355`: `GetPTY` returns the file under `ptmxMu`, which is released before the write), `Instance.SendKeys` takes none (`session/instance_tmux.go:1539-1545`), `TmuxSession.Close` (`tmux.go:1826-1860`) sets `ptyClosed`, closes the PTY triple and kills the tmux session without waiting on a writer, and Pause, Delete, Hibernate and Stop are not lease acquirers (the acquirer list is closed, Story 5.1d, T-RO-45). **INFERRED, not read:** whether the blocked `file.Write` then returns (the PTY master is an `*os.File`, which Go registers with its poller when the descriptor allows it, so `Close` should wake a blocked write with an error; in blocking mode only the kill of the attach process, which closes the slave side, ends it). T-WL-07 settles it with a real PTY pair whose reader stops, in both variants (close the master; close the slave): the write returns an error, the lease is released, and Pause, Delete, Hibernate and Stop of that session complete while the write is wedged. If neither variant unblocks the write, the plan returns to the operator instead of shipping a remedy that does not work. **Reachability and plumbing** (review-repair iteration 6, ADV-m14): (a) the remedy must be reachable from the hidden view the warning opens: the pane header's session actions menu (`PaneHeader.tsx:62-72`) stays in the read-only view (`design/ux.md` Surface 12 now lists it as kept, with the warning copy), and the Story 5.3 hidden-view e2e asserts the menu is present and Pause clickable; (b) no callback crosses the `session`/`server` boundary: `Instance` exposes `LeaseHeldFor() (writer string, held time.Duration, acquisitionID uint64)` and a server-side ticker in `session_service_events.go` (10s, fake clock in tests) raises the warning once per `acquisitionID` that has outlived `LeaseWedgeWarnAfter`; (c) the copy and actions are in `design/ux.md` Surface 12; (d) T-WL-07 (a real `Instance` against a real PTY pair) is an **Integration** test, not a Unit test.
- Lock order (ARCH-C29d).
  - *Given* the lease, *Then* it is never acquired while holding `i.mu`, the actor mutex or the `PendingQuestionStore` mutex, and a `-race` lock-order test runs a driver tick, a steer, a nudge and (once it exists) a Reply claim concurrently with the status poller (`HasUpdated`, `Preview`) and asserts no deadlock and no inversion (T-WL-05).
- A live-settable escape hatch (ARCH-C36c: PR 5a changes behavior for **every** session, visible ones included; `WriteToSession`, the visible steer, MCP writes and every driver write now return a retryable busy result while another writer holds the lease).
  - *Given* the flag `terminal_write_lease` (global only, default **on**, registered in `knownFeatureFlags`, no env var, read through an injected atomic `LeaseFlag` that fails closed, so an unreadable config keeps the lease on), *Then* with it **off** `TryTerminalWriteLease` and `AcquireTerminalWriteLease` return a valid, **non-exclusive** `*HeldLease` at once (the capability types keep working; only the serialization is skipped, which is today's behavior), so a lease regression is reversible without a deploy; with it off `ReplyToPendingQuestion` returns `NOT_SENT` (Reply's safety needs real serialization, Story 5.6; T-RP-78) and the O7 steer proceeds as it did before the lease; flips are recorded as `flag_change` lines by Task 2.8h (T-WL-11). **Flip class**: **off** is declared an escape hatch like `hidden_session_readonly_guards` to off: it is a loosening (it removes serialization), but it is persisted first and, when the sink is down, recorded only by the `flag_change_audit_degraded` log record, never refused with `Internal`, so a sink outage cannot block reversing a lease regression; **on** is a tightening flip (persist, queue the line). The flag is registered in `knownFeatureFlags` after PR 2a-2's registration of `hidden_session_gate` (both edit `feature_flag_service.go`).
- The write surface is pinned (ARCH-C28, ADV-N13c; simplified in review-repair iteration 5).
  - *Given* the `session` and `server` packages, *Then* every `acquirer` and `receiver` row of the Story 5.1d pinned table is converted to the lease in this PR (the type signature makes a receiver that is passed no lease a compile error, **for the five lease-typed primitives only**: `WriteToPTY` is not one of them, so the `ratelimit` wrapper's acquire-then-write is held in place by its `receiver-via-wrapper` table row and T-RO-45, not by the compiler; review-repair iteration 6b, ARCH-C51); every other row is `ui`, `lifecycle` or `none` with a reason; a caller of a primitive that is missing from the table fails the Story 5.1d pinned-caller test.
**Files**: `session/instance_write_lease.go`, `instance_write_lease_test.go`, `session/pane_submit.go`, `pane_submit_test.go`, `session/session_driver.go`, `session/autonomous_driver.go`, `session/detection/ratelimit/manager.go`, `server/mcp/tools_diagnose.go`, `server/mcp/tools_terminal.go`, `server/services/terminal_service.go`, `server/services/capacity_monitor.go`, `server/services/session_service_update.go`, `server/services/session_service_liveness.go`, `server/services/feature_flag_service.go`, `docs/how-to/manage-notifications.md`.

#### Story 5.1: Server-side read-only terminal streams
**As an** operator, **I want** a hidden session's terminal stream to refuse input and resize, **so that** a read-only view cannot be bypassed from the client (ADR-005).
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Input and Resize dropped at all four sites, and the resize closures behind them.
  - *Given* a stream attached to hidden session `h1` at each of `session_service_stream_terminal.go:405`, `connectrpc_websocket.go:2701`, `:3337`, `connectrpc_websocket_shell.go:257`, *When* the client sends an `Input{Data:"rm -rf"}` frame and a `Resize{Cols:200,Rows:50}` frame, *Then* the fake tmux target receives 0 bytes and 0 resize calls and a debug line is logged; for a visible session both are applied. **At the capture-pane site (`:3337`, input via `handleCapturePaneInput`, resize via `handleCapturePaneResize`) and the shell and fallback sites the "fake target" is the injectable `tmuxInputSender` seam** (Task 5.1c-a): `sendInputToTmux` is a package-level function that execs `tmux`, with no test seam today (VERIFIED: no test file under `server/` references `sendInputToTmux` or `handleCapturePaneInput`), so a fake that never sees the exec would report 0 bytes with or without the guard. The capture-pane row asserts **0 calls on the seam for a hidden attach and 1 for a visible one**, and T-RO-01 asserts 0 seam calls at **each of the five `sendInputToTmux` call sites** for a hidden attach (`handleCapturePaneInput`, the resize helper, `:1414`, `:2150`, `connectrpc_websocket_shell.go:304`), not only capture-pane (ARCH-C55).
- The capture-pane route is reachable for a hidden **managed** session (review-repair iteration 6, ADV-B1; VERIFIED `routeMainTerminalStream`, `connectrpc_websocket.go:1032-1050`: capture-pane polling is chosen with `STAPLER_SQUAD_USE_CONTROL_MODE=false` or `IsManaged == false`).
  - *Given* a hidden managed session attached with `STAPLER_SQUAD_USE_CONTROL_MODE=false` (and again with `IsManaged == false`), *When* the client sends `Input` and `Resize` frames, *Then* the sender seam sees 0 calls (T-RO-48).
- Resize paths that do not go through the frame dispatch are covered too.
  - *Given* a read-only attach, *Then* none of `applyOneControlModeResize` (`connectrpc_websocket.go:1504,1512`), `applyOneShellResize` (`connectrpc_websocket_shell.go:148`, `:463`), `shellPanePTY.ResizePTY` (`connectrpc_websocket.go:2358,2364`), or `StreamHub.RequestResize` (`connectrpc_websocket.go:1986,2161`) is invoked, and the attach-time size negotiation (`session/streamhub/hub.go` `AttachSubscriber` at L339 and `applyNegotiatedSize`) does not take this subscriber's size as a vote (a hub test with a read-only capability subscriber of size 200x50 leaves the pane size unchanged).
- Scroll forwarding is a pane write too (review-repair iteration 3, found while classifying the write surface).
  - *Given* a read-only attach, *Then* `Instance.ForwardScroll` (`session/instance_scroll_forward.go:105`, which sends the PageUp sequence through `SendInputViaControlMode`; called from the shared stream helper at `server/services/connectrpc_websocket.go:2805`) is **not invoked**: the helper takes the tmux-native scrollback path it already uses when `ForwardScroll` errors (`:2806-2810`), and a test with a fake instance asserts 0 `SendInputViaControlMode` calls for a hidden attach and the unchanged call for a visible one (T-RO-41). `AppScrollGate` limits the forward to idle or unknown status (`session/scroll_gate.go:71-76`) behind `terminalAppScrollForwardingClaudeFlagName`, so it never types into a dialog today, but it is still a UI-reachable write into the pane and the read-only promise names none.
- Read-only is decided server-side from `Snapshot().Hidden`.
  - *Given* a client that omits any read-only field, *Then* the hidden stream is still read-only.
- Output continues to stream.
  - *Given* a hidden stream, *When* the pane produces output, *Then* the client receives it.
- Shared helper keeps `dupl`/`gocognit` green.
  - *Given* `make ready-complexity-gate`, *Then* it passes.
**Files**: `server/services/session_service_stream_terminal.go`, `server/services/connectrpc_websocket.go`, `server/services/connectrpc_websocket_shell.go`, `server/services/terminal_readonly.go`, `server/services/terminal_readonly_test.go`.

- Files: `server/services/terminal_readonly_test.go`.

##### Task 5.1b: `writer TerminalWriter` field (nil when read-only) on params structs (`inputReadLoopParams` at `connectrpc_websocket.go:2695`, capture-pane, shell) + `acceptsInput()`
- Files: `server/services/terminal_readonly.go`, `server/services/connectrpc_websocket.go`, `server/services/connectrpc_websocket_shell.go`.

##### Task 5.1c-a: Apply the writer in `dispatchInputReadLoopFrame` and the capture-pane handler (`connectrpc_websocket.go:2701`, `:3337`) and skip scroll forwarding for a read-only attach (`:2805`)
- Set `writer` from `AccessFor(inst).Writer()` (Story 5.1d) at attach; the dispatch calls the primitives through the writer, so a read-only attach has nothing to call; the scroll helper at `:2805` does not call `ForwardScroll` without a writer. The per-site tests from Task 5.1a for these two sites pass.
- **Seam** (ARCH-NB7): introduce `type tmuxInputSender interface { SendInput(serverSocket, tmuxSession string, data []byte) error; Resize(p capturePaneStreamParams, cols, rows int) }` with one real implementation that wraps today's `sendInputToTmuxWithRetry` and `resizeExternalCapturePaneSession` unchanged, held as **one field on the server-level service struct that builds every one of those params structs** (review-repair iteration 6b, ARCH-C55; VERIFIED `capturePaneStreamParams`, `connectrpc_websocket.go:3226-3237`, holds no sender, and the fallbacks at `:1414`, `:2150` and `connectrpc_websocket_shell.go:304` live in functions with other params structs, so a per-struct field would have three different sources): production wiring sets the real implementation once at construction, each params struct copies the field, and a test injects a fake per service instance (no package-level variable, so parallel tests cannot share a fake). `handleCapturePaneInput` keeps its existing `p.snap.Permissions.CanSendCommand` check as a **separate, additional** condition, so the hidden-session guard is not conflated with it. **Check (d) covers the seam** (review-repair iteration 6b, ADV-N32): `tmuxInputSender.SendInput` and `tmuxInputSender.Resize` are members of check (d)'s set, and the real implementation's two methods are the **only** exempt definitions besides the three helpers' own (named, pinned like the other by-name exemptions), so a handler that obtains a sender from the service field or the params struct directly, instead of through the `TerminalWriter`, still fails R1/R2. `handleCapturePaneInput` and `handleCapturePaneResize` take the sender through the `TerminalWriter` (a read-only attach holds none), so the writer is also the only route to the sender. `sendInputToTmux` and `resizeExternalCapturePaneSession` keep their bodies; only the call sites change.
- Files: `server/services/connectrpc_websocket.go`, `server/services/terminal_readonly.go`.

#### Story 5.1d: Typed `TerminalAccess`/`TerminalWriter`, the RPC-descriptor classification and the pinned-caller guard set
**As an** operator, **I want** "no UI path can write to a hidden session, except the audited Reply and the backlog steer of a live review session (O7)" to rest on compile-time capabilities plus four checks, **so that** the next UI handler cannot regress it silently and the guard machinery stays small enough to maintain (review-repair iteration 5: simplified from the earlier whole-tree five-class scan with rules L1-L3, R1-R2, P1-P3 and S1-S3, which the type-level capability had made largely redundant for the lease class and in which every review pass found fresh defects; ADR-005 decision 3 records what was removed and why).
**Gate**: starts only after the focused re-review PASS on item 1 ("Pending focused re-review"), after Story 5.0 for the unary half only, PR 5u (checks (b) and (c) name `*session.HeldLease` and the acquire API; the stream half, PR 5, does not need it, see "PR membership" below) and **after Task 5.1d-0, the short spike that derives the pinned-caller list from the real tree and decides whether the heavier scan (below) is worth adding**. The Reply capability (`QuestionReplyWriter`, the Reply table rows) is **introduced by Task 5.6d, not by this story**, so Epic 5 ships without it; its contract (Story 1.7) additionally waits on Spike 1.3g.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- `TerminalAccess` is parsed once at the boundary and yields a capability (architecture C4).
  - *Given* `AccessFor(inst)` is the constructor used by every stream site, *When* it is called with a hidden instance, *Then* `ReadOnly`; visible, `ReadWrite`; it reads `inst.Snapshot().Hidden` only (`.claude/rules/instance-lock-free-reads.md`). `AccessForUnary(inst, flag GuardsFlag)` is the only other constructor (the flag is an injected `GuardsFlag` interface backed by an atomic, never a `bool` any caller could pass as `true`, which the compiler enforces, and it fails closed: an unreadable config keeps the guards on): it returns `ReadWrite` for a hidden target only while the live flag `hidden_session_readonly_guards` is off, it is called only by the Story 5.2 unary handlers, and **the pinned-caller test (check (c)) fails if any other function, a stream site included, calls it**. `access.Writer()` returns a `TerminalWriter` for `ReadWrite` and `ErrReadOnly` (mapped to `FailedPrecondition`) for `ReadOnly`. The non-MCP write helpers (`TerminalService.WriteToSession` path, the `UpdateSession` steer branch, stream input/resize helpers) and the params structs of Tasks 5.1b and 5.1c-a..c hold a `TerminalWriter`, never a `TerminalAccess` or a `readOnly bool`, so an ignored check cannot compile into a write.
- The UI exceptions are capabilities too (the Reply one lands with Task 5.6d, the steer one with Task 5.2e).
  - *Given* `access.QuestionReplyWriter(claim PendingQuestionClaim)`, *Then* it succeeds only with a valid unconsumed claim from `PendingQuestionStore.Claim` (Story 5.6) and exposes one method, `SubmitReply(ctx, text)`, which calls the Reply-specific `session.SubmitReplyOnce` (Task 5.6k: one content write, an Enter only in the recorded `ReplyKeystrokes` mode, no blind retry, under the per-instance `TerminalWriteLease`), **not** `SubmitContentWithEnter`; there is no way to obtain it without a claim (`PendingQuestionClaim` has unexported fields and one constructor, and check (a) keeps it that way inside package `services`).
  - *Given* `access.BacklogSteerWriter(link BacklogReviewLink)` (operator decision O7, Task 5.2e), *Then* it succeeds only with a `BacklogReviewLink` from `BacklogLinkResolver.ResolveLiveReviewLink` (newest `item_session` row has `Role == review` and `EndedAt == nil`, instance live; never a tag), exposes one method `Steer(ctx, text)` that runs `steerHiddenReviewViaBacklogLink` (the chain's acquirer: it takes the lease once and calls `steerAuthorized`) with a `steerAuthorization` token (kind `backlog_link`, carrying the instance UUID) that `decideSteerAccess` builds from the writer, and `BacklogReviewLink` has unexported fields and one constructor.
- **The guard set is four checks, not a whole-tree scan** (review-repair iteration 5). The compile-time half is the capabilities above plus the passed `*HeldLease` (Story 5.0): every primitive that types under the lease takes one, so a caller that "forgot to take the lease" for those primitives does not compile; the steer writer takes a token; the stream and unary writers hold a `TerminalWriter`. What compilation cannot see is **construction inside a package** (an unexported type is constructible by any function in its own package), the **RPC surface** and **new callers of the primitives**; the four checks cover exactly those, run in `make test-delivery-guards` behind the `sinkguard` build tag, and are type-based (`golang.org/x/tools/go/packages`, `go/types`; interface calls resolved to the concrete method set). **Mechanics** (review-repair iteration 6, ARCH-C49; VERIFIED: `grep -n 'golang.org/x/tools' go.mod` shows it `// indirect`, and the Makefile has no `sinkguard` tag or `test-delivery-guards` target yet): the tag and the target are **created by Task 2.6d**, so Task 5.1d-a does not start before Task 2.6d has merged; `go/packages` must load the generated `gen/` and `session/ent` output (gitignored), so the target depends on the existing `proto-gen` and `ent-gen` steps like `build` and `test` do; `golang.org/x/tools` becomes a direct requirement (`go mod tidy`). The checks are **independent of any handler set or write-class partition**: none needs a "unit class", a boundary rule or a fixture handler set.
  - **(a2) Tmux-subcommand literal lint** (T-RO-12; review-repair iteration 6, ARCH-NB7). *Given* `./session/...`, `./server/...`, `./daemon/...`, `./cmd/...` and `main.go` type-checked, *Then* no non-test file outside `session/tmux` and `session/tymux` contains a string constant (any `*ast.BasicLit` or constant expression whose value is exactly) `"send-keys"`, `"paste-buffer"`, `"load-buffer"`, `"resize-window"` or `"resize-pane"`, except inside the definitions of `sendInputToTmux` and `resizeExternalCapturePaneSession` (the named functions, which check (d) covers instead). It closes the subprocess-`tmux` class that is not a method call and so is invisible to checks (c) and (d); a new exec path in a new function fails until a reviewer reads it. It is a literal walk, not a call graph; a command built from a computed string is the stated residual (blind spot 1 below).
  - **(a) Capability-literal lint** (T-RO-36). *Given* `./session/...` and `./server/...` type-checked, *Then* no expression whose type is `session.HeldLease`, `steerAuthorization`, `BacklogReviewLink`, `PendingQuestionClaim` or the concrete `TerminalWriter` implementation is a composite literal, `new(T)`, a conversion `T(...)` or an initializer-less `var x T` (the first three are found through `types.Info.Types`; the `var x T` spelling has no value expression, so it is found by an `ast.ValueSpec` walk with `Info.Defs`) outside that type's **one defining file**: `session/instance_write_lease.go`; `server/services/steer_authorization.go` (it holds `decideSteerAccess` and the one `internal`-kind constructor used by `steerInternal`, so the only creators of a token are in one file); `hidden_review_steer.go`; `hidden_question_reply.go`; `terminal_access.go`. Across packages the unexported fields already make construction a compile error; this closes the same-package gap the compiler leaves open. **Not linted** (they yield a zero value with no spelling to match): named results, struct fields, `make([]T, n)` and array types of these types; they rely on the runtime zero-value refusal below. The zero value of a lease and of a token is also refused at runtime (T-WL-01 for the lease, T-RO-37 for the token), and a handler passing `steerAuthorized` a token built for another instance is refused at runtime by the UUID check (T-RO-37).
  - **(b) RPC-descriptor classification** (T-RO-17; the part that protects hidden sessions from a **new write RPC**). *Given* the descriptors of **every** service in the generated `proto/session/v1` package (14 services, `git grep -h '^service ' proto/ | wc -l`, not `SessionService` alone), *Then* every RPC is classified as a terminal write or not (a per-service table of names, with "not a terminal write" as an explicit listed default), an unclassified new RPC fails, `UpdateSession` is classified **by field** (`steer_message`, `program`, `auto_approve`; `status` has a stated reason, below) and `SwitchWorkspace` as a terminal write (details in the RPC bullet below).
  - **(c) Pinned-caller test** (T-RO-42, T-RO-45, T-RO-46). *Given* the **primitive set** `Instance.SendKeys`, `SendKeysN` (Task 5.6k), `WriteToPTY`, `TapEnter`, `SubmitDriverContent`, `SubmitContentWithEnter`, `SendKeysWithTimeout`, `SubmitReplyOnce` (Task 5.6k), `ClaudeController.SendCommandImmediate` (with `SendCommand` and `Instance.SendPrompt`, which have no production caller), `Instance.changeDirectory`, plus the **chain entry points** `steerUnderLease`, `steerInternal`, `steerAuthorized`, `steerHiddenReviewViaBacklogLink`, `replyToPendingQuestion`, `SteerActiveSession`, and the **capability and lease constructors** `AccessForUnary`, `TryTerminalWriteLease` and `AcquireTerminalWriteLease`, *When* the test lists every non-test function or function literal in `./session/...`, `./server/...`, `./daemon/...`, `./cmd/...` and `main.go` that calls one of them, *Then* that caller set must **equal the pinned table below in both directions**: a new caller fails (a reviewer has to read it and add a row with a one-line reason) and a removed caller fails (the stale row is deleted). A function literal is its own unit. **Interface calls** (`ratelimit.SessionAccessor.WriteToPTY`, `paneSubmitter`, `keySender`) are matched by method name and signature against `*Instance`'s method set, otherwise a call through an interface in another package is invisible to the check (ARCH-C45). **Exempt by name, not derived**: the PTY layer, so the table lists callers of the surface and not its internals: packages `session/tmux`, `session/tymux` and `session/mux`; the files `tmux_process_manager.go`, `native_process_manager.go`, `tmux_backend.go` and `backend_tymux.go` (the `ProcessManager`/`TmuxManager` implementations); and the `Instance` primitive definitions in `session/instance_tmux.go`, which call one another. The hub resize (`session/streamhub/hub.go:757`) is covered by the hub test (Story 5.1e), not by the table.
  - **(d) `uiWrite` R1/R2** (T-RO-10; these protect the read-only guarantee and stay). *Given* `./server/...`, *Then* every non-test function that calls a UI-stream primitive (`WriteToPTY`, `SendInputViaControlMode`, `ResizePTY`, `ResizePTYContext`, `SetWindowSize`, `SetWindowSizeContext`, `RequestResize`, `ForwardScroll`, **the capture-pane subprocess helpers `sendInputToTmux`, `sendInputToTmuxWithRetry` and `resizeExternalCapturePaneSession`** (review-repair iteration 6, ARCH-NB7 / ADV-B1) **and the `tmuxInputSender` seam methods `SendInput` and `Resize`** (iteration 6b, ADV-N32: after Task 5.1c-a the handlers call the seam, not the helpers, so without the seam methods in the set check (d) would no longer see `handleCapturePaneInput` and `handleCapturePaneResize` at all)) and every table row of kind `ui` below must have a `TerminalWriter` or `QuestionReplyWriter` parameter, receiver field or params-struct field (R1) **and reference it in its body** via `types.Info.Uses` (R2), so a function that accepts the capability and ignores it fails. **The helpers are real `./server` callers** (VERIFIED at `origin/main` `a91e7e46a`: `sendInputToTmux` `connectrpc_websocket.go:3532`, a `tmux send-keys -H` subprocess; `sendInputToTmuxWithRetry` `:3576`; `resizeExternalCapturePaneSession` `:3398`, `resize-window`/`resize-pane` subprocesses; callers `handleCapturePaneInput` `:3350` (which calls **only** `sendInputToTmuxWithRetry`, `:3361`) and `:3375` for the resize, plus the fallbacks at `:1414`, `:2150` and `connectrpc_websocket_shell.go:304`), so they are in the set and `handleCapturePaneInput` and `handleCapturePaneResize` need a `TerminalWriter` and must reference it. The only exemptions are the **definitions** of those three helpers (they contain the tmux subcommand literals, see check (a)) and, **by name**, the two methods of the one real `tmuxInputSender` implementation (they call the helpers and hold no `TerminalWriter`; without this exemption check (d) would fail on the real tree and T-RO-44 would be red, or the implementer would add an unlisted exemption); Task 5.1d-0 lists every other `./server` function that writes to a pane without a set member and the plan records the list. The internal (non-UI) writers call `SendKeys`, `SubmitContentWithEnter` and `SendCommandImmediate`, which the table pins instead.

A unit test asserts that `steerAuthorized` returns an error and writes nothing when the token's UUID differs from `inst.UUID`, when the token is the zero value, or when the lease belongs to another instance (T-RO-37).
