#### Story 5.5: Push click hands off to the open window (in scope; the last Epic 5 story before Story 5.6, which is sequenced after it; after Story 5.3)
**As an** operator, **I want** a push click to use my already-open window, **so that** the app does not reload and terminals are not remounted.
**Acceptance Criteria**:
- Service worker focuses an existing window and posts the URL instead of `openWindow`.
  - *Given* an open client at `/sessions`, *When* `notificationclick` fires with `data.url="/?session=h1&tab=terminal"`, *Then* `client.focus()` is called and `client.postMessage({type:"notification-click", url})` is sent; `openWindow` is called only when no client exists.
- Page navigates via the router on message, keeping the terminal pool.
  - *Given* the message, *Then* the app routes to the session without a full reload (`performance.navigation` unchanged / Playwright `page.on("load")` not fired).
**Files**: `web-app/public/push-sw.js`, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/usePushNotifications.ts`, `web-app/src/app/page.tsx`, `tests/e2e/push-click-handoff.spec.ts`.

##### Task 5.5a: Failing SW unit test with a mocked `clients` API
- Files: `web-app/public/__tests__/push-sw.test.js` (create only if no existing SW test location; Grep first).

##### Task 5.5b: SW change + page message listener
- Files: `web-app/public/push-sw.js`, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/usePushNotifications.ts`.

**PR 5c record (worker, 2026-10-09)**: Story 5.5 implemented. The click logic is extracted to `web-app/public/push-sw-logic.js` (pure, UMD; `importScripts`'d by `push-sw.js`): same-origin client picked (focused, then visible, then any) -> `postMessage({type:"notification-click", url})` + `focus()`; `openWindow` only when no client exists or `focus()` rejects. Page half: `usePushClickHandoff` in `usePushNotifications.ts`, mounted in `web-app/src/app/page.tsx`, routes through `router.push` and accepts only same-origin paths. Tests: `web-app/src/lib/hooks/__tests__/pushClickHandoff.test.ts` (12 cases, mocked `clients`), `tests/e2e/push-click-handoff.spec.ts` (validation TP-3 `tp3_should_route_without_load_event`; 4/4 on chromium and chromium-dom with `--retries=0`; it dispatches the SW message on `navigator.serviceWorker`, it does not drive a real push click). Deviations: (1) the SW test lives under `web-app/src` because jest `roots` is `src` only (the plan's `public/__tests__` is never collected); (2) the listener is a separate exported hook, not a side effect of `usePushNotifications()`, which is mounted only on the settings page and so never runs when a push click arrives. Wrong plan assumption: the old SW code focused a client whose URL *contained* the target and never navigated it, so an open window at another URL was left unrouted rather than reloaded.
**AWAITING OPERATOR (not run, not faked): real-device push click handoff (TP-3 on device).** Steps: (1) installed PWA on iOS 16.4+ and on Android Chrome, subscribed under Settings > Push; (2) with the app open at `/sessions`, trigger a hidden-session notification (URL ends `&notification=<id>`); (3) tap the push; expect the open window to focus and route to `/?session=<id>&tab=terminal` with no splash/reload and the terminal not remounted (check `performance.getEntriesByType('navigation').length` unchanged in remote inspector); (4) repeat with the app backgrounded and with the app fully closed (expect `openWindow` cold start); (5) on iOS note whether `client.focus()` rejects (falls back to `openWindow`, may produce a second window) and record it. Desktop Chrome with a real push is also unverified.

#### Story 5.6: Audited Reply to a pending question in a hidden session (decision O2, ADR-010; sequenced LAST in Epic 5)
**As an** operator, **I want** to answer a hidden session's "Claude has a question" from the read-only view, the toast, the tray or a Background row, **so that** a needs-human event is not a dead end; and **as** the owner of the read-only guarantee, **I want** that to be the narrowest possible write path, so everything else stays provably blocked.
**What exists today (VERIFIED)**: the question is `AskUserQuestion`; the approval hook defers to the native terminal dialog and the notification carries `nil` metadata (`server/services/approval_handler.go:420-428`, `:756-773`), so the only way to answer is to type into the terminal. `GuidanceRequestService` is a different mechanism (durable agent-initiated questions; `AnswerGuidanceRequest` stores the answer and never types into a terminal; `server/services/guidance_request_service.go:317-350`); a session-scoped guidance request on a hidden session stays answerable through that existing RPC and is not a terminal write. The steer branch (`UpdateSession`, `session_service_update.go:~315-320`) uses `session.SubmitContentWithEnter` (`session/pane_submit.go:141`); Reply does **not** reuse that primitive (`SubmitDriverContent` writes a blind second Enter at `:126` and the wrapper returns `DeadlineExceeded` at `:159` after content may be written, so "error" does not mean "nothing typed"): it has its own one-content-write, one-Enter, no-retry `SubmitReplyOnce` (Task 5.6k) behind a claim-gated capability, not by re-opening steer.
**Sequencing and hard gates (operator decision: Reply stays in scope, sequenced LAST in Epic 5, hard-gated on two things)**: Story 5.6 is the last item of Epic 5 and **nothing else in Epics 1-5 depends on it**: the write lease (Story 5.0), the audit sink core (Task 2.8h), the guard set (Story 1.8) and the Story 5.1d guard checks (whose Reply rows are added by Task 5.6d) stand alone for the steer and prune paths, and the `INPUT_REQUIRED` toast, tray row and Background row show "View output" only until it ships. It starts only when (a) **Spike 1.3g has recorded its results** (items (a)-(h) of Story 1.3: a reliable "still waiting" predicate, the pane-fingerprint rule, the real `tool_input` payload shape, the per-question-shape keystroke function, the hook list, the forged-hook negative test and the measured supersede frequency) **and (b) a passing re-review of the Reply design as it then stands** (a second review after the spike's answers replace the UNVERIFIED assumptions that the earlier reviews judged); and (c) Story 5.0, Contract PR 3 (Story 1.7), PR 1f-b (Story 1.8, the guard set; it needs PR 1f-a) and PR 2a-2 (Task 2.8h, the audit sink core) have merged and the focused re-review PASS on item 1 ("Pending focused re-review") exists. It ships behind the `hidden_session_reply` kill switch. **Residual risk, in plain words** (ADR-010 "Residual risk"): a typed answer can land in the wrong dialog, and the worst case is **approving a permission dialog** (a tool run in a hidden agent), because option 1 is "Yes". The kill switch stops further replies but **cannot undo a keystroke that was already sent**. The design lowers the chance (supersede on every dialog-producing hook, a live fingerprint check before the first byte and again before the Enter, a keystroke plan per question shape, a sender check on the hook) and does not remove it; what the design does not close is the gap between the last fingerprint compare and the write (microseconds to milliseconds; `DigitOnly` shrinks the worst case to a digit) and operator-typed partial input outside the fingerprint region (INFERRED); a change during the settle wait is caught by the pre-Enter compare (review-repair iteration 4, ADV-m11). **Accepted limit (default applied): Reply works only for sessions started after hook proof ships**, see the hook-proof criterion below. The question notification carries `nil` metadata today and the dialog is native to the terminal (`approval_handler.go:420-428`, `:756-773`), so there is no existing "answered elsewhere" signal the claim can trust (pre-mortem #4); a Reply typed into a prompt that is no longer a question would reach Claude as a new user message. If the spike finds detection unreliable, the plan returns to the operator (ADR-010 consequences) instead of shipping the weak "no meaningful output" fallback; the hidden `INPUT_REQUIRED` toast then shows "View output" only (the C15 variant).
**Acceptance Criteria** (server; rewritten in review-repair iteration 1 for focused re-review ARCH-B1/B2/B4, ADV-B1/B2 and the Reply concerns; ADR-010 decisions 1-9):
- A question is registered only when it is attributable, and is replyable only after the dialog is seen.
  - *Given* an `AskUserQuestion` permission request whose `X-CS-Session-ID` header resolves to a live instance, *When* `broadcastQuestionNotification` runs (`approval_handler.go:759-776`), *Then* the `INPUT_REQUIRED` event carries metadata `question_id=<128 random bits>` and `question_shape`, and `PendingQuestionStore` holds a **`candidate`** `{QuestionID, SessionUUID, PromptText, QuestionToken, QuestionShape, CreatedAt}` keyed by the instance **UUID** (`QuestionToken` is the whitespace-normalized first 40 runes of the question text from `tool_input`, never longer than the longest token that survives terminal wrapping at 80 columns (Spike 1.3g), else the first option label; **when `tool_input` yields neither, or the token is on the dialog-boilerplate denylist** ("Do you want to proceed?", "Chat about this" and the other titles the spike records from permission and question dialogs; ADV-N15), **the question is registered not replyable**, because the fingerprint then has nothing that identifies this question); the store is bounded (256 total, 8 per session, oldest evicted) with a TTL (default 30 min, INFERRED, adjusted by Spike 1.3g).
  - *Given* a question whose session was resolved only by the longest cwd-prefix fallback or came back `unknown` (`resolveSessionID`, `approval_handler.go:841-870`, `:318-321`; a sibling `resolveSessionAttribution` returns `header | cwd | unknown` and `resolveSessionID` keeps its signature), *Then* nothing is registered, no `question_id` is stamped and the toast keeps "View output" only (ARCH-B4).
  - *Given* the hook endpoint `/api/hooks/permission-request` is a plain mux route with no sender authentication (VERIFIED: `server/server.go:824`; the injected hook sends only `X-CS-Session-ID`, which is the session **title**, `server/services/hook_injector.go:380`, `approval_handler.go:1001`, and any local process, or any LAN host on a wildcard bind, can post to it), *Then* (review-repair iteration 3, ADV-N15; **redesigned in iteration 4, ADV-N23, ARCH-C37**):
    - **What the proof is and is not.** It stops a *forged request from another local process or a LAN host that cannot read the session's files* from registering a replyable question. It does **not** stop the agent itself (the hidden review agent is the untrusted principal: it reads PR content, the driver auto-answers its approvals, and it can read its own `.claude/` files, the same-user secret file and any session's hook file), nor any same-user process; a same-user reader can forge a proof for any session, and that is inside the stated rail. **The control that actually stops attacker-influenced text from approving a permission dialog is the live pane check** (title region and boilerplate denylist, T-RP-61), not the proof; the proof only narrows who can create a candidate.
    - (a) **Design**: `proof = HMAC-SHA256(secret, "ssq-hook-v1" || session UUID)` under a random 32-byte secret kept 0600 in the config directory, **keyed to the stable session UUID, not the title**, so a rename (`UpdateSession` edits the title) no longer silently ends replyability; the header value is `<uuid>.<proof>`, and when the proof verifies (constant-time compare) the server resolves the instance **by that UUID, and that UUID is the attribution: the `X-CS-Session-ID` title header is ignored when a proof verifies** (so a rename, or the first-writer title that a shared `settings.local.json` fixes for every later session in the same root, cannot mis-attribute a question or supersede another session's; ADV-N29). A request without a valid proof is attributed as today (title, then cwd) and registers nothing replyable. **There is no per-request nonce** (rejected: Claude Code runs the same hook command every time, so a nonce can only be per injection, revoking one needs server-side per-session state that survives a restart, and the realistic attacker reads the file or is the agent; rotating the secret is the revocation tool and its effect is labelled in (e)). **Not in argv, per session, fail-open** (review-repair iteration 6, ARCH-NB8, ADV-N29, ARCH-C46): the proof lives in a **per-session file outside every worktree**, `<config dir>/hook-proofs/<session UUID>` (directory 0700, file 0600, one line `X-CS-Hook-Proof: <uuid>.<proof>`, written atomically: a temporary file in the same directory created `O_EXCL` 0600, `fsync`, rename), because a hidden review session shares its repository root with other sessions (`SpawnReviewSession` makes a directory session on `item.RepoPath`, `session_service_diagnose.go:53-60`) and `hookAlreadyPresent` keeps the first entry written to a shared `settings.local.json`, so a per-root file or a per-root command cannot carry a per-session UUID; being outside the worktree also means `git add -A` cannot commit it, so no `info/exclude` entry is needed. The session's environment **already carries `STAPLER_SESSION_UUID`, so no new variable and no new setting site is added** (review-repair iteration 6b, ADV-N33; VERIFIED at `origin/main` `a91e7e46a`: `Instance.buildExtraEnv`, `session/instance_tmux.go:729-733`, adds `STAPLER_SESSION_UUID=<uuid>` and `wireTmuxSession` (`:771`; `SetExtraEnv` at `:795`) passes it as tmux `new-session -e`; `wireTmuxSession` is the one constructor behind every tmux start path: creation and restart at `session/instance.go:2399` and `:2600`, restore and resume at `session/instance_serialization.go:467-552`, and the program-switch path at `session/instance_tmux.go:822`; `claudeSettingsEnvOverrideArgs` carries the same keys through Claude Code's `--settings` env block; `session/tmux/tmux_ownership.go` already reads it back; remote ssh sessions get neither, which matches "remote is not proofed"). **Spike 1.3g h8 keeps the one fact still unverified: that a Claude Code hook subprocess sees the pane's environment** (INFERRED yes, since the variable is in the pane process environment and in `--settings` env), and the one shared command resolves the file at run time. **The command must stay fail-open**: VERIFIED by running curl 8.22.0 here, `curl -H @/nonexistent ...` prints "option -H: error encountered when reading a file" and **exits 26 without opening a connection**, which would stop the approval hook of every session whenever the file is missing (`git clean`, a recreate, a failed write). The injected command therefore adds the header only when the file is readable: `F='<escaped config dir>/hook-proofs/'"$STAPLER_SESSION_UUID"; T='<escaped token header file>'; set --; [ -n "$STAPLER_SESSION_UUID" ] && [ -f "$F" ] && [ -r "$F" ] && set -- -H "@$F"; [ -f "$T" ] && [ -r "$T" ] && set -- "$@" -H "@$T"; curl -s --max-time N -X POST '<url>' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -H 'X-CS-Session-ID: <escaped title>' "$@" -d @-` (the `T=...` assignment and its guarded `set --` are emitted only when the local-token header file exists at injection, as `hookAuthArg()` does today) (the positional-parameter form is used instead of `$( [ -r F ] && printf -- '-H @%s' F )`, which word-splits a path containing a space or a quote; both were run against a missing file, a present file and a path with a space and a quote here: absent gives exit 7 "connection refused" with 0 extra args, present adds `-H @path`; VERIFIED). Every interpolated value (config dir, URL, title) goes through one single-quote escaper (`'` becomes `'\''`), which also fixes the existing unescaped title in `hook_injector.go` `buildHookCommand` and `approval_handler.go` `InjectHookConfig`. A missing or unreadable file therefore degrades to `no_proof`; a curl older than 7.55 (checked at injection with `curl --version`) injects the command without the proof branch, which also reads as `no_proof`). **"Fail-open" covers the token header too** (review-repair iteration 6b, ADV-N35; VERIFIED at `origin/main` `a91e7e46a`, `server/services/hook_injector.go:382-415`): today `buildHookCommand` appends `hookAuthArg()`, an unguarded `-H @'<token header file>'` (`pkg/localtoken/localtoken.go:105-111`), to the same curl, so a missing token file makes that curl exit 26 with no request, exactly the failure of NB8. The claim "never stops the approval hook" therefore holds only because the token header is **guarded the same way** in the same command (the `T=` branch above, one fix for one bug class); with local-token auth off there is no token branch and nothing to guard. **One producer, no second rewriter**: `buildHookCommand` produces the **whole** desired command (proof branch, token branch, escaped values) and the merge compares an existing entry against that output; `upgradeHookAuth` (`hook_injector.go:409-441`, which splices `hookAuthArg()` before `-d @-` in place) is **removed** and its job is folded into the comparison, so there is no ordering question between two in-place rewriters, a command that has both branches is the fixed point (a third injection changes nothing), and an entry that predates both (no proof branch, no token) is replaced once with the full command (T-RP-73).
    - (b) **Re-injection actually replaces** (the earlier "session start re-injects hooks so an older `settings.local.json` gains it" was false): VERIFIED `hookAlreadyPresent` returns true for an existing entry that references the URL (`hook_injector.go`; line numbers are re-derived at implementation time, see the code-reference convention at the top of "PR Slicing"), so an existing entry is kept without the new header, and the worktree is reused across reopen cycles. Task 5.6n changes the merge: an existing entry whose command differs from `buildHookCommand`'s output (no `$STAPLER_SESSION_UUID` proof branch, or no guarded token branch) is **replaced in place**, an entry equal to it is untouched, and the session's proof file is rewritten by one idempotent helper at the call sites named in Task 5.6n (creation, resume, restart and service boot), so a rotated secret reaches a running session that already has the new command (the hook reads the file on each run). **Live panes keep their original environment and their original hook snapshot** (review-repair iteration 6b, ARCH-C57): a session whose pane has the variable and whose settings entry already has the file form picks up a rotated secret on its next hook run; a session whose pane has the variable but whose entry predates the file form gets the new entry at the next injection, but a running agent that snapshots hooks at start keeps the old command (INFERRED; Spike 1.3g h8); a pane started before `STAPLER_SESSION_UUID` could be read keeps no proof either way (that is case (f)). **Rotating the secret therefore revokes the proof of every session that has the new command and does not revoke running sessions that still hold the old one; it is not a revocation tool for every live session.**
    - (c) **Sender inventory** (VERIFIED by grepping `X-CS-Session-ID`): the per-session curl hook (`hook_injector.go:380`, `buildHookCommand`), the single-hook curl (`approval_handler.go:1001`, `InjectHookConfig`), the remote socat form (`remoteApprovalHookCommand`, defined at `hook_injector.go:241`, called from `buildHookCommand` at `:377` and from `approval_handler.go:1209`), the ssq-mux HTTP hook with a static header map (`session/mux/hooks.go:113`, value `meta.TmuxSession`), the sshremote relay's in-process request that sets the header itself (`session/sshremote/approval_relay.go:627`) and the standalone `scripts/ssq-hook-handler:494` (`scripts/cs-hook-handler` is a symlink to it). **Decision per sender: only the first two carry a proof; every other sender is not proofed and therefore not replyable** (none of those sessions is hidden today); T-RP-74 pins the table so a new sender fails until it is decided.
    - (d) **A proofless duplicate never supersedes a proofed request**: a global plus a per-session hook can send the same dialog twice, one proofed and one not, and the proofless copy must not run `supersedeForDialog` against the proofed registration. The rule is **dedupe by dialog digest** (`SHA-256(session UUID || tool_name || normalized tool_input)`): a proofless request whose digest equals that of a live registered question is dropped as a duplicate (counted `duplicate_proofless`) and neither supersedes nor registers; a proofless request with a **different** digest still supersedes, because a different dialog may be on screen (the safe direction; the cost of a forged one is a refused Reply, as for a forged toast today) (T-RP-75).
    - (e) **Cause label and a visible state** (the earlier design lost Reply silently for sessions that predate the proof): every question that is not replyable increments `hidden_session_reply_registered_unreplyable_total{cause}` with `cause` in `no_proof` (header absent: a session whose hook predates the proof, or a sender of (c) that is not proofed), `bad_proof` (present but invalid: a rotated or lost secret, or a forgery), `no_token`, `boilerplate`, `shape`, `path_only`, and stamps the notification metadata `reply_unavailable=<cause>`; for `no_proof` and `bad_proof` the card says "Reply unavailable for this session (started before reply support was enabled; restart the session)" (UX RP-17), and the other causes keep their existing degraded copy (T-RP-77).
    - (f) **Accepted limit (default applied; the operator may override): Reply works only for sessions started after hook proof ships.** Claude Code most likely snapshots its hooks at start (INFERRED; Spike 1.3g checks it), so a rewritten file does not reach a running agent that has the old command; every session alive at upgrade, including long-lived hidden review sessions kept alive by `--tmux-keep-server` (whose tmux server and panes keep their original environment across a service restart), shows the `no_proof` state until it is restarted. This is fail-safe (it never silently accepts), and it is now labelled and counted instead of silent. Stated in `design/ux.md` Surface 12b, ADR-010 decision 2d4, Risk Control and `docs/how-to/manage-notifications.md`.
    - (g) Tests: T-RP-61 (a forged hook with a boilerplate token over a real permission dialog: no promotion, 0 writes), T-RP-62 (no proof: supersede yes, replyable no; a valid proof: replyable; a proof for another session: refused), T-RP-73 (replace, file form, not in argv), T-RP-74 (sender table), T-RP-75 (duplicate), T-RP-76 (a rename keeps the proof valid), T-RP-77 (cause label and metadata).
  - *Given* two instances that share a `Path` (a visible session and a hidden review session created with `CreateDirectorySession(item.RepoPath, ...)`, `session_service_diagnose.go:57-60,103-108`), a question from the visible one with a missing or stale header, and one header-attributed to the hidden one, *Then* the first is not registered, the second is registered under the hidden UUID, and a `Reply` naming the visible session's id (or its title or tmux name, which `MatchesID` also accepts, `session/instance_terminal.go:72-80`) returns `NO_PENDING` or `NOT_HIDDEN` with 0 writes: `Claim` resolves the request `session_id` through `findInstanceByID` and requires `inst.UUID == pending.SessionUUID`.
  - *Given* a `candidate`, *When* the detector reports a status in the **set recorded by Spike 1.3g for the AskUserQuestion dialog** (with an active controller; the status is a cached pre-filter, never hard-coded to `StatusNeedsApproval`, which is the permission-dialog state, `session/detection/detector.go:93-94`) **within the confirmation window (seconds from the hook, recorded by the spike; not the 30-minute TTL)** and a **live pane capture** contains the `QuestionToken`, *Then* it becomes `confirmed` and the live fingerprint of the region that contains the question text and option labels is captured at that moment; *Given* the dialog is never seen in the window, *Then* the candidate expires and any claim returns `NOT_WAITING`; *Given* a capture that lacks the token (a permission dialog, a shell prompt), *Then* it is never promoted. **The promotion is a compare-and-set under the store mutex** (review-repair iteration 3, ARCH-C31c): the pane capture runs outside the mutex, so the store reads a per-session supersede epoch before the capture and promotes only if the entry is still `candidate`, unexpired, not superseded and the epoch is unchanged; a supersede that arrives between the capture and the promotion is never lost (T-RP-33 extended under `-race`).
  - *Given* a second question registered for the same session (a queued or parallel `AskUserQuestion`, or question A answered in the terminal and B asked), *Then* **every older id for that session is superseded**, and `Reply` for the older id returns `SUPERSEDED` with 0 writes even though the session is still waiting (on B).
  - *Given* **any other dialog-producing hook for the session** (review-repair iteration 2, ARCH-NB2): every `PermissionRequest` hook, whatever the classifier then decides, handled in `approval_handler.go` right after the session is resolved (`:316-321`) and before every branch (including the defer-to-terminal branch at `:420-428`), and every `AskUserQuestion` whose attribution is `cwd` or `unknown` and whose cwd matches a live instance (it registers nothing, but it supersedes), plus any further hook event the spike lists, *Then* **every pending question for that session, and for every live instance whose path matches the cwd, is superseded** (over-superseding is the safe direction: the cost is a refused Reply). **Path matching (review-repair iteration 3, ARCH-C31)**: a helper `matchingInstanceIDs(cwd)` returns **every** live instance for which `Path`, `WorkingDir` **or `Worktree.WorktreePath`** (`InstanceData.Worktree.WorktreePath`, `session/storage.go:186-188`) is a path-boundary prefix of the hook's cwd (or the cwd of it), because `resolveSessionID` (`approval_handler.go:841-870`) keeps only the single longest `Path` or `WorkingDir` prefix, uses a bare `strings.HasPrefix` and ignores the worktree path, so reusing it unchanged would miss a hidden session whose `Path` is the repository root while the hook's cwd is its worktree; `resolveSessionID` keeps its behavior and signature. **Expected frequency**: hidden review sessions share `item.RepoPath` with visible work sessions (`session_service_diagnose.go:57-60`), so every `PermissionRequest` of a visible session in the same repository supersedes a hidden session's pending question and `SUPERSEDED` may be common in a busy repository; `hidden_session_reply_total{outcome=superseded}` carries a `cause` label (`question`, `permission_request`, `path_share`), Spike 1.3g measures the `path_share` rate before Contract PR 3 freezes the UX, and if it makes Reply unusable the recorded fallback narrows the path-share supersede to pending questions whose live fingerprint no longer matches (the live fingerprint stays authoritative, at an extra stated risk that the re-review of the Reply design judges). *Given* the operator answers q1 in the terminal, a Bash permission dialog appears during the confirmation polls and the hook fires, *Then* q1 is superseded and never promoted with the permission dialog's fingerprint, and a later `Reply "1"` returns `SUPERSEDED` with 0 writes (T-RP-44); *Given* two questions that share an option layout where the second is never registered (no header), *Then* the live fingerprint, whose region contains the question text, separates them (`STALE_PROMPT`, T-RP-45).
  - *Given* Spike 1.3g's recorded payload shape (its **first** check, Story 1.3 (h1): if the real payload has no `tool_input["prompt"]` text and no token can be derived, every question is registered not replyable and the plan returns to the operator), *Then* a question whose shape is not a single-answer prompt is registered as not replyable (`Claim` returns `NO_PENDING`) and `question_shape` tells the client to show "Open the terminal to answer" instead of a composer; a payload with neither question text nor an option label has no `QuestionToken` and is registered not replyable the same way (the card says "Open the terminal output to read the question" with View output only), so a Reply can never be bound to a dialog that cannot be identified.
  - An entry is removed on reply, TTL, supersede, the session leaving the waiting state and session deletion.
- Reply is accepted only for the question on screen in a hidden session, in this order (ADR-010 decisions 3-4).
  - *Given* hidden `h1` with a confirmed pending question `q1`, *When* `ReplyToPendingQuestion{session_id, question_id:q1, reply_text:"2", reply_id}` is called, *Then*, in this order (review-repair iteration 2, ARCH-C19: the earlier order ran the limiters before the idempotency lookup, so a double tap or a lost-response Retry inside 5s got `RATE_LIMITED` instead of the first result): (1) the feature flag is read (fail closed) and the input is validated; (2) **the idempotency lookup**: a cached result for the `reply_id` is returned, else a request whose `reply_id` is in flight waits for it (bounded by the send deadline) and returns the first result; a `reply_id` seen before with a different SHA-256 of `(session UUID, question_id, text)` is `InvalidArgument` (ARCH-C20, ADV-N10); (3) the per-session and global limiters are checked with `rate.Limiter.Reserve()` and `Delay() == 0` (not `Allow()`), so the reservation can be cancelled on `NOT_SENT`; a non-zero delay cancels it and returns `RATE_LIMITED`; (4) `Claim` runs as **one critical section** under the store mutex (lookup, expiry, supersede check, state `confirmed -> claimed`); (5) the pre-write audit line is appended and `fsync`ed; (6) the per-instance `TerminalWriteLease` is taken with **`TryTerminalWriteLease`, no wait** (review-repair iteration 4, ADV-m11: the earlier "bounded wait" disagreed with Story 5.0, which listed busy behavior for every writer except Reply; a busy lease is `NOT_SENT` at once, claim released, reservation cancelled, and the card's Retry covers it; with the flag `terminal_write_lease` off Reply returns `NOT_SENT` because its safety needs real serialization, T-RP-78; `replyToPendingQuestion` is the chain's acquirer and passes the `*HeldLease` to `SubmitReplyOnce`); (7) **under the lease and immediately before the first byte**, the before-first-byte checks run: the session is still hidden (`AccessFor`) with `inst.UUID` equal to the claim's, the claim is not `claimed-superseded`, a status in the **set recorded by Spike 1.3g** is reported with an active controller (cached pre-filter; `StatusUnknown` is rejected, so a hidden `other` session at a shell prompt is never typed into, and a permission dialog is rejected if the spike's set excludes it), and the **live** pane capture's fingerprint of the question region (which contains the `QuestionToken`) equals the stored one: the live fingerprint is authoritative; (8) `SubmitReplyOnce` writes the content once, waits, **re-runs the live-fingerprint compare under the lease and, on a mismatch, writes no Enter and returns `SEND_INDETERMINATE` (text typed, not submitted; ADV-N16)**, then writes an Enter once **only if the keystroke plan recorded for this question shape has one**, with no retry; (9) the outcome line is appended, the lease is released when the writing goroutine exits and the response is `SENT`.
  - *Given* no pending question, a `question_id` that belongs to another session, an expired or already-answered question, a superseded id, a candidate, a session whose status is outside the recorded set or has no active controller (including a fake pane at a shell prompt and a fake pane showing a permission dialog), a changed live pane fingerprint, or a visible session, *Then* the response outcome is `NO_PENDING`, `SUPERSEDED`, `NOT_WAITING` or `STALE_PROMPT` or `NOT_HIDDEN` respectively, with 0 terminal writes and the claim not left consumed.
  - *Given* the same `reply_id` sent twice **while the first is in flight**, *Then* the second waits on the first (singleflight keyed by `reply_id`, bounded by the send deadline) and returns the first result, never `NO_PENDING` and **never `RATE_LIMITED`, including inside the 5s per-session window** (the lookup runs before the limiters); *Given* the same `reply_id` after a `SENT` or `SEND_INDETERMINATE` result, *Then* the cached result is returned and nothing is written. *Given* the same `reply_id` with a different `question_id`, `session_id` or text, *Then* `InvalidArgument` (the cache stores the payload hash). The result cache and the in-flight map are **bounded** (1024 entries, LRU, and a 10-minute TTL, INFERRED; an evicted id retried later finds the question consumed and returns `NO_PENDING` with 0 writes, never a second write). *Given* a supersede or a session delete that arrives while the entry is `claimed`, *Then* it is marked `claimed-superseded`, the before-first-byte checks stop the write, and a later `NOT_SENT` release moves it to `superseded` and never back to `confirmed`; a released claim is re-validated (expiry, newest id, still hidden) before it can be claimed again (T-RP-55).
  - *Given* two goroutines replying to the same session with different `reply_id`s under `go test -race` with a barrier, *Then* exactly one claim succeeds and one terminal write happens.
- The send sequence and its failure classes replace "release the claim on any error" (re-review ARCH-B1, ADV-B1; ADR-010 decision 3; this supersedes validation G-3's earlier resolution, which let Retry type the answer twice).
  - *Given* a fault-injecting `paneSubmitter` per stage, *Then*: a failure strictly **before the first byte** (ctx already done, `VerifyPaneOwner` error, a before-first-byte check, a busy write lease, **or a content write that the count-returning `Instance.SendKeysN` proves wrote zero bytes**: PTY not initialized, instance not started or paused, `0, err`; review-repair iteration 2, ADV-N2: the count accessor is mandatory, otherwise the commonest zero-byte failures would be indeterminate and consume the claim for nothing) is `NOT_SENT` (or the more specific outcome), releases the claim, cancels the limiter reservation, and a Retry with the same `reply_id` attempts the write again with a new audit pair; an error returned by the **content** write with `n > 0` bytes or after a timeout, a ctx expiry or a client disconnect between the two writes (the text stays typed, not submitted), or an **Enter** write error is `SEND_INDETERMINATE` (the card says it may have been typed but not submitted): the claim is consumed, the result is cached under `reply_id`, there is no automatic retry, and a Retry with the same `reply_id` returns the cached result; the fake pane that accepts the content and then errors on Enter records exactly **one content write and at most one Enter across the original attempt and a Retry**; a pane that never changes after the Enter returns `SENT` with no second Enter (the blind Enter retry of `SubmitDriverContent`, `session/pane_submit.go:126`, is not used); **the abort flag is a compare-and-swap state word, not a check-then-act flag** (ARCH-C15): the writing goroutine takes `idle -> writing` immediately before each `SendKeys` and the caller on timeout takes `idle -> aborted` (or `writing -> writing-aborted` when a write is already in the syscall), whichever loses backs off, so after the caller returns at most the one write already inside the syscall can still land (stated limit); a test blocks the first write past its timeout, then unblocks the fake pane and asserts **zero further writes** (T-RP-53). The per-instance **write lease is held until the abandoned goroutine exits**, not until the caller returns, so a second Reply started inside the 5s limiter window cannot interleave bytes with a still-blocked first write: it gets `NOT_SENT` (ARCH-C16, T-RP-51).
  - *Given* the pinned-caller test (Task 5.1d), *Then* `replyToPendingQuestion` is a pinned `acquirer` row whose chain reaches only `SubmitReplyOnce`; `SubmitContentWithEnter` and `SubmitDriverContent` have no row for it (T-RP-41).
  - *Given* the keystroke plan is a **function of the question shape and the reply text**, `(question_shape, reply_text) -> ReplyKeystrokes`, recorded by Spike 1.3g (review-repair iteration 3, ADV-N16: one global mode cannot express a dialog that accepts a digit and also has an "Other" text field, and a digit that selects immediately followed by an Enter approves the next dialog, whose default is option 1 "Yes"), *Then* a single digit on a select dialog is always `DigitOnly` (no Enter), free text is accepted only for a recorded text-field shape, and any other shape is not replyable (the card offers numbered picks or "Open the terminal" only). *Given* a fake pane where a digit selects immediately and a permission dialog appears during the settle wait, *Then* no Enter is written (T-RP-63). *Given* the pane fingerprint changes between the content write and the Enter (the operator answered in the terminal, or a new dialog drew), *Then* the pre-Enter compare fails, no Enter is written, the outcome is `SEND_INDETERMINATE` ("typed, not submitted"; claim consumed, no Retry) and the audit outcome line records `aborted_before_enter` (T-RP-64). The fingerprint normalizes the selection glyph, so arrowing in the terminal reads as a changed dialog, which is the safe direction (a false `STALE_PROMPT`).
- The session driver and nudges are serialized against the Reply write (review-repair iteration 2, ADV-N1; VERIFIED at `013856269`).
  - The pane has other automated writers: the session driver's `sendAnswerKey` sends `"1\n"` into any `StatusNeedsApproval` dialog whose text contains "allow reading", "allow writing", "allow executing" or "do you want to proceed" (`session/session_driver.go:365,824-836`, `shouldApprovePrompt` at `:1338-1354`, latched once per dialog hash), plus the initial prompt (`:706`) and the idle nudge (`:884`, `SubmitDriverContent`); `performNudge` writes through `SubmitContentWithEnter` (`server/mcp/tools_diagnose.go:266,287`). Backlog sessions, which include hidden review and diagnose sessions, run the driver. Without serialization a driver tick can land between a reply's content and its Enter and the mangled answer is still reported `SENT`.
  - *Given* a reply in flight (the per-instance `TerminalWriteLease` held from before the before-first-byte checks until the writing goroutine exits) and a fake driver that tries `"1\n"`, a nudge and `performNudge` during the settle wait, *Then* each finds the lease busy, writes nothing and returns a retryable result (the driver skips the tick without latching the dialog hash, a nudge or steer returns a retryable error); the fake pane records exactly the reply's content and Enter and nothing between them (T-RP-50); *Given* a hidden `AskUserQuestion` dialog whose text matches the driver's phrases and that the driver answers before the operator sees the notification (existing behavior, recorded by Spike 1.3g), *Then* the Reply returns `STALE_PROMPT` or `NOT_WAITING` with 0 writes. `SubmitReplyOnce` takes a `*HeldLease`, so a call without one does not compile.
- Input is a single narrow line, measured in **bytes** (the unit is bytes on both sides; ux.md and the client are aligned to it).
  - *Given* `reply_text` that is empty, longer than `MaxQuestionReplyLength` (500 **bytes**), invalid UTF-8, or rejected by the one shared `session.ValidateSingleLineText` (C0 including ESC, CR, LF, TAB, NUL; DEL; the **C1 range U+0080-U+009F** including U+0085; **U+2028/U+2029**; **every Unicode `Cf` (format) code point except U+200C and U+200D**, which covers the bidi and invisible controls U+202A-U+202E, U+2066-U+2069, U+200E, U+200F, U+061C, U+180E, U+2060-U+2064, U+FEFF, the soft hyphen U+00AD, the interlinear annotation characters U+FFF9-U+FFFB, U+0600-U+0605 and the tag characters, **plus the whole tag block U+E0000-U+E007F including its unassigned code points**; Unicode noncharacters), *Then* `CodeInvalidArgument` and 0 writes, so a reply cannot inject escape sequences (INFERRED: C1 characters such as U+009B can act as escape introducers on some terminals), a second command, or a spoofed echo in the card and audit trail. The rule is `Cf` wholesale with an exception list of exactly two, U+200D (the emoji joiner, used in family and skin-tone sequences) and U+200C (needed for Persian), which resolves the earlier open design call in the re-review's favor (review-repair iteration 2, ADV-N8): the 200-emoji and ZWJ-sequence fixtures stay valid and a tag-character row is added (T-RP-60). The client sanitizer is a TS port checked against the same vector fixture.
- Reachability and audit (ADR-010 decisions 6-7; replaces "Authenticated like every other UI write", which is false on `:8543`: `SetupAuth` has no non-test caller, `localChain` runs only `ProbeGuard` for `ProbeProgram`, `server/server.go:1700-1708`, `server/middleware/probeguard.go:27-45`).
  - *Given* the local listener (`:8543`), *Then* `ReplyToPendingQuestion` is in the `LocalWriteGuard` procedure set (Story 1.8's guard, registered by Task 5.6l: POST only, **rebinding gate**: Host a loopback name, a local IP literal or a **verified** hostname, Origin loopback, allowed or same-origin; no `LoopbackBound` condition; the `:8444` chain skips the Host test because auth is its boundary) and a request with a rebinding Host (for example `evil.example:8543` resolving to 127.0.0.1) or a foreign Origin is rejected with 403 before the handler; *Given* the remote listener (`:8444`), *Then* the auth middleware applies; *Given* `auth_mode == none` and a caller that is remote (the peer is not loopback, **or any proxy header is present**, so a local reverse proxy or Tailscale Serve whose peer is loopback is treated as remote), *Then* the **local-caller gate** returns `PermissionDenied`, `refused` counted, 0 writes (the in-handler verdict reuses `services.WithRequestHost`'s record and the `isProxyHeader` rule of `requireLoopbackCaller`, `server/services/capture_tap_service.go:33-38,124-180`; ARCH-C17). State plainly (ADR-010 decision 7): this is a **safety rail, not a security boundary**.
  - *Given* an accepted attempt, *Then* **before** the terminal write one JSON line is appended and `fsync`ed to `<config dir>/audit/hidden-session-replies.jsonl` (file 0600 in a 0700 directory, `O_APPEND|O_CREATE|O_WRONLY`, rotated at 5 MiB keeping 3, one process-wide mutex around append and rotate shared with the O7 steer and flag-change writers, config directory resolved once at construction so a workspace switch cannot move it) with `ts`, `kind`, `session_uuid`, `session_title`, `question_id`, `reply_id`, `reply_len`, `reply_sha256`, `listener`, `peer_addr` (connect `req.Peer()`), `host`, `origin`, `user_agent`, `auth_mode`, and a second line with `outcome` and the failure stage afterwards. **There is no `reply_preview` for Reply** (the answer to "what is the token?" is arbitrary text), and the INFO log `hidden_session_reply` carries `reply_len` and `reply_sha256` only; the reply text appears in no log line (a test greps captured logs and the audit file for a sentinel secret). The product has no per-user identity (`server/middleware/auth.go:9-12`), so "who" is peer address, Host, user agent and auth mode. **No audit, no write**: a failed append or rotation returns `Internal` with 0 terminal writes (and, while the guards flag is on, the O7 steer fails the same way, with an explicit client message; the explicit degraded modes, including the guards-off case, are in ADR-010 decision 6 and Story 5.2). The sink core is Task 2.8h; this story extends it (Task 5.6c-c).
- Rate-limited; the limiter is an operator rail, not a security control.
  - *Given* a per-session limiter of 1 reply per 5s (burst 1) and a global limiter of 10 per minute (`golang.org/x/time/rate`, as `server/services/rate_limiter.go`), checked **after the idempotency lookup and before the claim** (`Reserve()` with `Delay() == 0`, cancelled on `NOT_SENT`), *When* a second reply with a **different** `reply_id` to the same session arrives inside 5s, *Then* the response outcome is `RATE_LIMITED` with `retry_after_seconds` (client text "Please wait N seconds"), 0 writes, and no claim was taken, so nothing needs to be un-claimed; a second request with the **same** `reply_id` gets the first (in-flight or cached) result instead (T-RP-54); the per-session limiter map is bounded (LRU 1024) and evicted on session delete; O7 steers are not rate-limited.
- The read-only guarantee is intact everywhere else (ADR-005).
  - *Given* the same test run, *Then* `WriteToSession` and the `UpdateSession` steer branch (for a hidden target that does not qualify for the O7 path) still return `FailedPrecondition`, stream `Input`/`Resize` frames are still dropped, and the Story 5.1d guard checks pass with two **UI** exceptions as pinned rows (this story's `replyToPendingQuestion` and the O7 `steerHiddenReviewViaBacklogLink`), the token-gated `steerAuthorized` and the `acquirer` rows.
- A dedicated kill switch (triad engineering gap; Task 5.6j). `hidden_session_readonly_guards` must not also gate Reply rollback; flag reads **fail closed**.
  - *Given* `hidden_session_reply` turned off by `UpdateFeatureFlag` (no deploy), *Then* `ReplyToPendingQuestion` returns outcome `DISABLED`, 0 writes, the claim is not taken; the client hides the Reply card and the Reply action (View output remains); `hidden_session_readonly_guards` has no effect on Reply in either state, and `hidden_session_reply` has none on the unary guards. *Given* an unreadable or unparsable config, *Then* `hidden_session_reply` reads as off and `hidden_session_readonly_guards` as on. Turning `hidden_session_reply` on is appended to the audit file before it is persisted (and refused with `Internal` if the append fails); turning it off is persisted first and recorded through the bounded non-blocking queue of Task 2.8h, so a stalled `fsync` or a full audit disk can never delay or block the kill switch (ADV-N6).
- Observability.
  - *Given* each outcome, *Then* `hidden_session_reply_total{outcome}` increments with the closed set `sent`, `send_indeterminate`, `not_sent`, `no_pending`, `superseded`, `stale_prompt`, `not_hidden`, `not_waiting`, `rate_limited`, `disabled`, `invalid`, `audit_failed`, `refused` (the same names as the `ReplyOutcome` enum, which has no `in_progress`, plus the three non-enum ones; Contract PR 3, plan Observability Plan and ux.md use these names only); `superseded` also carries a `cause` label (`question`, `permission_request`, `path_share`); `hidden_session_reply_registered_unreplyable_total{cause}` counts questions registered not replyable by `cause` (`no_proof`, `bad_proof`, `no_token`, `boilerplate`, `shape`, `path_only`) and `hidden_session_reply_duplicate_proofless_total` counts the dropped proofless duplicates (review-repair iteration 4, ADV-N23).
**Acceptance Criteria** (client; UX criteria RP-1..RP-10, `design/ux.md` Surface 12b):
- Reply card in the read-only view.
  - *Given* a hidden session with a pending question, *Then* `data-testid="reply-card"` shows the question text, a single-line input (`data-testid="reply-input"`, labelled "Your reply to Claude's question", `enterkeyhint="send"`) with a visible **byte counter** ("12 / 500 bytes"; the limit is enforced in UTF-8 **bytes** with `TextEncoder`, because HTML `maxlength` counts UTF-16 code units and would admit a multi-byte reply the server rejects; Send is disabled and the counter shows the error state above 500 bytes, and pasted newlines and control characters are stripped), a Send button (`data-testid="reply-send"`, >= 44px) and the line "Sent to the background session's terminal once. This reply is logged."; the card is a `role="group"` labelled by the question heading.
- States: composing, sending, sent, and each error.
  - *Given* Send, *Then* the control is disabled with `aria-busy` and the text preserved, and the response `outcome` decides the state (the response enum is the only signaling channel for expected outcomes, ADR-010 decision 1): `SENT` shows a receipt "Reply sent 10:42 - waiting for the session to continue" that replaces the composer, is announced politely through the Announcer (the element has no live role of its own) and takes focus; **`SEND_INDETERMINATE` shows "Sent? It may have been typed but not submitted. Check the terminal output" with a "View output" action and no Retry button** (the claim was consumed, so a second send could type the answer twice; the copy says "typed but not submitted" because a timed-out content write stops before the Enter, ADV-N2/m2; announced assertively; the typed text stays visible read-only); `NOT_SENT`, an `Internal` error (audit failed, 0 writes) or a network failure with no response shows "Could not send - Retry" (announced assertively; no `role` on the element) with the text preserved, and Retry re-sends with the **same `reply_id`** (safe: `NOT_SENT` released the claim, `Internal` means 0 writes, a lost response is answered from the server's in-flight or cached result); `NO_PENDING` shows "This question is no longer waiting. You may have answered it elsewhere."; `SUPERSEDED` shows "Claude asked a newer question." with an action that opens the newest question's card when a newer registered question exists, and otherwise (superseded by a permission dialog or another hook, no newer question) "Claude's prompt changed. Check the terminal and answer there." with "View output" only (no write happened; RP-16); `STALE_PROMPT` shows "The question on screen changed. Check the terminal and answer there." with no Retry; `NOT_WAITING` shows "The question isn't showing yet - try again in a moment" with Retry; `RATE_LIMITED` shows "Please wait N seconds" with the text preserved; `NOT_HIDDEN` and `DISABLED` hide the card; `PermissionDenied` shows "This device is not allowed to reply" with no Retry; an `InvalidArgument` shows "Replies are one line, up to 500 bytes". Offline: Send disabled with the reason.
  - *Given* a reply of 200 emoji (800 bytes, 200 characters), *Then* the counter shows 800 / 500, Send is disabled, and the server would have rejected it (client and server agree on bytes).
- Graceful degradation (ADR-010 decision 2f; ADV-B2 payload shape).
  - *Given* a pending question whose notification carries no question text but whose `tool_input` has an option label (so a `QuestionToken` exists; Spike 1.3g), *Then* the card shows "Open the terminal output to read the question" above the composer and the composer still works when `question_shape` is `single`; *Given* neither text nor an option label (no token), *Then* the card shows "Open the terminal output to read the question" with "View output" and **no composer** (not replyable); *Given* `question_shape` is `multi` or `unknown`, *Then* the card shows "Open the terminal to answer" with "View output" and **no composer**, and the Reply action on the toast, tray row and Background row is not shown (only "View output"); *Given* a notification without a `question_id` (cwd-attributed or unattributed), *Then* the same "View output"-only rendering.
- Prompt length and quick-picks (triad UX; RP-11, RP-12; tied to Spike 1.3g).
  - *Given* a captured prompt longer than 280 characters, *Then* the card shows the first 280 with a "Show full prompt" disclosure (>= 44px) that reveals the rest, so the operator does not have to scroll a phone past the banner and card to read a multi-option question before answering it.
  - *Given* Spike 1.3g recorded that the dialog is answered by option number AND the numbered options parse reliably from the captured prompt, *Then* numbered quick-pick buttons (>= 44px, text = the option label) appear above the composer; tapping one **fills the composer with the option number and does not auto-send** (the operator presses Send, so the same audited RPC, byte limit and rate limit apply). *Given* the spike did not record that, *Then* the card is free text only.
- Entry points and testids.
  - *Given* a hidden `INPUT_REQUIRED` toast, tray row and Background row with a replyable pending question (header-attributed, `question_shape` single), *Then* each shows a "Reply" action (`data-testid="notification-reply"`) plus "View output" (never more than 2 actions on touch); each opens `/?session=<id>&tab=terminal&notification=<id>&reply=1`; desktop focuses the composer, mobile scrolls the card into view without focusing it (no surprise soft keyboard). Visible sessions show no Reply.
- Mobile.
  - *Given* 390x844 and the keyboard open, *Then* the card stays above the keyboard (sized from `--viewport-height`), targets are >= 44px, and the read-only terminal sends no resize.
**Files**: `session/pane_reply.go`, `pane_reply_test.go`, `session/instance_tmux.go` (`SendKeysN`), `server/server.go` (guard-set registration; the guard itself is Story 1.8), `server/services/pending_question_store.go`, `pending_question_store_test.go`, `server/services/hidden_question_reply.go`, `hidden_question_reply_test.go`, `server/services/terminal_access.go`, `server/services/approval_handler.go` (register the question, stamp `question_id`, verify the hook proof, `matchingInstanceIDs`), `server/services/hook_proof.go`, `hook_proof_test.go`, `server/services/hook_injector.go`, `server/services/session_service_delegates.go`, `docs/registry/features/`, `web-app/src/components/sessions/ReplyCard.tsx`, `web-app/src/lib/hooks/useReplyToQuestion.ts`, `web-app/src/components/ui/NotificationItem.tsx`, `NotificationToast.tsx`, `BackgroundActivity.tsx`, `tests/e2e/hidden-session-reply.spec.ts`, `docs/how-to/manage-notifications.md`. ##### Task 5.6b: `PendingQuestionStore` (UUID key, candidate/confirmed/claimed/consumed/superseded states, single-critical-section `Claim`, in-flight `reply_id` singleflight) and registration from `broadcastQuestionNotification` only for header-attributed live instances
- Add `resolveSessionAttribution(headerVal, cwd)` beside `resolveSessionID` (`approval_handler.go:841-870`; its signature and its callers at `:318` and `:682` do not change); register a `candidate` only for `attribution == header` resolving to a live instance, keyed by `inst.UUID`, with a 128-bit random `question_id` and `question_shape` in the notification metadata (`broadcastQuestionNotification`, `:759-776`, currently publishes `nil` metadata); supersede older ids of the session on every registration; the confirmation poll that turns a `candidate` into `confirmed` within the **window of seconds** the spike records (it needs a status in the recorded set **and** a live capture containing the `QuestionToken` derived from `tool_input`; a payload with no token is registered not replyable) and captures the live pane fingerprint (accessor and region rule per Spike 1.3g); **supersede on every dialog-producing hook**: a `supersedeForDialog(headerVal, cwd)` call at `approval_handler.go:316-321`, right after the session is resolved and before every branch, supersedes all pending questions of the resolved instance and of every instance returned by `matchingInstanceIDs(cwd)` (`Path`, `WorkingDir` or worktree path, path-boundary prefix), for every `PermissionRequest` and every unattributed `AskUserQuestion` (and each further hook event the spike lists), and runs **before** the new question is registered so a registration never supersedes itself; states `candidate`, `confirmed`, `claimed`, `claimed-superseded`, `consumed`, `superseded`, `expired`, with a release that re-validates (expiry, newest id, still hidden); bounds 256 total / 8 per session / TTL (30 minutes for confirmed entries only); removal triggers. `Claim` resolves the request `session_id` through `findInstanceByID`, normalizes to the UUID and requires `inst.UUID == pending.SessionUUID`; it is one critical section under the store mutex. Promotion `candidate -> confirmed` is a compare-and-set under the same mutex with a per-session supersede epoch read before the pane capture (ARCH-C31c); the `QuestionToken` rejects the boilerplate denylist and is whitespace-normalized (ADV-N15, ARCH-C32c); the superseded counter carries its `cause` label.

##### Task 5.6c-b: The idempotency lookup, the limiters and their ordering
- **Order: flag, validate, idempotency lookup (cache, then in-flight), limiters, claim** (ARCH-C19). The `reply_id` result cache stores the SHA-256 of `(session UUID, question_id, text)`; a repeated id with a different hash is `InvalidArgument` (ARCH-C20, ADV-N10); the cache and the in-flight map are bounded (1024 entries, LRU, 10-minute TTL, INFERRED) and cache `SENT` and `SEND_INDETERMINATE` only; the in-flight singleflight is keyed by `reply_id`. Per-session limiter (1 per 5s, burst 1, bounded LRU 1024 and evicted on session delete) and global limiter (10 per minute) with `golang.org/x/time/rate` (`server/services/rate_limiter.go` precedent), used through `Reserve()` and a `Delay() == 0` check so `NOT_SENT` can `Cancel()` the reservation; a non-zero delay cancels at once and returns `RATE_LIMITED`. A double tap or a lost-response Retry inside 5s therefore returns the first result, not `RATE_LIMITED`.
- Files: `server/services/hidden_question_reply.go`, `hidden_question_reply_test.go`.

The reply text is in no log line. No audit, no write.

##### Task 5.6j: The `hidden_session_reply` kill-switch flag
- Register in `knownFeatureFlags` (global only, default on, fails closed on a read error, no env var: Task 2.1e's scan covers it); a flip to on is audited before it is persisted (Task 2.8h's blocking path) and a flip to off is persisted first and queued without waiting on the sink; the server check at the top of the handler (Task 5.6c-a); the client reads it through `useFeatureFlags()` and hides the Reply card and the Reply action when off; Settings status line "Hidden-session Reply is OFF"; tests for on and off, for the fail-closed read, and that `hidden_session_readonly_guards` has no effect on Reply.
- Files: `server/services/feature_flag_service.go`, `server/services/hidden_question_reply.go`, `web-app/src/components/sessions/ReplyCard.tsx`, `web-app/src/app/settings/features/page.tsx`, their tests.

##### Task 5.6k: The Reply-specific send primitive `SubmitReplyOnce` and its fault-injecting tests
- New `session/pane_reply.go`: `SubmitReplyOnce(ctx, lease *HeldLease, inst replySender, plan ReplyKeystrokes, beforeFirstByte, beforeEnter func() error)` returning a typed stage and error (`StageNotStarted`, `StageContentWriteEntered`, `StageContentWritten`, `StageEnterAborted`, `StageEnterWritten`): lease check (a nil, zero or foreign lease is refused), ctx check, `VerifyPaneOwner`, the `beforeFirstByte` callback (the handler's hidden/UUID/not-superseded/status/live-fingerprint checks), one content write, a bounded `waitForPaneSettle`, a ctx check, **the `beforeEnter` callback (the live-fingerprint compare again, under the lease: a mismatch means the operator answered in the terminal or a new dialog drew during the settle wait; no Enter is written and the stage is `StageEnterAborted`, which the handler reports as `SEND_INDETERMINATE` with an `aborted_before_enter` outcome line; ADV-N16)**, **one Enter write only when `plan` has one** (`DigitOnly` writes no Enter: if a digit selects immediately a stray Enter would pick the default of the next dialog), **no** `waitForPaneUpdate` retry and no second Enter. **`plan` is a function of the question shape and the reply text**, `(question_shape, reply_text) -> ReplyKeystrokes`, recorded by Spike 1.3g, not one global mode: a single digit on a select dialog is always `DigitOnly`; free text only for a recorded text-field shape; any other shape is not replyable. Each write runs under its own timeout (`ReplySendTimeout`, value chosen here and recorded; the steer primitive's real bound is 8s, `3*DefaultPaneSettleMaxWait + 2s` with `DefaultPaneSettleMaxWait = 2s`, `session/pane_submit.go:20,147`, not the "5s" an earlier ADR text claimed).
- **Mandatory count accessor** (ADV-N2, ARCH-C15 to C21): `ProcessManager.SendKeys` returns `(int, error)` (`session/process_manager.go:34`, `session/tmux_process_manager.go:374-380`) and `TmuxSession.SendKeys` returns `0, err` when `GetPTY` fails (`session/tmux/tmux.go:1349-1354`), but `Instance.SendKeys` drops the count (`session/instance_tmux.go:1539-1545`, `_, err := i.pm().SendKeys(keys)`). Add `Instance.SendKeysN(keys) (int, error)` (about ten lines; the not-started and paused check returns `0, err`) and make `replySender` use it, so a proven `0, err` is `NOT_SENT` (claim released) and `n > 0`, a partial write or a timeout is `SEND_INDETERMINATE`; a client disconnect or ctx cancel between the two writes is indeterminate and leaves the text typed (m7).
- **Abort is a compare-and-swap state word** (ARCH-C15): states `idle`, `writing`, `writing-aborted`, `aborted`; the goroutine takes `idle -> writing` immediately before each write and `writing -> idle` after it; the caller on timeout takes `idle -> aborted`, or `writing -> writing-aborted` when a write is already in the syscall; a goroutine whose CAS fails returns without writing. Stated limit: after the caller returns, at most the one write already inside the syscall can still land. The write lease (Story 5.0) is passed into the writing goroutine and released only when that goroutine exits.
- The primitive satisfies the existing unexported `paneOwnerVerifier` interface and a small `replySender` interface (count-returning), so tests inject a fake pane that counts content writes, Enters and bytes per stage, can block a write past its timeout and unblock it later, and can write from a fake driver during the settle wait. Classified as a terminal write in the Story 5.1d table, with `SendKeysN` (the scan then requires `SubmitReplyOnce`'s only caller to be `replyToPendingQuestion`).
- Files: `session/pane_reply.go`, `session/pane_reply_test.go`, `session/instance_tmux.go` (`SendKeysN`), `server/services/terminal_write_guards_test.go`.

##### Task 5.6l: Register `ReplyToPendingQuestion` in the Story 1.8 `LocalWriteGuard` set and apply the local-caller gate in the handler
- The guard, the verdict profiles and the request record are **Story 1.8 (PR 1f-a for the record, PR 1f-b for the guard and the verdict)**, moved out of this task in review-repair iteration 2 so that PR 2c can use them. This task adds `/api` + `SessionServiceReplyToPendingQuestionProcedure` to the set in `localChain` (`server/server.go:1700-1708`) with the rebinding gate, applies the Reply-only local-caller gate in the handler from the Story 1.8 adapter (auth off and a remote caller, where a proxy header makes the peer count as remote, is `PermissionDenied`, outcome `refused`, 0 writes), records `listener`, `auth_mode`, `peer_loopback` and `proxied` in the audit line, and carries the Host-rebinding test for Reply (T-RP-39).
- Files: `server/server.go`, `server/services/hidden_question_reply.go`, `server/server_probeguard_test.go`.

##### Task 5.6n: Hook sender proof `X-CS-Hook-Proof` keyed to the session UUID, kept out of argv, with real re-injection, the sender table, duplicate dedupe, the cause label, the boilerplate denylist and the pane-derived question text (ADV-N15; redesigned in review-repair iteration 4, ADV-N23, ARCH-C37)
- New `server/services/hook_proof.go`: a random 32-byte secret generated once and stored 0600 in the config directory (resolved once, like the audit sink); `proof(uuid) = HMAC-SHA256(secret, "ssq-hook-v1" || uuid)`; the header value is `<uuid>.<proof>`; verification is constant-time and resolves the instance by UUID (a rename keeps it valid). The injector writes the per-session proof file `<config dir>/hook-proofs/<uuid>` (0700 directory, 0600 file, atomic tmp-`O_EXCL`-`fsync`-rename, rewritten on every injection and at session start or resume), relies on the **already-injected `STAPLER_SESSION_UUID`** (no new variable; `Instance.buildExtraEnv`, `session/instance_tmux.go:729-733`, reached by every tmux start path through `wireTmuxSession`, so creation, resume, restore and restart all have it; a test asserts the variable is present after each of start, resume and restart, review-repair iteration 6b, ARCH-C56), and both curl builders (`server/services/hook_injector.go` `buildHookCommand`, `server/services/approval_handler.go` `InjectHookConfig`) emit the **fail-open guarded command** of Story 5.6 hook bullet (a) (`set -- -H "@$F"` only when `[ -n "$STAPLER_SESSION_UUID" ] && [ -f "$F" ] && [ -r "$F" ]`, the local-token header `-H @"$T"` behind the same `[ -f ] && [ -r ]` guard, every value through one single-quote escaper), so the proof is never in argv and a missing file still POSTs with `no_proof`; on a curl older than 7.55 the command has no proof branch. **Signatures**: `InjectHookConfig(rootDir, sessionTitle string)` becomes `InjectHookConfig(rootDir, sessionTitle, sessionUUID string)` (one production caller, `session_creation_pipeline.go:313`, which has the instance), `injectHookOptions` gains `sessionUUID`, and `RemoveHooksConfig(rootDir string, hooks []HookName)` gains the session UUID. **The proof file is deleted only when `HookPermissionApproval` is in the removed set** (review-repair iteration 6b, ADV-N34a; VERIFIED the only production `RemoveHooksConfig` call, `server/services/backlog_service_triage.go:1118` at `origin/main` `a91e7e46a`, removes only `HookGitDriftCheck` on every non-autonomous spawn, and `inst.UUID` there is the new session, so a per-call deletion would delete the proof of a session whose `PermissionRequest` entry is untouched and silently turn Reply off with a wrong "restart the session" card); the deletion is idempotent, the entry is shared by every session in a root, so a surviving entry whose file is gone is harmless (fail-open), and the instance cleanup path deletes the file when the session is deleted. **Every `InjectHooksConfig` caller** (VERIFIED by `git grep` at `origin/main` `a91e7e46a`): `session_creation_pipeline.go:313` calls `InjectHookConfig` (the single-hook builder) and is the only caller that has the new UUID and writes the proof file; `backlog_service_triage.go:1115` injects `HookGitDriftCheck` for autonomous spawns (no permission hook, no proof file, nothing changes); `server/mcp/tools_lifecycle.go:243` (`InjectHooksConfig(root, inst.Title, hookNames)`) and `server/mcp/tools_github.go:273` (`InjectHooksConfig(root, inst.Title, nil)`) run `buildHookCommand` for the same URL entries without a UUID: under the compare-against-desired rule they produce **the same command** (the branches read the environment at run time, so the UUID is not needed to build it) and **neither writes nor deletes a proof file**. **The proof file is (re)written by one idempotent helper, `ensureHookProofFile(uuid)` in `hook_proof.go`, called from** (iteration 6b, ADV-N34c, ARCH-C56): the creation pipeline right after `InjectHookConfig` (`session_creation_pipeline.go:313`); the server's boot-time instance load (`server/dependencies.go:650`, `storage.LoadInstances()`), which is what makes a secret rotated across a service restart reach every session that has the new command; and the server-side resume and restart entry points (`session_service_update.go:120` `Instance.Resume`, `session_service_lifecycle.go:641` `Instance.Restart`, `server/mcp/tools_lifecycle.go:441` `Instance.Resume`). The internal `Restart` callers (`SwitchProgram`, `SetAutoApprove`, `retry_state.go`) do not call it; the file persists across them because nothing deletes it except session delete and a `HookPermissionApproval` removal, and the boot call heals the rest. A test asserts the file is present after create, resume, restart and a simulated boot (T-RP-73). The valid proof's UUID is the attribution and the title header is ignored when a proof verifies; there is no `uuid_mismatch` cause because there is no mismatch to detect. The existing local-token `-H @'<file>'` argument (`hookAuthArg()`) had the same fail-closed property if its file vanished after injection; **it is fixed here** with the same guard (review-repair iteration 6b, ADV-N35). **The merge replaces a stale entry**: `hookAlreadyPresent` (`hook_injector.go`) no longer treats an entry that differs from `buildHookCommand`'s output as present; it is replaced in place, and `upgradeHookAuth` is removed (one producer of the whole command, Story 5.6 hook bullet (a)). The sender table is decided per injector and pinned (T-RP-74): the two curl builders carry a proof; the remote socat form, the ssq-mux HTTP hook (`session/mux/hooks.go:113`), the sshremote relay (`session/sshremote/approval_relay.go:627`) and `scripts/ssq-hook-handler:494` do not, so they are not replyable. `HandlePermissionRequest` (`approval_handler.go:299`) verifies the header **only to decide replyability**; a request without a valid proof still runs `supersedeForDialog` and still raises the toast but registers nothing replyable, **except** a proofless request whose dialog digest equals a live registered question's, which is dropped as a duplicate and neither supersedes nor registers. Every unreplyable registration increments `hidden_session_reply_registered_unreplyable_total{cause}` and stamps `reply_unavailable=<cause>` in the notification metadata. The denylist (seeded with "Do you want to proceed?" and "Chat about this", extended from Spike 1.3g's captures) is applied to the `QuestionToken`; promotion parses the dialog title region of the live capture; the card shows the pane-derived question text when it differs from the hook text. No nonce (rejected, with the reason, in ADR-010 decision 2d4).
- Files: `server/services/hook_proof.go`, `hook_proof_test.go`, `server/services/hook_injector.go`, `hook_injector_test.go`, `server/services/approval_handler.go`, `server/services/session_creation_pipeline.go` (the `InjectHookConfig` call and the `ensureHookProofFile` call), `server/dependencies.go` (the boot-time call), `server/services/session_service_update.go`, `session_service_lifecycle.go` and `server/mcp/tools_lifecycle.go` (the resume and restart calls), `server/services/pending_question_store.go`.

## Verification Gate (every PR)
- Go: `make build && make test` for touched packages; `make test-delivery-guards` (the `sinkguard`-tagged sink guard, the capability-literal lint, the pinned-caller table and R1/R2, their automated negative controls, the RPC-descriptor classification and the no-env-var flag scan; part of `make ci`/`make ready`); `make ready-complexity-gate` (gocognit/funlen/dupl are new-code-only and also apply to modified existing functions); `go test -race` for `pkg/events`, `server/deliverygate` (including the lock-order test, Story 2.3) and `session/streamhub`, plus the write-lease lock-order test (Story 5.0, T-WL-05).
- PR 2a-1 and PR 2a-2 only merge with the flag default off and every legacy check intact; the gate flag is turned on only after PR 2a-2 (the stats RPC) has merged; PR 2b only opens after the Stage 2 soak criteria pass.
- **Focused re-review PASS (hard precondition).** A PASS from the focused architecture + adversarial re-review of (1) the Reply write path and the `AccessFor`/`AccessForUnary`/`QuestionReplyWriter`/`BacklogSteerWriter` capabilities, (2) the O7 steer exemption, (3) the per-kind flag scope (`HiddenKind`, the `FlagMutation` enum) and (4) `GetDeliveryGateStats` with its persisted bucket file is required BEFORE the dependent work starts: items 1 and 2 gate Stories 5.1d, 5.2 (b, e, f), 5.6 and Contract PR 3 (Story 1.7); item 3 gates Story 2.11, Task 2.9d and Contract PR 2 (Story 1.6); item 4 gates Tasks 2.8c-2.8g, PR 2a-2 and Contract PR 2. Status today: **patched in review-repair iterations 1, 2 and 3 (iteration 3 found 2 architecture blockers, NB3 and NB4, and 0 adversarial blockers); the focused re-review has not been re-run** ("Pending focused re-review"); nothing in this plan marks any review as covering the patched text. The same PASS also gates PR 1f-b (Story 1.8), whose verdict definition is part of items 1 and 2; PR 1f-a (the raw record stamp) needs no PASS.
  - **PASS definition (triad repair 1):** a run with 0 blockers is a PASS; concerns are recorded, not looped (see the stop rule in "Pending focused re-review").
- **Contract ordering gates (review-repair iteration 1).** Contract PR 2 (Story 1.6) does not merge until the gate-on-time stats shape and the `FlagMutation` enum are settled (it freezes both); Contract PR 3 (Story 1.7) does not merge until the `ReplyOutcome` enum is complete; `make proto-breaking` (Task 1.4e) exists and is clean for each contract PR (`buf breaking` ran nowhere before); Tasks 5.2b and 5.2e merge in one PR; `hidden_session_gate` is registered in `knownFeatureFlags` only in PR 2a-2 (Task 2.8g), so it cannot be set live before the stats RPC; the Stage 1b reminders (three of them) are created only after both PR 2a-2 and PR 2e have merged. **Review-repair iteration 2 gates**: Contract PR 3 has no `IN_PROGRESS` and spells every value with the `REPLY_OUTCOME_` prefix, Contract PR 2 spells `FlagMutation` with `FLAG_MUTATION_` and carries the reshaped soak message (`make proto-lint` and `make proto-breaking` clean); PR 1f-b (Story 1.8) merges before PR 2c and PR 5u and keeps `ProbeProgram` byte-for-byte; PR 2a-2 carries Task 2.8h (the audit sink core) before Task 2.8g, so `flag_change` is recorded from the first moment the gate can flip; `make test-delivery-guards` covers the capability-literal lint and the pinned-caller table and their negative controls (a `steerAuthorization` constructed five ways outside its file, an unlisted caller of a primitive, a Pause-like unit that acquires the lease; iteration 5 removed the scan rules S1-S3 and L1-L3 that this sentence formerly named). **Review-repair iteration 3 gates**: Story 5.0 (PR 5a) merges before PR 5u; PR 1f carries the verified-hostname set (since split, below) and merges before PR 2c and PR 5u; Stories 5.2 (b, e, f) need PR 5a, PR 1f-b and PR 2a-2; Story 5.6 is last, needs a passing re-review of the Reply design after Spike 1.3g and ships behind `hidden_session_reply`; Contract PR 2 carries `failure_delivered_while_on` and `explicit_off_scopes`. **Review-repair iteration 4 gates**: PR 1f is split: PR 1f-a (Task 1.8e, the raw record stamp with `auth_mode` from a real validator) merges before PR 2a-2, and PR 1f-b (the verdict, the guard set and the verified-hostname set recomputed each cycle from `validateFn`) merges before PR 2c and PR 5u; Contract PR 2 also carries `needs_human_delivered_while_on` and `probe_delivered_while_on`; PR 5 (streams: Stories 5.1, 5.3, the stream half of 5.1d) and PR 5u (Story 5.2, the unary half of 5.1d) merge without Reply, which is PR 5r; PR 5a ships behind `terminal_write_lease`; Task 5.1d-0 (the short spike) precedes Task 5.1d-a, `make test-delivery-guards` runs the positive control that the real tree passes with the write-surface table as written (T-RO-44), the closed acquirer list and the pinned lifecycle caller list (T-RO-45, T-RO-46); the `UpdateSession` `program` and `auto_approve` guard and the `SwitchWorkspace` ordering are tested (T-RO-47, T-RO-40); and no hook-proof sender is added without a row in the sender table (T-RP-74). **Review-repair iteration 5 gates** (simplification): the guard set is four checks (capability-literal lint T-RO-36, RPC-descriptor classification T-RO-17, pinned-caller table T-RO-42/45/46, R1/R2 T-RO-10) with automated negative controls (T-RO-12) and one positive control that the real tree passes them (T-RO-44); Planning artifacts are committed on the branch first (`docs/how-to/commit-sdd-planning-artifacts.md`).
- **Flaky-test hazards for the new gate, flag and stats tests (triad repair 1).** Open bugs describe four ways tests in these packages flake, and the new tests must not add a fifth: BUG-084 (`docs/bugs/open/BUG-084-commitimportexternalsession-eventbus-goleak-flake-under-full-suite.md`: an `EventBus` whose goroutines outlive the test trips `goleak` under the full suite; every new bus-publish test closes the bus and waits for its subscribers before returning), BUG-087 (`docs/bugs/open/BUG-087-captureLogs-global-slog-swap-races-under-t-parallel.md`: a test that swaps the global `slog` default races any `t.Parallel()` test; new gate, flag-cache and stats tests take an **injected `*slog.Logger`** with a test handler and never call `slog.SetDefault`), BUG-089 (`docs/bugs/open/BUG-089-server-shutdown-joins-background-tickers-flake.md`: a background ticker that shutdown does not join flakes; the `FlagCache` ticker, the stats-bucket flush and the lease-wedge poller each expose `Stop()` that **joins** the goroutine, tests start them with an **injected clock** and assert the join, and no new test uses a real `time.Sleep` or a real ticker), and BUG-100 (`docs/bugs/open/BUG-100-slacknotifier-recoversfrompanic-crosstest-log-capture-race.md` and `docs/bugs/open/BUG-100-jules-keyringtokensource-runprobe-goroutine-outlives-test.md`: cross-test log capture and a probe goroutine outliving its test; the Slack cell of the Story 2.6 matrix uses an injected logger and waits for every goroutine it starts). Validation rows T-FT-01 and T-FT-02 enforce the first three; a test found flaky is fixed in the same PR (`fix-flaky-tests-dont-defer`).
- **Baseline before shipping the gate.** PR 2a-1's description cites the Story 1.5 numbers; PR 2b's description carries the before/after table (Task 2.9e).
- **Filter latency budget and test-time budget are measured, not guessed**: Task 2.3f benchmarks and Task 2.6h `server/services` wall time are recorded in the PR description (BUG-067, BUG-083, BUG-099).
- Web: `cd web-app && npx jest --no-coverage` for touched tests; `pnpm run lint:duplicates` (threshold 0.14 in `web-app/.jscpd.json:5`, VERIFIED; repo `CLAUDE.md` says 0.1%, a doc-drift collateral-debt item, Task 3.10f).
